L39: Abstract
L40: EPUB® 3 defines a distribution and interchange format for digital publications and documents. The EPUB
L41: format provides a means of representing, packaging, and encoding structured and semantically enhanced
L42: web content — including HTML, CSS, SVG, and other resources — for distribution in a single-file
L43: container.
L44: This specification defines the authoring requirements for EPUB publications and represents the third
L45: major revision of the standard.
L46: Status of This Document
L47: This section describes the status of this
L48: document at the time of its publication. A list of current W3C
L49: publications and the latest revision of this technical report can be found
L50: in the
L51: W3C standards and drafts index.
L52: This document was published by the Publishing Maintenance Working Group as
L53: a Recommendation using the
L54: Recommendation track.
L55: W3C recommends the wide deployment of this specification as a standard for
L56: the Web.
L57: A W3C Recommendation is a specification that, after extensive
L58: consensus-building, is endorsed by
L59: W3C and its Members, and
L60: has commitments from Working Group members to
L61: royalty-free licensing
L62: for implementations.
L63: Future updates to this Recommendation may incorporate
L64: new features.
L65: This document was produced by a group
L66: operating under the
L67: W3C Patent
L68: Policy.

L70: public list of any patent disclosures
L71: made in connection with the deliverables of
L72: the group; that page also includes
L73: instructions for disclosing a patent. An individual who has actual
L74: knowledge of a patent that the individual believes contains
L75: Essential Claim(s)
L76: must disclose the information in accordance with
L77: section 6 of the W3C Patent Policy.

L403: 1. Introduction
L404: 1.1 Overview
L405: This section is non-normative.
L406: EPUB 3 has been widely adopted as the format for digital books (ebooks), and this revision continues
L407: to increase the format's capabilities to better support a wider range of publication requirements,
L408: including complex layouts, rich media and interactivity, and global typography features. The
L409: expectation is that publishers will utilize the EPUB 3 format for a broad range of content,
L410: including books, magazines, and educational, professional, and scientific publications.
L411: This specification represents the core of EPUB 3 and includes the conformance requirements for EPUB publications — the product of the standard. The other specifications that comprise EPUB 3 are as
L412: follows:
L413: EPUB 3 Reading Systems [epub-rs-33] — defines the processing
L414: requirements for EPUB reading systems — the applications that consume EPUB publications
L415: and present their content to users.
L416: EPUB Accessibility [epub-a11y-11] — defines accessibility
L417: conformance and discovery requirements for EPUB publications.
L418: These specifications represent the formal list recognized as belonging to EPUB 3 and that contain
L419: functionality normatively referenced as part of the standard. The development of extension
L420: specifications periodically adds new functionality to EPUB publications. Features and functionality
L421: defined outside of core revisions to the standard, while not formally recognized in this
L422: specification, are nonetheless available for EPUB creators and reading system developers to
L423: use.
L424: The non-normative EPUB 3 Overview [epub-overview-33] provides
L425: a general introduction to EPUB 3. A list of technical changes from the previous version is also
L426: available in the change log.
L427: 1.2 Organization
L428: This section is non-normative.
L429: This section reviews the organization of this specification through the central product it defines:
L430: the EPUB publication.
L431: An EPUB publication is, in its most basic sense, a bundle of resources with instructions on how to
L432: render those resources to present the content in a logical order. The types of resources that are
L433: allowed in EPUB publication, as well as restrictions on their use, are defined in 3. Publication resources.
L434: A ZIP-based archive with the file extension .epub bundles the EPUB publication's
L435: resources for distribution. As conformant ZIP archives, EPUB publications can be unzipped by many
L436: software programs, simplifying both their production and consumption.
L437: The container format not only provides a means of determining that the zipped content represents an
L438: EPUB publication (the mimetype file), but also provides a universally named directory
L439: of non-normative resources (/META-INF). Key among these resources is the
L440: container.xml file, which directs reading systems to the available package documents. Refer to 4. Open Container Format (OCF) for more information about the container format.
L441: An EPUB publication is typically represented by a single package document. This document includes
L442: metadata used by reading systems to present the content to the user, such as the title and
L443: author for display in a bookshelf as well as rendering metadata (e.g., whether the content is
L444: reflowable or has a fixed layout). It also provides a manifest of resources and includes a spine that lists the default sequence in which to render documents as a user progresses
L445: through the content. Refer to 5. Package document for the requirements for the package
L446: document.
L447: The actual content of an EPUB publication — what users are presented with when they begin reading —
L448: is built on the Open Web Platform and comes in two flavors: XHTML and SVG. Called EPUB content documents, these documents typically reference many additional resources required for their proper
L449: rendering, such as images, audio and video clips, scripts, and style sheets.
L450: Refer to 6. EPUB content documents for detailed information about the rules and requirements to
L451: produce EPUB content documents, and [epub-a11y-11] for accessibility requirements.
L452: An EPUB publication also includes another key file called the EPUB navigation document. This
L453: document provides critical navigation capabilities, such as the table of contents, that allow users
L454: to navigate the content quickly and easily. The navigation document is a specialized type of XHTML content document which also allows EPUB creators to use it in the content (i.e., avoiding one
L455: table of contents for machine processing and another for user consumption). Refer to 7. EPUB navigation document for more information about this document.
L456: EPUB publications by default are intended to reflow to fit the available screen space. It is also
L457: possible to create publications that have pixel-precise fixed layouts using images and/or CSS
L458: positioning. The metadata to control layouts are defined in 8. Layout rendering control.
L459: Media overlay documents complement EPUB content documents. They provide declarative markup for
L460: synchronizing the text in EPUB content documents with prerecorded audio. The result is the ability
L461: to create a read-aloud experience where reading systems highlight the text as it is narrated.
L462: Refer to 9. Media overlays for the definition of media overlay documents.
L463: While conceptually simple, an EPUB publication is more than just a collection of HTML pages and
L464: dependent assets in a ZIP package as presented here. Additional information about the primary
L465: features and functionality that EPUB publications provide to enhance the reading experience is
L466: available from the referenced specifications, and a more general introduction to the features of
L467: EPUB 3 is provided in the non-normative [epub-overview-33].
L468: Refer to [epub-rs-33] for the processing requirements for reading systems. Although it is not
L469: necessary that EPUB creators read that document to create EPUB publications, an understanding of
L470: how reading systems present the content can help craft publications for optimal presentation to
L471: users.
L472: 1.3 Relationship to other specifications
L473: This section is non-normative.
L474: Caution
L475: The technologies EPUB 3 builds on are constantly evolving. Some, typically referred to as
L476: "living" or "evergreen" standards, are subject to change daily and their impact on the validity
L477: of EPUB publications is immediate. Others are updated less frequently and the changes may not
L478: affect EPUB publications until EPUB 3 undergoes a new revision.
L479: In all cases, it is possible that previously valid features may become obsolete (e.g., due to a
L480: lack of support or because of security issues). EPUB creators should therefore be cautious
L481: about using any feature without broad support and keep their EPUB conformance checkers up to
L482: date.
L483: 1.3.1 Relationship to HTML
L484: The [html] standard is continuously evolving — there are no longer versioned releases of it.
L485: That standard, in turn, references various technologies that continue to evolve, such as MathML,
L486: SVG, CSS, and JavaScript.
L487: The benefit of this approach for EPUB is that EPUB publications always keep pace with changes
L488: to the web without the need for new revisions. EPUB creators, however, must keep track of
L489: the various changes to HTML and the technologies it references to ensure they keep their
L490: processes up to date.
L491: The XHTML profile defined by this specification inherits all definitions
L492: of semantics, structure and processing behaviors from HTML unless otherwise specified.
L493: In addition, this specification defines a set of extensions
L494: to the [html] document model that EPUB creators may include in XHTML content documents.
L495: 1.3.2 Relationship to SVG
L496: This specification does not reference a specific version of [svg], but instead uses an undated
L497: reference. Whenever there is any ambiguity in this reference, the latest recommended
L498: specification is the authoritative reference.
L499: This approach ensures that EPUB will always keep pace with changes to the SVG standard. EPUB creators, however, must keep track of changes to the SVG standard to ensure they keep their
L500: processes up to date.
L501: 1.3.3 Relationship to CSS
L502: EPUB 3 supports CSS as defined by the CSS Working Group Snapshot [csssnapshot]. EPUB 3 also
L503: maintains some prefixed CSS properties, to ensure consistent support for global languages.
L504: 1.3.4 Relationship to MathML
L505: EPUB 3 only supports Presentation
L506: Markup [mathml3]. Content
L507: Markup is only allowed in structured
L508: markup annotations.
L509: 1.3.5 Relationship to SMIL
L510: This specification relies on a subset of [smil3], from which the media overlays elements and
L511: attributes defined in 9.2.2 Media overlay document definition are derived.
L512: 1.3.6 Relationship to URL
L513: This specification refers to the [url] standard for terminology and processing related to URLs
L514: expressed in EPUB publications. It is anticipated that new and revised web formats will
L515: adopt this standard, but until then this may put this specification in conflict with the
L516: internal requirements for some formats (e.g., valid relative paths), specifically with respect
L517: to the use of internationalized URLs. If a format does not allow internationalized URLs (i.e.,
L518: URLs must conform to [rfc3986] or earlier), that requirement takes precedence within those
L519: resources.
L520: 1.4 Terminology
L521: This specification defines the following terms specific to EPUB 3.
L522: Note
L523: Only the first instance of a term in a section links to its definition.
L524: codec
L525: Codec refers to content that has intrinsic binary format qualities, such as video and audio
L526: media types designed for optimum compression or that provide optimized streaming
L527: capabilities.
L528: container resource
L529: A publication resource that is located within the EPUB container, as opposed to a
L530: remote resource which is not.
L531: Refer to 3.6 Resource locations for media type-specific rules for resource
L532: locations.
L533: container root URL
L534: The URL [url] of the root directory representing the OCF abstract container. It is implementation specific, but EPUB creators must assume it has properties
L535: defined in 4.2.5 URLs in the OCF abstract container.
L536: content URL
L537: The URL of a file or directory in the OCF abstract container, defined in 4.2.5 URLs in the OCF abstract container.
L538: core media type resource
L539: A publication resource that conforms to one of the MIME media types [rfc2046] listed in
L540: 3.2 Core media types and, therefore, does not require the provision of a
L541: fallback (cf. foreign resource).
L542: The designation "core media type resource" only applies when a resource is used in the
L543: rendering of EPUB content documents and foreign content documents. A core media type
L544: resource cannot be used in the spine, for example, without a fallback
L545: unless it also has the media type of an EPUB content document.
L546: EPUB conformance checker
L547: An application that verifies the requirements of this specification against EPUB publications and reports on their conformance.
L548: EPUB container
L549: OCF ZIP container
L550: The ZIP-based packaging and distribution format for EPUB publications defined in 4.3 OCF ZIP container.
L551: EPUB container and OCF ZIP container are synonymous.
L552: EPUB content document
L553: A publication resource referenced from the spine or a manifest fallback chain that conforms to either the XHTML or SVG content document definitions.
L554: EPUB content documents contain all or part of the content of an EPUB publication (i.e.,
L555: the textual, visual and/or audio content).
L556: EPUB creators can include EPUB content documents in the spine without the provision of fallbacks.
L557: EPUB creator
L558: An individual, organization, or process that produces an EPUB publication.
L559: Note
L560: The creation of an EPUB publication often involves the work of many individuals, and may
L561: be split across multiple organizations (e.g., when a publisher outsources all or part of
L562: the work). Depending on the process used to produce an EPUB publication,
L563: responsibilities may fall on the organization (e.g., the publisher), the individuals
L564: preparing the publication (e.g., technical editors), or automatic procedures (e.g., as
L565: part of a publication pipeline). As a result, not every party or process may be
L566: responsible for ensuring every requirement is met, but there is always an EPUB creator
L567: responsible for the conformance of the final EPUB publication.
L568: Previous versions of this specification referred to the EPUB creator as the Author.
L569: EPUB manifest (or manifest)
L570: The section of the package document that lists the publication resources.
L571: Refer to 5.6.1 The manifest element for more information.
L572: EPUB navigation document
L573: A specialization of the XHTML content document that contains human- and machine-readable
L574: global navigation information. The EPUB navigation document conforms to the constraints
L575: expressed in 7. EPUB navigation document.
L576: EPUB publication
L577: A logical document entity consisting of a set of interrelated resources packaged in an EPUB container.
L578: An EPUB publication typically represents a single intellectual or artistic work, but this
L579: specification does not restrict the nature of the content.
L580: EPUB
L581: reading system (or reading system)
L582: A system that processes EPUB publications for presentation to a user in a manner
L583: conformant with this specification.
L584: EPUB spine (or spine)
L585: The section of the package document that defines an ordered list of EPUB content documents and foreign content documents. This list represents the default reading
L586: order of the EPUB publication.
L587: Refer to 5.7.1 The spine element for more information.
L588: exempt resource
L589: Exempt resources are a special class of publication resources that reading systems are
L590: not required to support the rendering of, but EPUB creators do not have to provide fallbacks for.
L591: Refer to 3.4 Exempt resources for more information.
L592: file name
L593: The name of any type of file within an OCF abstract container, whether a directory or a
L594: file within a directory.
L595: file path
L596: The file path of a file or directory is its full path relative to the root directory, as
L597: defined by the algorithm specified in 4.2.4 Deriving file paths.
L598: fixed-layout document
L599: An EPUB content document with fixed dimensions directly referenced from the spine. Fixed-layout documents are designated pre-paginated in the package document, as defined in 8.2 Fixed layouts.
L600: foreign content document
L601: Any publication resource referenced from a spine itemref element, or
L602: a manifest fallback chain, that is not an EPUB content document.
L603: When a foreign content document is referenced from a spine itemref element, it
L604: requires a manifest fallback chain with at least one EPUB content document.
L605: Note
L606: With the exception of XHTML and SVG, all core media type resources are foreign
L607: content documents when referenced directly from the spine.
L608: foreign resource
L609: A publication resource with a MIME media type [rfc2046] that does not match any of
L610: those listed in 3.2 Core media types. Foreign resources are subject to the
L611: fallback requirements defined in 3.3 Foreign resources.
L612: The designation "foreign resource" only applies to resources used in the rendering of EPUB content documents and foreign content documents.
L613: Note
L614: Foreign resource and foreign content document are not interchangeable terms. The types of
L615: resources considered foreign when used in the spine is greater than the types of
L616: resources considered foreign when used in EPUB content
L617: documents.
L618: linked resource
L619: A resource that is only referenced from a package document link element (i.e., not
L620: also used in the rendering of an EPUB publication.
L621: Linked resources are not publication resources but may be stored in the EPUB container. They do not require fallbacks.
L622: media overlay document
L623: An XML document that associates the XHTML content document with pre-recorded audio
L624: narration to provide a synchronized playback experience, as defined in 9. Media overlays.
L625: non-codec
L626: Non-codec refers to content types that benefit from compression due to the nature of their
L627: internal data structure, such as file formats based on character strings (for example, HTML,
L628: CSS, etc.).
L629: OCF abstract container
L630: The OCF abstract container defines a file system model for the contents of the OCF ZIP container, as defined in 4.2 OCF abstract container.
L631: package document
L632: A publication resource that describes the rendering of an EPUB publication, as
L633: defined in 5. Package document. The package document carries meta information
L634: about the EPUB publication, provides a manifest of resources, and defines a default reading
L635: order.
L636: publication resource
L637: A resource that contains content or instructions that contribute to the logic and rendering
L638: of an EPUB publication. In the absence of this resource, reading systems may not
L639: render the EPUB publication as the EPUB creator intends. Examples of publication
L640: resources include the package document, EPUB content documents, CSS Style Sheets,
L641: audio, video, images, embedded fonts, and scripts.
L642: EPUB creators must list publication resources in the package document manifest and typically bundle them all in the EPUB container (the exception being they
L643: may locate resources listed in 3.6 Resource locations outside the EPUB
L644: container).
L645: Note
L646: Resources on the web identified in outbound hyperlinks (e.g., referenced from the
L647: href attribute of an [html] a element) are not publication
L648: resources.
L649: Data urls are also not publication resources — they are
L650: considered part of the resource they are embedded in.
L651: remote resource
L652: A publication resource that is located outside of the EPUB container, typically on
L653: the web.
L654: Publication resources within the EPUB container are referred to as container resources.
L655: Refer to 3.6 Resource locations for media type specific rules for resource
L656: locations.
L657: root directory
L658: The root directory represents the base of the OCF abstract container file system. This
L659: directory is virtual in nature.
L660: scripted content document
L661: An EPUB content document that includes scripting or an XHTML content document that
L662: contains [html] form elements.
L663: Refer to 6.3.2 Scripting for more information.
L664: SVG content document
L665: An EPUB content document that conforms to the constraints expressed in 6.2 SVG content documents.
L666: synthetic spread
L667: The rendering of two adjacent pages simultaneously on a device screen.
L668: top-level content document
L669: An EPUB content document or foreign content document referenced from the spine, whether directly or via a fallback
L670: chain.
L671: unique identifier
L672: The primary identifier for an EPUB publication. The unique identifier is the value of
L673: the dc:identifier element specified by the unique-identifier attribute in the package document.
L674: Significant revision, abridgement, etc. of the content requires a new unique identifier.
L675: viewport
L676: The region of an EPUB reading system in which an EPUB publication is rendered
L677: visually to a user.
L678: XHTML content document
L679: An EPUB content document that conforms to the profile of [html] defined in 6.1 XHTML content documents.
L680: XHTML content documents use the XML syntax defined
L681: in [html].
L682: 1.5 Conformance
L683: As well as sections marked as non-normative, all authoring guidelines, diagrams, examples, and notes in this specification are non-normative. Everything else in this specification is normative.
L684: The key words MAY, MUST, MUST NOT, OPTIONAL, RECOMMENDED, REQUIRED, SHOULD, and SHOULD NOT in this document
L685: are to be interpreted as described in
L686: BCP 14
L687: [RFC2119] [RFC8174]
L688: when, and only when, they appear in all
L689: capitals, as shown here.
L690: All algorithm explanations are non-normative.
L691: 1.6 Authoring shorthands
L692: This section is non-normative.
L693: In package document metadata examples, reserved
L694: prefixes are used without declaration.
L695: References to Dublin Core elements [dcterms] use the dc: prefix. This prefix must be
L696: declared in the package document for their use to be valid
L697: (xmlns:dc="http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/")
L698: The epub namespace prefix [xml-names] is also used on elements and attributes without
L699: always having an explicit declaration (xmlns:epub="http://www.idpf.org/2007/ops").
L700: 2. EPUB publication conformance
L701: An EPUB publication:
L702: MUST define at least one rendering of its content as follows:
L703: MUST contain a package document that conforms to 5. Package document and meet all publication resource requirements for
L704: the package document.
L705: MUST contain an EPUB navigation document that conforms to 7. EPUB navigation document.
L706: SHOULD conform to the accessibility requirements defined in
L707: [epub-a11y-11].
L708: MUST be packaged in an EPUB container as defined in 4. Open Container Format (OCF).
L709: In addition, all publication resources MUST adhere to the requirements in 3. Publication resources.
L710: The rest of this specification covers specific conformance details.
L711: 2.1 Conformance checking
L712: This section is non-normative.
L713: Due to the complexity of this specification and number of technologies used in EPUB publications,
L714: EPUB creators are advised to use an EPUB conformance checker to verify the conformance of
L715: their content.
L716: EPUBCheck is the de facto EPUB conformance
L717: checker used by the publishing industry and has been updated with each new version of EPUB. It is
L718: integrated into a number of authoring tools and also available in alternative interfaces and other
L719: languages (for more information, refer to its Apps and Tools
L720: page).
L721: When verifying their EPUB publications, EPUB creators should ensure they do not violate the
L722: requirements of this specification (practices identified by the keywords "MUST", "MUST NOT", and
L723: "REQUIRED"). These types of issues will often result in EPUB publications not rendering or rendering
L724: in inconsistent ways. These issues are typically reported as errors or critical errors.
L725: EPUB creators should also ensure that their EPUB publications do not violate the recommendations of
L726: this specification (practices identified by the keywords "SHOULD", "SHOULD NOT", and "RECOMMENDED").
L727: Failure to follow these practices does not result in an invalid EPUB publication but may lead to
L728: interoperability problems and other issues that impact the user reading experience. These issues are
L729: typically reported as warnings.
L730: Note
L731: Vendors, distributors, and other retailers of EPUB publications should consider the importance of
L732: recommended practices before basing their acceptance or rejection on a zero-issue outcome from
L733: an EPUB conformance checker. There will be legitimate reasons why EPUB creators cannot follow
L734: recommended practices in all cases.
L735: 3. Publication resources
L736: 3.1 Introduction
L737: This section is non-normative.
L738: An EPUB publication is made up of many different categories of resources, not all of which are
L739: mutually exclusive. Some resources are publication resources, some are not. Some publication
L740: resources are allowed in the spine by default, while all others require fallbacks.
L741: Some resources can be used in rendering EPUB content documents, while others can only be used
L742: with fallbacks.
L743: Trying to understand these differences by reading the technical definitions of each category of
L744: resource can be complex. To make the categorizations easier to understand, this introduction uses
L745: the concept of different planes to explain how resources are grouped and referred to.
L746: The three planes are:
L747: The manifest plane — The manifest plane holds all the resources of the EPUB publication
L748: (namely, publication resources and linked resources).
L749: The spine plane — The spine plane holds only the resources used in rendering the spine
L750: (namely, EPUB content documents and foreign content documents).
L751: The content plane — The content plane holds only the resources used in the rendering of EPUB
L752: and foreign content documents (namely, core media type resources, foreign resources and
L753: exempt resources).
L754: The same resource may exist on more than one plane and will be referred to differently in this
L755: specification depending on which plane is being discussed. For example, a core media type resource
L756: used in the rendering of an EPUB content document (on the content plane) may also be a foreign
L757: content document if it is also listed in the spine (the spine plane).
L758: The following sections describe these planes in more detail.
L759: Note
L760: Refer to H.1 Resources for a detailed example showing how
L761: resources fit into the different planes.
L762: 3.1.1 The manifest plane
L763: To manifest plane defines all the resources of an
L764: EPUB publication. It is analogous to the package document manifest,
L765: but includes resources not present in that list.
L766: The primary resources in this group are designated publication resources, which are all the
L767: resources used in rendering an EPUB publication to the user. EPUB creators always have to
L768: list these resources in the manifest element.
L769: Publication resources are further classified by their use(s) in the spine plane and content plane.
L770: The manifest plane also contains a set of linked resources. These resources are tangential to
L771: the direct rendering. They include, for example, metadata records and links to external content
L772: (e.g., where to purchase an EPUB publication).
L773: Unlike publication resources, they are not listed in the package document manifest (i.e., because
L774: they are not essential to rendering the EPUB publication). They are instead defined in link
L775: elements in the package document metadata. These elements define their nature and purpose
L776: similar to how manifest item elements define publication resource. (In this way, they are
L777: like an extension of the manifest.)
L778: Refer to 5.5.6 The link element for more information about linked resources.
L779: Resources in the manifest plane are also sometimes broken down by where they are located.
L780: Although most publication resources have to be located in the EPUB container (called container resources), EPUB 3 allows audio, video, font and script data
L781: resources to be hosted outside the container. These exceptions were made to speed up the
L782: download and loading of EPUB publications, as these resources are typically quite large, and, in
L783: the case of fonts, not essential to the presentation. When remotely hosted, these publication
L784: resources are referred to as remote resources.
L785: Since linked resources are not essential to the rendering of an EPUB publication, there are no
L786: requirements on where they are located and consequently no special naming of them based on their
L787: location. They may be located within the EPUB container or outside it.
L788: Note
L789: Hyperlinked content outside the EPUB container (e.g., web pages) are not publication
L790: resources, and consequently are not listed in the manifest. Reading systems will normally
L791: open these links in a separate browser instance, not as part of the EPUB publication.
L792: 3.1.2 The spine plane
L793: The spine plane defines resources used in the
L794: default reading order established by the spine, which includes both linear and non-linear content. The spine instructs
L795: reading systems on how to load these resources as the user progresses through the EPUB publication. Although many resources may be bundled in an EPUB container, they are not all
L796: allowed by default in the spine.
L797: EPUB 3 defines a special class of resources called EPUB content documents that EPUB creators can use in the spine without any restrictions. EPUB content documents encompass both
L798: XHTML content documents and SVG content documents.
L799: To use any other type of resource in the spine, called a foreign content document, requires
L800: including a fallback to an EPUB content document. This extensibility model allows EPUB creators
L801: to experiment with formats while ensuring that reading systems are always able to render
L802: something for the user to read, as there is no guarantee of support for foreign content
L803: documents.
L804: A mechanism called manifest fallbacks allows EPUB creators
L805: to provide fallbacks for foreign content documents. In this model, the manifest entry for the foreign content document must include a fallback attribute that points to the next
L806: possible resource for reading systems to try when they do not support its format. Although not
L807: common, a fallback resource can specify another fallback, thereby making chains many resources
L808: deep. The one requirement is that there must be at least one EPUB content document in a
L809: manifest fallback chain.
L810: Although they are not directly listed in the spine, all of the resources in the fallback chain
L811: are considered part of the spine, and by extension part of the spine plane, since any may be
L812: used by a reading system.
L813: Refer to 3.5.1 Manifest fallbacks for more information.
L814: Caution
L815: Although manifest fallbacks fulfill the technical requirements of
L816: EPUB, there is little practical support for them in reading systems. Their use is strongly
L817: discouraged as it can lead to unreadable publications.
L818: Note
L819: It is possible to provide manifest fallbacks for EPUB content documents, but this is not
L820: required or common. For example, a scripted content document could have a fallback to an
L821: unscripted alternative for reading systems that do not support scripting.
L822: 3.1.3 The content plane
L823: The content plane classifies resources that are
L824: used when rendering EPUB content documents and foreign content documents. These types of
L825: resources include embedded media, CSS style sheets, scripts, and fonts. These resources fall
L826: into three categories based on their reading system support: core media type resources,
L827: foreign resources, and exempt resources.
L828: A core media type resource is one that reading systems have to support, so it can be used
L829: without restriction in EPUB or foreign content documents. For more information about core media
L830: type resources, refer to 3.2 Core media types.
L831: Note
L832: Being a core media type resource does not mean that reading systems will always render the
L833: resource, as not all reading systems support all features of EPUB 3. A reading system
L834: without a viewport, for example, will not render visual content such as images.
L835: The opposite of core media type resources are foreign resources. These are resources that reading
L836: systems are not guaranteed to support the rendering of. As a result, similar to how using
L837: foreign content documents in the spine requires fallbacks to ensure their rendering, using
L838: foreign resources in content documents also requires fallbacks. These fallbacks are provided in
L839: one of two ways: using the capabilities of the host format or via manifest fallbacks.
L840: The preferred method is to use the fallback capabilities of the host format. Many HTML elements,
L841: for example, have intrinsic fallback capabilities. One example is the picture
L842: element [html], which allows EPUB creators to specify multiple alternative image
L843: formats.
L844: If an intrinsic fallback method is not available, it is also possible to use manifest fallbacks,
L845: but this method, as cautioned against in the previous section,
L846: is discouraged. For more information about foreign resources, refer to 3.3 Foreign resources.
L847: Falling between core media type resources and foreign resources are exempt resources. These are
L848: most closely associated with foreign resources, as there is no guarantee that reading systems
L849: will render them. But like core media types, they do not require fallbacks.
L850: Exempt resources tend to address specific cases for which there are no core media types defined,
L851: but for which providing a fallback would prove cumbersome or unnecessary. These include
L852: embedding video, adding accessibility tracks, and linking to resources from the [html] link element.
L853: Refer to 3.4 Exempt resources for more information about these exceptions.
L854: Note
L855: A common point of confusion arising from core media type resources is the listing of XHTML
L856: and SVG as core media type resources with the requirement the markup conform to their
L857: respective EPUB content document definitions. This allows EPUB creators to embed both XHTML
L858: and SVG documents in EPUB content documents while keeping consistent requirements for
L859: authoring and reading system support.
L860: In practice, it means that EPUB creators can put XHTML and SVG core media type resources in
L861: the spine without any modification or fallback (they are also conforming XHTML and SVG
L862: content documents), but this is a unique case. All other core media type resources become
L863: foreign content documents when used in the spine (i.e., foreign content documents include
L864: all foreign resources and all core media type resources except for XHTML and SVG).
L865: 3.2 Core media types
L866: EPUB creators MAY include publication resources that conform to the MIME media type
L867: [rfc2046] specifications defined in the following table without fallbacks when they are used in
L868: EPUB content documents and foreign content documents. These resources are classified as
L869: core media type resources.
L870: With the exception of XHTML content documents and SVG content documents, EPUB creators MUST
L871: provide manifest fallbacks for core media type resources
L872: referenced directly from the spine. In this case, they are foreign content documents.
L873: The columns in the table represent the following information:
L874: Media Type—The MIME media type [rfc2046] used to represent the given
L875: publication resource in the manifest.
L876: If the table lists more than one media type, the first one is the preferred media type. EPUB
L877: creators should use the preferred media type for all new EPUB publications.
L878: Content Type Definition—The specification to which the given core media type
L879: resource must conform.
L880: Applies to—The publication resource type(s) that the Media Type and Content
L881: Type Definition applies to.
L882: Media Type
L883: Content Type Definition
L884: Applies to
L885: Images
L886: image/gif
L887: [gif]
L888: GIF Images
L889: image/jpeg
L890: [jpeg]
L891: JPEG Images
L892: image/png
L893: [png]
L894: PNG Images
L895: image/svg+xml
L896: SVG content documents
L897: SVG documents
L898: image/webp
L899: [rfc9649]
L900: WebP Images
L901: Audio
L902: audio/mpeg
L903: [mp3]
L904: MP3 audio
L905: audio/mp4
L906: [mpeg4-audio], [mp4]
L907: AAC LC audio using MP4 container
L908: audio/ogg; codecs=opus
L909: [rfc7845]
L910: OPUS audio using OGG container
L911: Style
L912: text/css
L913: CSS Style Sheets
L914: CSS Style Sheets.
L915: Fonts
L916: font/ttf
L917: application/font-sfnt
L918: [truetype]
L919: TrueType fonts
L920: font/otf
L921: application/font-sfnt
L922: application/vnd.ms-opentype
L923: [opentype]
L924: OpenType fonts
L925: font/woff
L926: application/font-woff
L927: [woff]
L928: WOFF fonts
L929: font/woff2
L930: [woff2]
L931: WOFF2 fonts
L932: Other
L933: application/xhtml+xml

L937: application/javascript

L940: [rfc4329]
L941: Scripts.
L942: application/x-dtbncx+xml
L943: [opf-201]
L944: The legacy NCX.
L945: application/smil+xml
L946: Media overlays
L947: EPUB media overlay documents
L948: Note
L949: Inclusion as a core media type resource does not mean that all reading systems will support the
L950: rendering of a resource. Reading system support also depends on the capabilities of the
L951: application (e.g., a reading system with a viewport must support image core media type
L952: resources, but a reading system without a viewport does not). Refer to Core media types [epub-rs-33] for more
L953: information about which reading systems rendering capabilities require support for which core
L954: media type resources.
L955: The Working Group typically only includes formats as core media type resources when they have
L956: broad support in web browser cores — the rendering engines that EPUB 3 reading systems build
L957: upon. They are an agreement between reading system developers and EPUB creators to ensure the
L958: predictability of rendering of EPUB publications.
L959: 3.3 Foreign resources
L960: A foreign resource, unlike a core media type resource is one
L961: which is not guaranteed reading system support when used in an EPUB content document or
L962: foreign content document.
L963: EPUB creators MUST provide fallbacks for foreign resources, where fallbacks take
L964: one of the following forms:
L965: intrinsic fallback mechanisms provided by the host format (e.g., [html] elements often
L966: provide the ability to reference more than one media type or to display an alternate
L967: embedded message when a media type cannot be rendered); or
L968: manifest fallback chains defined on item elements
L969: in the package document.
L970: Note
L971: Refer to the [html] and [svg] specifications for the intrinsic fallback capabilities their
L972: elements provide.
L973: 3.5.2 Intrinsic fallbacks also provides additional information about how fallbacks
L974: are interpreted for specific elements.
L975: 3.4 Exempt resources
L976: An exempt resource shares properties with both foreign resources and core media type resources. It is most similar to a foreign resource in that it is not guaranteed reading system support, but, like a core media type resource, does not require a fallback.
L977: There are only a small set of special cases for exempt resources. Video, for example, are exempt from
L978: fallbacks because there is no consensus on a core media type video format at this time (i.e., there
L979: is no format to fallback to). Similarly, audio and video tracks are exempt to allow EPUB creators to meet accessibility requirements using whatever format reading systems support
L980: best.
L981: The following list details cases of content-specific exempt resources, including any restrictions on
L982: where EPUB creators can use them.
L983: Fonts
L984: All font resources not already covered as font core media types
L985: are exempt resources.
L986: This exemption allows EPUB creators to use any font format without a fallback, regardless of
L987: reading system support expectations, as CSS rules will ensure a fallback font in case of no
L988: support.
L989: Refer to the reading system support
L990: requirements for fonts [epub-rs-33] for more information.
L991: Linked resources
L992: Any resource referenced from the [html] link element that is not already a core media type resource (e.g.,
L993: CSS style sheets) is an exempt resource.
L994: Tracks
L995: All audio and video tracks (e.g., [webvtt] captions, subtitles and descriptions)
L996: referenced from the [html] track element are exempt resources.
L997: Video
L998: All video codecs referenced from the [html] video — including any child source
L999: elements — are exempt resources.
L1000: Note
L1001: Although reading systems are encouraged to support at least one of the H.264 [h264]
L1002: and VP8 [rfc6386] video codecs, support for video codecs is not a conformance
L1003: requirement. EPUB creators must consider factors such as breadth of adoption, playback
L1004: quality, and technology royalties when deciding which video formats to include.
L1005: Note
L1006: The exemptions made above do not apply to the spine. If an exempt resource is
L1007: used in the spine, and it is not also an EPUB content document, it will require a fallback
L1008: in that context.
L1009: In addition to the content-specific exemptions, a resource is
L1010: classified as an exempt resource if:
L1011: it is not referenced from a spine itemref element (i.e., used as a foreign content document); and
L1012: it is not embedded directly in EPUB content documents (e.g., via [html] embedded content and [svg] image and foreignObject elements).
L1013: This exemption allows EPUB creators to include resources in the EPUB container that are not for
L1014: use by EPUB reading systems. The primary case for this exemption is to allow data files to travel
L1015: with an EPUB publication, whether for scripts to use in their constituent EPUB content documents
L1016: or for external applications to use (e.g., a scientific journal might include a data set with
L1017: instructions on how to extract it from the EPUB container).
L1018: It also allows EPUB creators to use foreign resources in foreign content documents without reading
L1019: systems or EPUB conformance checkers having to understand the fallback capabilities of those
L1020: resources (i.e., the requirement for a fallback for the foreign content document covers any
L1021: rendering issues within it). As the resource is not referenced from an EPUB content document, it
L1022: automatically becomes exempt from fallbacks.
L1023: 3.5 Resource fallbacks
L1024: 3.5.1 Manifest fallbacks
L1025: Manifest fallbacks are a feature of the package document that create a manifest fallback chain for a publication resource, allowing reading systems
L1026: to select an alternative format they can render.
L1027: Fallback chains are created using the fallback
L1028: attribute on manifest item elements. This attribute references
L1029: the ID [xml] of another manifest item that is a
L1030: fallback for the current item. The ordered list of all the references that a
L1031: reading system can reach, starting from a given item's fallback
L1032: attribute, represents the full fallback chain for that item. This chain also
L1033: represents the EPUB creator's preferred fallback order.
L1034: There are two cases for manifest fallbacks:
L1035: Spine fallbacks
L1036: EPUB creators MUST specify a fallback chain for a foreign content document to ensure
L1037: that reading systems can always render the spine item. In this case,
L1038: the chain MUST contain at least one EPUB content document.
L1039: EPUB creators MAY provide fallbacks for EPUB content documents (e.g., to provide a fallback for scripted content).
L1040: When a fallback chain includes more than one EPUB content document, EPUB creators can use
L1041: the properties attribute to differentiate
L1042: the purpose of each.
L1043: Content fallbacks
L1044: Note
L1045: The original purpose for content fallbacks was to specify fallback images for the
L1046: [html] img element. As HTML now has intrinsic fallback mechanism for images,
L1047: the use of content fallbacks is strongly discouraged. EPUB creators should always
L1048: use the intrinsic fallback capabilities of [html] and [svg] to provide fallback
L1049: content.
L1050: EPUB creators MUST provide a content fallback for foreign resources when the elements
L1051: that reference them do not have intrinsic fallback capabilities. In this case, the
L1052: fallback chain MUST contain at least one core media type resource.
L1053: EPUB creators MAY also provide manifest fallbacks for core media type resources (e.g., to
L1054: allow reading systems to select from more than one image format).
L1055: Regardless of the type of manifest fallback specified, fallback chains MUST NOT contain
L1056: self-references or circular references to item elements in the chain.
L1057: Note
L1058: As it is not possible to use manifest fallbacks for resources represented in data URLs, EPUB creators can only represent foreign resources
L1059: as data URLs where an intrinsic fallback mechanism is available.
L1060: 3.5.2 Intrinsic fallbacks
L1061: The following sections provide additional clarifications about the intrinsic fallback
L1062: requirements of specific elements.
L1063: 3.5.2.1 HTML audio and video fallbacks
L1064: EPUB creators MUST NOT use embedded
L1065: [html] flow content within a media element
L1066: (i.e, audio or video) as an intrinsic fallback for
L1067: audio foreign resources. Only child source elements [html] provide intrinsic
L1068: fallback capabilities.
L1069: Only older reading systems that do not recognize the audio or the
L1070: video elements (e.g., EPUB 2 reading systems) will render the embedded
L1071: content. When reading systems support these elements but not the available media formats,
L1072: they do not render the embedded content for the user.
L1073: Note
L1074: The requirement for fallbacks only applies to audio foreign resources referenced from
L1075: audio and video elements. Fallbacks are not required for
L1076: video resources; they are exempt resources.
L1077: 3.5.2.2 HTML img fallbacks
L1078: Due to the variety of sources that EPUB creators
L1079: can specify in the [html] img element, the following fallback conditions apply to its
L1080: use:
L1081: If it is the child of a picture element:
L1082: it MUST reference core media type resources from its src and
L1083: srcset attributes, when EPUB creators specify those attributes;
L1084: and
L1085: each sibling source element MUST reference a core media type resource
L1086: from its src and srcset
L1087: attributes unless it specifies the MIME media type [rfc2046] of a foreign resource in its type attribute.
L1088: Otherwise, it MAY reference foreign resources in its src and
L1089: srcset attributes provided EPUB creators define a manifest fallback.
L1090: 3.5.2.3 HTML script element
L1091: Although data blocks have a separate MIME media type [rfc2046] from their containing
L1092: XHTML content document, it is not possible to provide intrinsic fallbacks as no such
L1093: mechanisms are specified for the [html] script element. It is also not possible to
L1094: provide manifest fallbacks because data blocks cannot be defined as standalone files in the
L1095: EPUB container but are always embedded as inline script elements.
L1096: But, as the script element does not represent user content — data blocks are not rendered unless manipulated by
L1097: script, and content rendered by scripts already has core
L1098: media type requirements — requiring fallbacks for the raw data does not serve a
L1099: useful purpose.
L1100: Consequently, to ensure that EPUB creators can include data blocks for scripting
L1101: purposes, they are exempt from fallback requirements.
L1102: Note
L1103: This exemption aligns data blocks with the exemption for data files.
L1104: Note
L1105: [svg] does not define data blocks as of publication, but the same exclusion would apply
L1106: if a future update adds the concept.
L1107: 3.6 Resource locations
L1108: EPUB creators MAY host the following types of publication resources outside the EPUB container:
L1109: Audio resources.
L1110: Video resources.
L1111: Resources retrieved via scripting APIs (e.g.,
L1112: XmlHttpRequest [xhr] and Fetch [fetch]).
L1113: Font resources.
L1114: EPUB creators MUST store all other resources within the EPUB container.
L1115: Storing all resources inside the EPUB container is strongly encouraged whenever possible as it allows
L1116: users access to the entire presentation regardless of connectivity status.
L1117: When resources have to be located outside the EPUB container, EPUB creators are RECOMMENDED to
L1118: reference them via the secure https URI scheme [rfc9110] to limit the threat of
L1119: exposing their publications, and users, to network attacks. Reading systems might not load
L1120: remote resources referenced using insecure schemes such as http.
L1121: These rules for locating publication resource apply regardless of whether the given resource is a
L1122: core media type resource or a foreign resource.
L1123: Note
L1124: Refer to the remote-resources property for more
L1125: information on how to indicate that a manifest item references a
L1126: remote resource.
L1127: Example 1: Referencing a container resource
L1128: In this example, the audio file referenced from the [html] audio element is located inside the EPUB
L1129: container.
L1130: <html …>
L1131: …
L1132: <body>
L1133: …
L1134: <audio
L1135: src="audio/ch01.mp4"
L1136: controls="controls"/>
L1137: …
L1138: </body>
L1139: </html>
L1140: Example 2: Referencing a remote resource
L1141: In this example, the audio file referenced from the [html] audio element is hosted on the web.
L1142: <html …>
L1143: …
L1144: <body>
L1145: …
L1146: <audio
L1147: src="http://www.example.com/book/audio/ch01.mp4"
L1148: controls="controls"/>
L1149: …
L1150: </body>
L1151: </html>
L1152: 3.7 Data URLs
L1153: The data: URL scheme [rfc2397] is used to encode resources
L1154: directly into a URL string. The advantage of this scheme is that it allows EPUB creators to
L1155: embed a resource within another, avoiding the need for an external file.
L1156: EPUB creators MUST NOT use data URLs in the following scenarios where they can result in a
L1157: top-level content document or top-level browsing context [html]:
L1158: in href attributes in the package document — this applies both to manifest
L1159: item elements and metadata link elements;
L1160: in the href attribute on [html] or [svg] a elements, except
L1161: when inside an iframe element [html];
L1162: in the href attribute on [html] area elements, except when inside
L1163: an iframe element;
L1164: in calls to [ecmascript] window.open or document.open.
L1165: Note
L1166: These restrictions on the use of data URLs are to prevent security issues and also to ensure that
L1167: reading systems can determine where to take a user next (i.e., because data URLs cannot be
L1168: referenced from the spine).
L1169: The list of prohibited uses for data URLs is subject to change as the respective standards that
L1170: allow their use evolve.
L1171: A consequence of embedding is that the data in a data URL is not considered its own unique
L1172: publication resource for manifest reporting purposes (i.e., only its containing publication
L1173: resource gets listed). As this data has its own media type, however, it is still subject to foreign resource restrictions. EPUB creators MUST therefore
L1174: encode data URLs as core media type resources or provide a fallback using the intrinsic fallback
L1175: mechanisms of the host format.
L1176: 3.8 File URLs
L1177: The file: URL scheme is defined in [rfc8089] as
L1178: "identifying an object (a 'file') stored in a structured object naming and accessing environment on
L1179: a host (a 'file system')." It is typically used to retrieve files from the local operating
L1180: system.
L1181: Using a file URL in an EPUB publication, which can be transferred among different hosts,
L1182: represents a security risk and is also non-interoperable. As a consequence, EPUB creators MUST NOT use file URLs in EPUB publications.
L1183: 3.9 XML conformance
L1184: Any publication resource that is an XML-based media type [rfc2046]:
L1185: MUST be a conformant XML 1.0 Document as defined in Conformance of Documents [xml-names].
L1186: MAY only specify a document type
L1187: declaration that references an external
L1188: identifier appropriate for its media type — as defined in B. Allowed external identifiers — or that omits external identifiers [xml].
L1189: MUST NOT contain external entity
L1190: declarations in the internal DTD subset [xml].
L1191: MUST NOT make use of XInclude [xinclude].
L1192: MUST be encoded in UTF-8 or UTF-16 [unicode], with UTF-8 as the
L1193: RECOMMENDED encoding.
L1194: The above constraints apply regardless of whether the given publication resource is a core media type resource or a foreign resource.
L1195: Note
L1196: [html] and [svg] are removing support for the XML base attribute [xmlbase].
L1197: EPUB creators should avoid using this feature.
L1198: 4. Open Container Format (OCF)
L1199: 4.1 Introduction
L1200: This section is non-normative.
L1201: OCF is the required container technology for EPUB publications. OCF may play a role in the
L1202: following workflows:
L1203: During the preparation steps in producing an EPUB publication, OCF may be used as the container
L1204: format when exchanging in-progress publications between different individuals and/or different
L1205: organizations.
L1206: When providing an EPUB publication from publisher or conversion house to the distribution or
L1207: sales channel, OCF is the recommended container format to be used as the transport format.
L1208: When delivering the final EPUB publication to an EPUB reading system or user, OCF is the
L1209: required format for the container that holds all of the assets that make up the
L1210: publication.
L1211: This section defines the rules for structuring the file collection in the abstract: the "abstract
L1212: container". It also defines the rules for the representation of this abstract container within a ZIP
L1213: archive: the "physical container". The rules for ZIP physical containers build upon the ZIP
L1214: technologies used by [odf].
L1215: OCF also defines a standard method for obfuscating embedded fonts
L1216: for those EPUB publications that require this functionality.
L1217: 4.2 OCF abstract container
L1218: 4.2.1 Introduction
L1219: This section is non-normative.
L1220: The OCF abstract container file system model uses a single common root directory. All
L1221: container resources are located within the directory tree headed by the root directory, but
L1222: no specific file system structure for them is mandated by this specification.
L1223: The file system model also includes a mandatory directory named META-INF that is a
L1224: direct child of the root directory and stores the following special files:
L1225: container.xml
L1226: [required]
L1227: Identifies one or more package documents that define the EPUB publication.
L1228: signatures.xml
L1229: [optional]
L1230: Contains digital signatures for various assets.
L1231: encryption.xml
L1232: [optional]
L1233: Contains information about the encryption of publication resources. This file is
L1234: mandatory when EPUB creators use font
L1235: obfuscation.
L1236: metadata.xml
L1237: [optional]
L1238: Used to store metadata about the OCF ZIP container.
L1239: rights.xml
L1240: [optional]
L1241: Used to store information about digital rights.
L1242: manifest.xml
L1243: [optional]
L1244: A manifest of container contents as allowed by Open Document Format [odf].
L1245: Refer to 4.2.6 META-INF directory for conformance requirements for the various files
L1246: in the META-INF directory.
L1247: 4.2.2 File and directory structure
L1248: The virtual file system for the OCF abstract container MUST have a single common root directory for all the contents of the container.
L1249: The OCF abstract container MUST include a directory for configuration files named
L1250: META-INF that is a direct child of the container's root directory. Refer to 4.2.6 META-INF directory for the requirements for the contents of this
L1251: directory.
L1252: The file name mimetype in the root directory is reserved for use by OCF ZIP containers, as explained in 4.3 OCF ZIP container.
L1253: Files in the META-INF directory and the mimetype file are not
L1254: publication resources so MUST NOT be listed in the manifest.
L1255: EPUB creators MAY locate all other files within the OCF abstract container in any location
L1256: descendant from the root directory, provided they are not within the META-INF
L1257: directory. EPUB creators MUST NOT reference files in the META-INF directory from an
L1258: EPUB publication.
L1259: Note
L1260: Some reading systems do not provide access to resources outside the directory where the
L1261: package document is stored. EPUB creators should therefore place all resources at or
L1262: below the directory containing the package document to avoid interoperability issues.
L1263: This problem is more commonly encountered when creating multiple renditions [epub-multi-rend-11] of the publication.
L1264: 4.2.3 File paths and file names
L1265: In the context of the OCF abstract container, file paths and file names are scalar value strings [infra] (i.e., their values are case sensitive).
L1266: In addition, the following restrictions are designed to allow file paths and file names to be
L1267: used without modification on most operating systems:
L1268: File names MUST NOT exceed 255 bytes.
L1269: The file paths for any directory or file within the OCF abstract
L1270: container MUST NOT exceed 65535 bytes.
L1271: File names MUST NOT use the following [unicode] characters, as
L1272: commonly used operating systems may not support these characters consistently:
L1273: SOLIDUS: / (U+002F)
L1274: QUOTATION MARK: " (U+0022)
L1275: ASTERISK: * (U+002A)
L1276: FULL STOP as the last character: . (U+002E)
L1277: COLON: : (U+003A)
L1278: LESS-THAN SIGN: < (U+003C)
L1279: GREATER-THAN SIGN: > (U+003E)
L1280: QUESTION MARK: ? (U+003F)
L1281: REVERSE SOLIDUS: \ (U+005C)
L1282: VERTICAL LINE: | (U+007C)
L1283: DEL (U+007F)
L1284: C0 range (U+0000 … U+001F)
L1285: C1 range (U+0080 … U+009F)
L1286: Private Use Area (U+E000 … U+F8FF)
L1287: All Unicode Non Characters, specifically:
L1288: The 32 contiguous characters in the Basic Multilingual Plane
L1289: (U+FDD0 … U+FDEF)
L1290: The last two code points of the Basic Multilingual Plane
L1291: (U+FFFE and U+FFFF)
L1292: The last two code points at the end of the Supplementary Planes
L1293: (U+1FFFE, U+1FFFF … U+EFFFE, U+EFFFF)
L1294: Specials (U+FFF0 … U+FFFF)
L1295: Supplementary Private Use Area-A (U+F0000 … U+FFFFF)
L1296: Supplementary Private Use Area-B (U+100000 … U+10FFFF)
L1297: Note
L1298: The Unicode Character Database [uax44] also includes a list of
L1299: deprecated characters. EPUB creators are advised to avoid these characters,
L1300: as well, as it is expected that EPUB conformance checkers will flag their
L1301: use.
L1302: For compatibility with older reading systems, file names SHOULD NOT
L1303: contain SPACE (U+0020) characters.
L1304: All file names within the same directory MUST be unique following Unicode
L1305: canonical normalization [uax15] and then full case folding [unicode]. (Refer to Unicode Canonical Case Fold
L1306: Normalization Step [charmod-norm] for more information.)
L1307: Note
L1308: If EPUB creators dynamically integrate resources (i.e., where the naming is beyond their
L1309: control), they should be aware that automatic truncation of file names to keep them within
L1310: the 255 bytes limit can lead to corruption. This is due to the difference between bytes and
L1311: characters in multibyte encodings such as UTF-8; it is, therefore, important to avoid
L1312: mid-character truncation. See the section on "Truncating or limiting the length of
L1313: strings" in [international-specs] for more information.
L1314: Note
L1315: EPUB creators should use an abundance of caution in their file naming when interoperability
L1316: of content is key. The list of restricted characters is intended
L1317: to help avoid some known problem areas, but it does not ensure that all other Unicode
L1318: characters are supported. Although Unicode support is much better now than in earlier
L1319: iterations of EPUB, older tools and toolchains may still be encountered (e.g., ZIP tools
L1320: that only support [us-ascii]).
L1321: 4.2.4 Deriving file paths
L1322: To derive the file path, given a file or directory file in the OCF abstract container, apply the following steps
L1323: (expressed using the terminology of [infra]):
L1324: Let path be an empty list.
L1325: Let current be file.
L1326: While current is not the root directory:
L1327: prepend the file name of current to path;
L1328: set current to the parent directory of current.
L1329: Return the concatenation of path
L1330: using the U+002F (/) character.
L1331: 4.2.5 URLs in the OCF abstract container
L1332: The container root URL is the URL [url] of the root directory. It is implementation-specific, but
L1333: EPUB creators MUST assume it has the following properties:
L1334: The result of parsing "/" with the container root URL as base is the container root URL.
L1335: The result of parsing ".." with the container root URL as base is the container root URL.
L1336: The content URL of a file or directory in the OCF abstract container is the result of
L1337: parsing the file's file path with the container root URL as base.
L1338: Note
L1339: The container root URL is the URL assigned by the reading system to the root of the
L1340: EPUB container. It typically depends on how the reading system internally implements the
L1341: container file system.
L1342: However, a reading system cannot arbitrarily use any URL, but one that honors the constraints
L1343: defined above. These constraints ensure that any relative URL string found in the EPUB will
L1344: always be parsed to a URL of a resource within the container (which may or may not exist).
L1345: The primary reason for these constraints is to avoid potential run-time security issues that
L1346: would be caused by parsed URLs "leaking" outside the container files.
L1347: For example, URLs like https://localhost:12345/ or
L1348: https://www.example.org:12345/ honor these properties. But URLs like
L1349: https://localhost:12345/path/to.epub/,
L1350: file:///path/to.epub#path=/, or jar:file:/path/to.epub!/EPUB/
L1351: do not (parsing the URL string ".." with these three examples as base would
L1352: return https://localhost:12345/path/, file:///path/, and a parsing
L1353: error, respectively). It is the responsibility of the reading system to assign a URL to the
L1354: root directory that complies with the properties defined above.
L1355: Note
L1356: Parsing may replace some characters in the file path by their percent encoded alternative. For example,
L1357: A/B/C/file name.xhtml becomes A/B/C/file%20name.xhtml.
L1358: A string url is a valid-relative-ocf-URL-with-fragment string if it is a
L1359: path-relative-scheme-less-url string, optionally followed by U+0023 (#)
L1360: and a url-fragment string, and if the following steps return true:
L1361: Set the container root URL to https://a.example.org/A/.
L1362: Explanation
L1363: The goal of the algorithm is to detect whether url could be seen as
L1364: "leaking" outside the container. To do that, the standard URL parsing algorithm is used with an artificial root URL; the detection of the "leak" is done
L1365: by comparing the result of the parsing with the presence of the first test path
L1366: segment (A). (Note that the artificial container root URL wilfully
L1367: violates, for the purpose of this algorithm, the required properties by using that first test path segment.)
L1368: Let base be the base URL that must be
L1369: used to parse url as defined by the context (document or environment) where
L1370: url is used, and according to the content URL of the package document
L1371: (see 5.2 Parsing URLs in the package document).
L1372: Explanation
L1373: In the case of a URL in the package document the base variable is set to
L1374: the content URL of the package document. In the case of a document within the
L1375: META-INF directory, the base variable is set to the
L1376: container root URL (see 4.2.6.2 Parsing URLs in the META-INF directory). In the case of a
L1377: URL in an XHTML content document, the base URL used for parsing is defined by
L1378: the HTML standard. Typically, it will be the
L1379: content URL of the content document (unless the discouraged
L1380: base element is used).
L1381: Let testURLRecord be the result of applying the URL parser to url,
L1382: with base.
L1383: Let testURLStringA be the result of applying the URL Serializer to
L1384: testURLRecord.
L1385: Set the container root URL to https://b.example.org/B/.
L1386: Explanation
L1387: The reasons to repeat the same steps twice with different, and artificial, settings
L1388: of the container root URL is to avoid collision which may occur if the
L1389: url string also includes /A/. Consider, for example, the
L1390: case where url is ../../A/doc.xhtml.
L1391: Set base to be the base URL that must be
L1392: used to parse url as defined by the context (document or environment) where
L1393: url is used, and according to the content URL of the package document (see 5.2 Parsing URLs in the package document).
L1394: Set testURLRecord to be the result of applying the URL parser to
L1395: url, with base.
L1396: Let testURLStringB be the result of applying the URL Serializer to
L1397: testURLRecord.
L1398: If testURLStringA does not start with https://a.example.org/ or
L1399: testURLStringB does not start with https://b.example.org/,
L1400: return true.
L1401: Explanation
L1402: If any of the result does not share the test URL host, it means that url,
L1403: or its base URL (for example, in HTML, if it is explicitly set with the
L1404: base element), was absolute and points outside the
L1405: container. This is acceptable.
L1406: If testURLStringA starts with https://a.example.org/A/ and
L1407: testURLStringB starts with https://b.example.org/B/, return
L1408: true.
L1409: Explanation
L1410: The presence of the first test path segments (A, respectively
L1411: B) indicate that the URL doesn't leak outside the container.
L1412: Return false.
L1413: In the OCF abstract container, any URL string MUST be an
L1414: absolute-url-with-fragment string or a valid-relative-ocf-URL-with-fragment string.
L1415: In addition, all relative-URL-with-fragment strings [url] MUST, after parsing, be equal to the content URL of an existing file in the OCF
L1416: abstract container.
L1417: Note
L1418: These constraints on URL strings mean that:
L1419: relative URL strings starting with a / (U+002F) (for example,
L1420: /EPUB/content.xhtml) are disallowed;
L1421: relative URL strings containing more double-dot URL path segments than needed to
L1422: reach the target file (for example, EPUB/../../../../config.xml) are
L1423: disallowed;
L1424: any other absolute or relative URL string is allowed.
L1425: Note that in any case, even the disallowed URL strings described above will not "leak"
L1426: outside the container after parsing (as explained in the first note of this section). They are nevertheless disallowed for better
L1427: interoperability with non-conforming or legacy reading systems and toolchains.
L1428: Example 3: Referencing a file in the same directory
L1429: In this example, the file image1.jpg is in the same directory as the XHTML
L1430: content document.
L1431: <html …>
L1432: …
L1433: <body>
L1434: <img
L1435: src="image1.jpg"
L1436: alt="…" />
L1437: …
L1438: </body>
L1439: </html>
L1440: Example 4: An "out-of-container" URL
L1441: Given the following container structure:
L1442: /
L1443: ├── mimetype
L1444: ├── META-INF
L1445: │ └── container.xml
L1446: └── EPUB
L1447: └── content.xhtml
L1448: A URL ../../../../EPUB/secret.xhtml appearing in content.xhtml would be parsed by a
L1449: reading system into a content URL with a path EPUB/secret.xhtml, following the constraints
L1450: on the container root URL. However, as the URL could be perceived as one of a resource
L1451: outside the container, and create interoperability issues; it would be reported as an error
L1452: by a checker tool.
L1453: 4.2.6 META-INF directory
L1454: 4.2.6.1 Inclusion in OCF abstract container
L1455: All OCF abstract containers MUST include a directory called META-INF in
L1456: their root directory.
L1457: This directory is reserved for configuration files, specifically those defined in 4.2.6.3 Reserved files.
L1458: 4.2.6.2 Parsing URLs in the META-INF directory
L1459: To parse a URL string
L1460: url used in files located in the META-INF directory the URL parser MUST be applied to url, with the
L1461: container root URL as base.
L1462: Example 5: Resolving paths in the container file
L1463: If container file (META-INF/container.xml) has the following content:
L1464: <?xml version="1.0"?>
L1465: <container version="1.0" xmlns="urn:oasis:names:tc:opendocument:xmlns:container">
L1466: <rootfiles>
L1467: <rootfile
L1468: full-path="EPUB/Great_Expectations.opf"
L1469: media-type="application/oebps-package+xml" />
L1470: </rootfiles>
L1471: </container>
L1472: then the path EPUB/Great_Expectations.opf is relative to the root directory for the OCF abstract container and not relative to the
L1473: META-INF directory.
L1474: 4.2.6.3 Reserved files
L1475: 4.2.6.3.1 Container file (container.xml)
L1476: The REQUIRED container.xml file in the META-INF directory
L1477: identifies the package documents available in the OCF abstract container.
L1478: All [xml] elements defined in this section are in the
L1479: urn:oasis:names:tc:opendocument:xmlns:container namespace [xml-names]
L1480: unless specified otherwise.
L1481: The contents of this file MUST be valid to the definition in this section after removing
L1482: all elements and attributes from other namespaces (including all attributes and contents
L1483: of such elements).
L1484: Note
L1485: An XML Schema also informally defines
L1486: the content of this file.
L1487: 4.2.6.3.1.1 The container element
L1488: The container element encapsulates all the information in the
L1489: container.xml file.
L1490: Element Name:
L1491: container
L1492: Usage:
L1493: REQUIRED root element [xml] of the
L1494: container.xml file.
L1495: Attributes:
L1496: version
L1497: [required]
L1498: This attribute MUST have the value
L1499: "1.0".
L1500: Content Model:
L1501: In this order:
L1502: rootfiles [exactly one]
L1503: links [0 or 1]
L1504: 4.2.6.3.1.2 The rootfiles element
L1505: The rootfiles element contains a list of package documents available
L1506: in the EPUB container.
L1507: Element Name:
L1508: rootfiles
L1509: Usage:
L1510: REQUIRED first child of container.
L1511: Attributes:
L1512: None
L1513: Content Model:
L1514: rootfile [1 or more]
L1515: 4.2.6.3.1.3 The rootfile element
L1516: Each rootfile element identifies the location of one package document in the EPUB container.
L1517: Element Name:
L1518: rootfile
L1519: Usage:
L1520: As child of the rootfiles element. Repeatable.
L1521: Attributes:
L1522: full-path
L1523: [required]
L1524: Identifies the location of a package document.
L1525: The value of the attribute MUST be a path-relative-scheme-less-URL string [url]. The path is relative to the root directory.
L1526: media-type
L1527: [required]
L1528: Identifies the media type of the package document.
L1529: The value of the attribute MUST be
L1530: "application/oebps-package+xml".
L1531: Content Model:
L1532: Empty
L1533: If an EPUB creator defines more than one rootfile element, each MUST
L1534: reference a package document that conforms to the same version of EPUB. Each package
L1535: document represents one rendering of the EPUB publication.
L1536: Note
L1537: Although the EPUB container provides the ability to reference more than one
L1538: package document, this specification does not define how to interpret, or select
L1539: from, the available options. Refer to [epub-multi-rend-11] for more
L1540: information on how to bundle more than one rendering of the content.
L1541: 4.2.6.3.1.4 The links element
L1542: The links element identifies resources
L1543: necessary for the processing of the OCF ZIP container.
L1544: Element Name:
L1545: links
L1546: Usage:
L1547: OPTIONAL second child of container. Repeatable.
L1548: Attributes:
L1549: None
L1550: Content Model:
L1551: link [1 or more]
L1552: Note
L1553: This specification currently does not define uses for the links
L1554: element. Refer to [epub-multi-rend-11] for an example of its use.
L1555: 4.2.6.3.1.5 The link element
L1556: Element Name:
L1557: link
L1558: Usage:
L1559: As child of the links element. Repeatable.
L1560: Attributes:
L1561: href
L1562: [required]
L1563: Identifies the location of a resource.
L1564: The value of the link element href
L1565: attribute MUST be a path-relative-scheme-less-URL string
L1566: [url]. The path is relative to the root directory.
L1567: media-type
L1568: [optional]
L1569: Identifies the type and format of the referenced resource.
L1570: The value of the attribute MUST be a media type [rfc2046].
L1571: rel
L1572: [required]
L1573: Identifies the relationship of the resource.
L1574: The value of the attribute MUST be a space-separated list of
L1575: tokens.
L1576: Content Model:
L1577: Empty
L1578: 4.2.6.3.1.6 Examples
L1579: This section is non-normative.
L1580: Example 6: A basic container file
L1581: <?xml version="1.0"?>
L1582: <container version="1.0" xmlns="urn:oasis:names:tc:opendocument:xmlns:container">
L1583: <rootfiles>
L1584: <rootfile
L1585: full-path="EPUB/My_Crazy_Life.opf"
L1586: media-type="application/oebps-package+xml" />
L1587: </rootfiles>
L1588: </container>
L1589: 4.2.6.3.2 Encryption file (encryption.xml)
L1590: The OPTIONAL encryption.xml file in the META-INF directory
L1591: holds all encryption information on the contents of the container. If an EPUB creator encrypts any resources within the container, they MUST include an
L1592: encryption.xml file to provide information about the encryption
L1593: used.
L1594: 4.2.6.3.2.1 The encryption element
L1595: Element Name:
L1596: encryption
L1597: Namespace:
L1598: urn:oasis:names:tc:opendocument:xmlns:container
L1599: Usage:
L1600: REQUIRED root element [xml] of the
L1601: encryption.xml file.
L1602: Attributes:
L1603: None
L1604: Content Model:
L1605: In any order:
L1606: EncryptedKey [1 or more]
L1607: EncryptedData [1 or more]
L1608: The encryption element contains child elements of type
L1609: EncryptedKey and EncryptedData as defined by
L1610: [xmlenc-core1].
L1611: An EncryptedKey element describes each encryption key used
L1612: in the container, while an EncryptedData element describes each
L1613: encrypted file. Each EncryptedData element refers to an
L1614: EncryptedKey element, as described in XML Encryption.
L1615: Note
L1616: An XML Schema also informally
L1617: defines the content of the encryption.xml file.
L1618: OCF encrypts individual files independently, trading off some security for improved
L1619: performance, allowing the container contents to be incrementally decrypted.
L1620: Encryption in this way exposes the directory structure and file naming of the whole
L1621: package.
L1622: OCF uses XML Encryption [xmlenc-core1] to provide a framework for encryption,
L1623: allowing a variety of algorithms to be used. XML Encryption specifies a process for
L1624: encrypting arbitrary data and representing the result in XML. Even though an OCF abstract container may contain non-XML data, EPUB creators can use XML
L1625: Encryption to encrypt all data in an OCF abstract container. OCF encryption
L1626: supports only the encryption of entire files within the container, not parts of
L1627: files. EPUB creators MUST NOT encrypt the encryption.xml file when
L1628: present.
L1629: Encrypted data replaces unencrypted data in an OCF abstract container. For example,
L1630: if an EPUB creator encrypts an image named photo.jpeg, they should
L1631: replace the contents of the photo.jpeg resource with its encrypted
L1632: contents. Within the ZIP directory, EPUB creators SHOULD store encrypted files
L1633: rather than Deflate-compress them.
L1634: Note that some situations require obfuscating the storage
L1635: of embedded fonts referenced by an EPUB publication to make them more difficult
L1636: to extract for unrestricted use. Although obfuscation is not encryption, reading
L1637: systems use the encryption.xml file in conjunction with the font obfuscation algorithm to identify fonts to
L1638: deobfuscate.
L1639: EPUB creators MUST NOT encrypt the following files:
L1640: mimetype
L1641: META-INF/container.xml
L1642: META-INF/encryption.xml
L1643: META-INF/manifest.xml
L1644: META-INF/metadata.xml
L1645: META-INF/rights.xml
L1646: META-INF/signatures.xml
L1647: [= package document =]
L1648: EPUB creators MAY subsequently encrypt signed resources using the Decryption
L1649: Transform for XML Signature [xmlenc-decrypt]. This feature enables a
L1650: reading system to distinguish data encrypted before signing from data encrypted
L1651: after signing.
L1652: Example 7: An encrypted image
L1653: In this example, adapted from Section 2.2.1 of [xmlenc-core1], the resource
L1654: image.jpeg is encrypted using a symmetric key algorithm (AES)
L1655: and the symmetric key is further encrypted using an asymmetric key algorithm
L1656: (RSA) with a key of "John Smith".
L1657: <encryption
L1658: xmlns ="urn:oasis:names:tc:opendocument:xmlns:container"
L1659: xmlns:enc="http://www.w3.org/2001/04/xmlenc#"
L1660: xmlns:ds="http://www.w3.org/2000/09/xmldsig#">
L1661: <enc:EncryptedKey Id="EK">
L1662: <enc:EncryptionMethod
L1663: Algorithm="http://www.w3.org/2001/04/xmlenc#rsa-1_5"/>
L1664: <ds:KeyInfo>
L1665: <ds:KeyName>
L1666: John Smith
L1667: </ds:KeyName>
L1668: </ds:KeyInfo>
L1669: <enc:CipherData>
L1670: <enc:CipherValue>
L1671: xyzabc
L1672: </enc:CipherValue>
L1673: </enc:CipherData>
