L1674: </enc:EncryptedKey>
L1675: <enc:EncryptedData Id="ED1">
L1676: <enc:EncryptionMethod
L1677: Algorithm="http://www.w3.org/2001/04/xmlenc#kw-aes128"/>
L1678: <ds:KeyInfo>
L1679: <ds:RetrievalMethod
L1680: URI="#EK"
L1681: Type="http://www.w3.org/2001/04/xmlenc#EncryptedKey"/>
L1682: </ds:KeyInfo>
L1683: <enc:CipherData>
L1684: <enc:CipherReference
L1685: URI="image.jpeg"/>
L1686: </enc:CipherData>
L1687: </enc:EncryptedData>
L1688: </encryption>
L1689: 4.2.6.3.2.2 Order of compression and encryption
L1690: When stored in an OCF ZIP container, EPUB creators SHOULD compress streams of
L1691: data with non-codec content types before encrypting them. EPUB creators MUST use
L1692: Deflate compression. This practice ensures that file entries stored in the ZIP
L1693: container have a smaller size.
L1694: EPUB creators SHOULD NOT compress streams of data with codec content types before
L1695: encrypting them. In such cases, additional compression introduces unnecessary
L1696: processing overhead at production time (especially with large resource files) and
L1697: impacts audio/video playback performance at consumption time. In some cases, the
L1698: combination of compression with some encryption schemes might even compromise the
L1699: ability of reading systems to handle partial content requests (e.g. HTTP byte
L1700: ranges), due to the technical impossibility to determine the length of the full
L1701: resource ahead of media playback (e.g. HTTP Content-Length header).
L1702: When EPUB creators compress streams of data before encrypting, they SHOULD provide
L1703: additional EncryptionProperties metadata to specify the size of the
L1704: initial resource (i.e., before compression and encryption), as per the
L1705: Compression XML element defined below. When EPUB creators do not
L1706: compress streams of data before encrypting, they MAY provide the additional
L1707: EncryptionProperties metadata to specify the size of the initial
L1708: resource (i.e., before encryption).
L1709: Element Name:
L1710: Compression
L1711: Namespace:
L1712: http://www.idpf.org/2016/encryption#compression
L1713: Usage:
L1714: OPTIONAL child of EncryptionProperty.
L1715: Attributes:
L1716: Method [required]
L1717: Identifies the compression method used.
L1718: Value is either "0" (no compression) or "8"
L1719: (Deflate algorithm).
L1720: OriginalLength [required]
L1721: Represents the size of the initial resource (number of bytes).
L1722: Value is a positive integer.
L1723: Content Model:
L1724: Empty
L1725: Example 8: A compressed video
L1726: In this example, the EPUB creator has Deflate compressed the MP4 file. Its
L1727: original size was 3500000 bytes.
L1728: <encryption
L1729: xmlns="urn:oasis:names:tc:opendocument:xmlns:container">
L1730: <enc:EncryptedData
L1731: xmlns:enc="http://www.w3.org/2001/04/xmlenc#">
L1732: …
L1733: <enc:CipherData>
L1734: <enc:CipherReference
L1735: URI="OEPBS/video.mp4"/>
L1736: </enc:CipherData>
L1737: <enc:EncryptionProperties>
L1738: <enc:EncryptionProperty
L1739: xmlns:ns="http://www.idpf.org/2016/encryption#compression">
L1740: <ns:Compression
L1741: Method="8"
L1742: OriginalLength="3500000"/>
L1743: </enc:EncryptionProperty>
L1744: </enc:EncryptionProperties>
L1745: …
L1746: </enc:EncryptedData>
L1747: </encryption>
L1748: 4.2.6.3.3 Manifest file (manifest.xml)
L1749: The OPTIONAL manifest.xml file in the META-INF directory
L1750: provides a manifest of files in the container.
L1751: The OCF specification does not mandate a format for the manifest.
L1752: Note that package documents specify the only manifests used for processing EPUB publications. Reading systems do not use this file.
L1753: Note
L1754: This feature exists only for compatibility with [odf].
L1755: 4.2.6.3.4 Metadata file (metadata.xml)
L1756: The OPTIONAL metadata.xml file in the META-INF directory is
L1757: only for container-level metadata.
L1758: If EPUB creators include a metadata.xml file, they SHOULD use only
L1759: namespace-qualified elements [xml-names] in it. The file SHOULD contain the root element [xml] metadata in the
L1760: namespace http://www.idpf.org/2013/metadata, but this specification allows
L1761: other root elements for backwards compatibility.
L1762: This version of the specification does not define metadata for use in the
L1763: metadata.xml file. Future versions of this specification MAY define
L1764: container-level metadata.
L1765: 4.2.6.3.5 Rights management file (rights.xml)
L1766: This specification reserves the OPTIONAL rights.xml file in the
L1767: META-INF directory for the trusted exchange of EPUB publications
L1768: among rights holders, intermediaries, and users.
L1769: When EPUB creators do not include a rights.xml file, no part of the
L1770: OCF abstract container is rights governed at the container level. Rights expressions
L1771: might exist within the EPUB publications.
L1772: 4.2.6.3.6 Digital signatures file (signatures.xml)
L1773: Note
L1774: Adding a digital signature is not a guarantee that a malicious actor cannot tamper
L1775: with an EPUB publication as reading systems do not have to check
L1776: signatures.
L1777: The OPTIONAL signatures.xml file in the META-INF directory
L1778: holds digital signatures for the container and its contents.
L1779: 4.2.6.3.6.1 The signatures element
L1780: Element Name:
L1781: signatures
L1782: Namespace:
L1783: urn:oasis:names:tc:opendocument:xmlns:container
L1784: Usage:
L1785: REQUIRED root element [xml] of the
L1786: signature.xml file.
L1787: Attributes:
L1788: None
L1789: Content Model:
L1790: Signature [1 or more]
L1791: The signature element contains child elements of type
L1792: Signature, as defined by [xmldsig-core1]. EPUB creators can
L1793: apply signatures to an EPUB publication as a whole or to its parts, and can specify
L1794: the signing of any kind of data (i.e., not just XML).
L1795: Note
L1796: An XML Schema also informally
L1797: defines the content of the signatures.xml file.
L1798: When an EPUB creator does not include a signatures.xml file, they are
L1799: not signing any part of the OCF abstract container at the container level.
L1800: Digital signing might exist within the EPUB publication.
L1801: When an EPUB creator creates a data signature for the OCF abstract
L1802: container, they SHOULD add the signature as the last child Signature
L1803: element of the signatures element.
L1804: Note
L1805: Each Signature in the signatures.xml file identifies by
L1806: URL [url] the data to which the signature applies, using the [xmldsig-core1]
L1807: Manifest element and its Reference sub-elements.
L1808: EPUB creator may sign individual container files separately or together.
L1809: Separately signing each file creates a digest value for the resource that
L1810: reading systems can validate independently. This approach might make a Signature
L1811: element larger. If EPUB creators sign files together, they can list the set of
L1812: signed files in a single XML Signature Manifest element and
L1813: reference them by one or more Signature elements.
L1814: EPUB creators can sign any or all files in the OCF abstract
L1815: container in their entirety, except for the signatures.xml file since
L1816: that file will contain the computed signature information. Whether and how EPUB
L1817: creators sign the signatures.xml file depends on their objective.
L1818: If the EPUB creator wants to allow signatures to be added or removed from the OCF
L1819: abstract container without invalidating their signature, they SHOULD NOT sign the
L1820: signatures.xml file.
L1821: If the EPUB creator wants any addition or removal of a signature to invalidate their
L1822: signature, they can use the Enveloped Signature transform defined in Section 6.6.4 of
L1823: [xmldsig-core1] to sign the entire pre-existing signature file excluding the
L1824: Signature being created. This transform would sign all previous
L1825: signatures, and it would become invalid if a subsequent signature were added to the
L1826: package.
L1827: Note
L1828: If the EPUB creator wants the removal of an existing signature to invalidate
L1829: their signature, but also wants to allow the addition of signatures, they could
L1830: use an XPath transform to sign just the existing signatures. The details of such
L1831: a transform are outside the scope of this specification, however.
L1832: The [xmldsig-core1] specification does not associate any semantics with a
L1833: signature; an agent might include semantic information, for example, by adding
L1834: information to the Signature element that describes the signature. The
L1835: [xmldsig-core1] specification describes how additional information can be added to
L1836: a signature, such as by use the SignatureProperties element.
L1837: Example 9: Signing resources
L1838: In this example, based on the examples found in Section 2 of [xmldsig-core1],
L1839: one signature applies to two resources (EPUB/book.xhtml and
L1840: EPUB/images/cover.jpeg).
L1841: <signatures
L1842: xmlns="urn:oasis:names:tc:opendocument:xmlns:container">
L1843: <Signature
L1844: Id="sig"
L1845: xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/09/xmldsig#">
L1846: <SignedInfo>
L1847: <CanonicalizationMethod
L1848: Algorithm="http://www.w3.org/TR/2001/REC-xml-c14n-20010315"/>
L1849: <SignatureMethod
L1850: Algorithm="http://www.w3.org/2000/09/xmldsig#dsa-sha1"/>
L1851: <Reference
L1852: URI="#Manifest1">
L1853: <DigestMethod
L1854: Algorithm="http://www.w3.org/2000/09/xmldsig#sha1"/>
L1855: <DigestValue>j6lwx3rvEPO0vKtMup4NbeVu8nk=</DigestValue>
L1856: </Reference>
L1857: </SignedInfo>
L1858: <SignatureValue>
L1859: …
L1860: </SignatureValue>
L1861: <KeyInfo>
L1862: <KeyValue>
L1863: <DSAKeyValue>
L1864: <P>…</P>
L1865: <Q>…</Q>
L1866: <G>…</G>
L1867: <Y>…</Y>
L1868: </DSAKeyValue>
L1869: </KeyValue>
L1870: </KeyInfo>
L1871: <Object>
L1872: <Manifest
L1873: Id="Manifest1">
L1874: <Reference
L1875: URI="EPUB/book.xhtml">
L1876: <Transforms>
L1877: <Transform
L1878: Algorithm="http://www.w3.org/TR/2001/REC-xml-c14n-20010315"/>
L1879: </Transforms>
L1880: <DigestMethod
L1881: Algorithm="http://www.w3.org/2000/09/xmldsig#sha1"/>
L1882: <DigestValue>…</DigestValue>
L1883: </Reference>
L1884: <Reference URI="EPUB/images/cover.jpeg">
L1885: <Transforms>
L1886: <Transform
L1887: Algorithm="http://www.w3.org/TR/2001/REC-xml-c14n-20010315"/>
L1888: </Transforms>
L1889: <DigestMethod
L1890: Algorithm="http://www.w3.org/2000/09/xmldsig#sha1"/>
L1891: <DigestValue>…</DigestValue>
L1892: </Reference>
L1893: </Manifest>
L1894: </Object>
L1895: </Signature>
L1896: </signatures>
L1897: 4.3 OCF ZIP container
L1898: 4.3.1 Introduction
L1899: This section is non-normative.
L1900: An OCF ZIP container is a physical single-file manifestation of an OCF abstract container. The container allows:
L1901: the exchange of in-progress EPUB publication between different individuals and/or
L1902: different organizations;
L1903: the transfer of EPUB publications from a publisher or conversion house to the
L1904: distribution or sales channel; and
L1905: the delivery of EPUB publications to EPUB reading systems or users.
L1906: 4.3.2 ZIP file requirements
L1907: An OCF ZIP container uses the ZIP format as specified by [zip], but with the following
L1908: constraints and clarifications:
L1909: The contents of the OCF ZIP container MUST be a conforming OCF abstract container.
L1910: OCF ZIP containers MUST NOT use the features in the ZIP application
L1911: note [zip] that allow ZIP files to be spanned across multiple storage media or be
L1912: split into multiple files.
L1913: OCF ZIP containers MUST include only stored (uncompressed) and
L1914: Deflate-compressed ZIP entries within the ZIP archive.
L1915: OCF ZIP containers MAY use the ZIP64 extensions defined as "Version
L1916: 1" in section V, subsection G of the application note [zip] and SHOULD use only those
L1917: extensions when the content requires them.
L1918: OCF ZIP containers MUST NOT use the encryption features defined by
L1919: the ZIP format; instead, encryption MUST be done using the features described in 4.2.6.3.2 Encryption file (encryption.xml).
L1920: OCF ZIP containers MUST encode file system names using UTF-8
L1921: [unicode].
L1922: The following constraints apply to specific fields in the OCF ZIP container archive:
L1923: In the local file header table, EPUB creators MUST set
L1924: the version needed to extract fields to the values 10,
L1925: 20 or 45 to match the maximum version level needed by the
L1926: given file (e.g., 20 for Deflate, 45 for ZIP64).
L1927: In the local file header table, EPUB creators MUST set the
L1928: compression method field to the values 0 or
L1929: 8.
L1930: 4.3.3 OCF ZIP container media type identification
L1931: EPUB creators MUST include the mimetype file as the first file in the OCF ZIP container. In addition:
L1932: The contents of the mimetype file MUST be the MIME media type [rfc2046]
L1933: string application/epub+zip encoded in US-ASCII [us-ascii].
L1934: The mimetype file MUST NOT contain any leading or trailing padding or
L1935: whitespace.
L1936: The mimetype file MUST NOT begin with the Unicode byte order mark U+FEFF.
L1937: EPUB creators MUST NOT compress or encrypt the mimetype file.
L1938: EPUB creators MUST NOT include an extra field in its ZIP header.
L1939: Note
L1940: Refer to I.2 The application/epub+zip media type for further information about the
L1941: application/epub+zip media type.
L1942: 4.4 Font obfuscation
L1943: Caution
L1944: Better methods of protecting fonts exist. Both [woff] and [woff2] fonts, for example, allow
L1945: the embedding of licensing information and provide some protection through font table
L1946: compression. The use of remotely hosted fonts also allows for font subsetting. EPUB creators
L1947: are advised to use font obfuscation as defined in this section only when no other options are
L1948: available to them. See also the limitations of
L1949: obfuscation.
L1950: 4.4.1 Introduction
L1951: This section is non-normative.
L1952: Since an OCF ZIP container is fundamentally a ZIP file, commonly available ZIP tools can be
L1953: used to extract any unencrypted content stream from the package. Moreover, the nature of ZIP
L1954: files means that their contents might appear like any other native container on some systems
L1955: (e.g., a folder).
L1956: While this simplicity of ZIP files is quite useful, it also poses a problem when ease of
L1957: extraction of fonts is not a desired side-effect of not encrypting them. An EPUB creator who
L1958: wishes to include a third-party font, for example, typically does not want that font extracted
L1959: and re-used by others. More critically, many commercial fonts allow embedding, but embedding a
L1960: font implies making it an integral part of the EPUB publication, not just providing the
L1961: original font file along with the content.
L1962: Since integrated ZIP support is so ubiquitous in modern operating systems, simply placing a font
L1963: in the OCF ZIP container is insufficient to signify that the font cannot be reused in other
L1964: contexts. This uncertainty can undermine the otherwise useful font embedding capability of EPUB
L1965: publications.
L1966: To discourage reuse of their fonts, some font vendors might only allow their use in EPUB
L1967: publications if the fonts are bound in some way to the EPUB publication. That is, if the font
L1968: file cannot be installed directly for use on an operating system with the built-in tools of that
L1969: computing device, and it cannot be directly used by other EPUB publications.
L1970: It is beyond the scope of this specification to provide a digital rights management or
L1971: enforcement system for fonts. This section instead defines a method of obfuscation that will
L1972: require additional work on the part of the final OCF recipient to gain general access to any
L1973: obfuscated fonts.
L1974: 4.4.2 Limitations
L1975: This section is non-normative.
L1976: This specification does not claim that obfuscation constitutes encryption, nor does it guarantee
L1977: that the resource will be secure from copyright infringement. The hope is only that this
L1978: algorithm will meet the requirements of vendors who require some assurance that their fonts
L1979: cannot be extracted simply by unzipping the OCF ZIP container and copying the resource.
L1980: Obfuscation, like any protection scheme, cannot fully protect fonts from being accessed in their
L1981: deobfuscated state. The mechanism only provides an obstacle for those who are unaware of the
L1982: license details. It will not prevent a determined user from gaining full access to the font
L1983: through such alternative means as:
L1984: applying the deobfuscation algorithm to extract the raw font file;
L1985: accessing the deobfuscated font through a reading system that must deobfuscate it to
L1986: render the content (e.g., by accessing the resources through a browser-based reading
L1987: system); or
L1988: accessing the deobfuscated font through authoring tools that provide the visual rendering of
L1989: the content.
L1990: As a result, whether this method of obfuscation satisfies the requirements of individual font
L1991: licenses remains a question for the licensor and licensee. EPUB creators are responsible for
L1992: ensuring their use of obfuscation meets font licensing requirements.
L1993: EPUB creators should also be aware that obfuscation may lead to interoperability issues in
L1994: reading systems as reading systems are not required to deobfuscate fonts. As a result, the
L1995: visual presentation of their publications may differ from reading system to reading system.
L1996: Also note that the algorithm is restricted to obfuscating fonts. It is not intended as a
L1997: general-purpose mechanism for obfuscating any resource in the EPUB container.
L1998: 4.4.3 Obfuscation key
L1999: EPUB creators MUST derive the key used in the obfuscation algorithm from the unique identifier.
L2000: All whitespace characters, as defined in section 2.3 of the XML
L2001: 1.0 specification [xml], MUST be removed from this identifier — specifically, the
L2002: Unicode code points U+0020, U+0009, U+000D and
L2003: U+000A.
L2004: EPUB creators MUST generate a SHA-1 digest of the UTF-8 representation of the resulting string as
L2005: specified by the Secure Hash Standard [fips-180-4]. They can then use this digest as the key
L2006: for the algorithm.
L2007: 4.4.4 Obfuscation algorithm
L2008: The algorithm employed to obfuscate fonts consists of modifying the first 1040 bytes (~1KB) of
L2009: the font file. (In the unlikely event that the font file is less than 1040 bytes, this process
L2010: will modify the entire file.)
L2011: To obfuscate the original data, store, as the first byte of the embedded font, the result of
L2012: performing a logical exclusive or (XOR) on the first byte of the raw font file and the first
L2013: byte of the obfuscation key.
L2014: Repeat this process with the next byte of source and key and continue for all bytes in the key.
L2015: At this point, the process continues starting with the first byte of the key and 21st byte of
L2016: the source. Once 1040 bytes are encoded in this way (or the end of the source is reached),
L2017: directly copy any remaining data in the source to the destination.
L2018: EPUB creators MUST obfuscate fonts before compressing and adding them to the OCF ZIP container. Note that as obfuscation is not encryption, this requirement is not a violation of
L2019: the one in 4.2.6.3.2 Encryption file (encryption.xml) to compress fonts before
L2020: encrypting them.
L2021: The following pseudo-code exemplifies the obfuscation algorithm.
L2022: set ocf to OCF ZIP container file
L2023: set source to font file
L2024: set destination to obfuscated font file
L2025: set keyData to key for file
L2026: set outer to 0
L2027: while outer < 52 and not (source at EOF)
L2028: set inner to 0
L2029: while inner < 20 and not (source at EOF)
L2030: read 1 byte from source (Assumes read advances file
L2031: position)
L2032: set sourceByte to result of read
L2033: set keyByte to byte inner of keyData
L2034: set obfuscatedByte to (sourceByte XOR
L2035: keyByte)
L2036: write obfuscatedByte to destination
L2037: increment inner
L2038: end while
L2039: increment outer
L2040: end while
L2041: if not (source at EOF) then
L2042: read source to EOF
L2043: write result of read to destination
L2044: end if
L2045: Deflate destination
L2046: store destination as source in ocf
L2047: 4.4.5 Specifying obfuscated fonts
L2048: Although not technically encrypted data, all obfuscated fonts MUST have an entry in the encryption.xml file accompanying the EPUB publication (see 4.2.6.3.2 Encryption file (encryption.xml)).
L2049: EPUB creators MUST specify an EncryptedData element for each obfuscated font.
L2050: Each EncryptedData element MUST contain a child EncryptionMethod
L2051: element whose Algorithm attribute has the value
L2052: http://www.idpf.org/2008/embedding. The presence of this attribute signals the
L2053: use of the algorithm described in this specification.
L2054: EPUB creators MUST list the path to the obfuscated font in the CipherReference child
L2055: of the CipherData element. As the obfuscation algorithm is restricted to fonts, the
L2056: URI attribute of the CipherReference element MUST reference a Font core media type resource.
L2057: Example 10: An entry for an obfuscated font
L2058: <encryption
L2059: xmlns="urn:oasis:names:tc:opendocument:xmlns:container"
L2060: xmlns:enc="http://www.w3.org/2001/04/xmlenc#">
L2061: <enc:EncryptedData>
L2062: <enc:EncryptionMethod
L2063: Algorithm="http://www.idpf.org/2008/embedding"/>
L2064: <enc:CipherData>
L2065: <enc:CipherReference
L2066: URI="EPUB/Fonts/BKANT.TTF"/>
L2067: </enc:CipherData>
L2068: </enc:EncryptedData>
L2069: </encryption>
L2070: To prevent trivial copying of the embedded font to other EPUB publications, EPUB creators MUST NOT provide the obfuscation key in the encryption.xml
L2071: file.
L2072: 5. Package document
L2073: All [xml] elements defined in this section are in the http://www.idpf.org/2007/opf
L2074: namespace [xml-names] unless otherwise specified.
L2075: 5.1 Introduction
L2076: This section is non-normative.
L2077: The package document is an XML document that consists of a set of elements that each encapsulate
L2078: information about a particular aspect of an EPUB publication. These elements serve to centralize
L2079: metadata, detail the individual resources, and provide the reading order and other information
L2080: necessary for its rendering.
L2081: The following list summarizes the information found in the package document:
L2082: Metadata — mechanisms to include and/or reference information
L2083: about the EPUB publication.
L2084: A manifest — identifies via URL [url], and describes via
L2085: MIME media type [rfc4839], the set of publication resources.
L2086: A spine — an ordered sequence of ID references to top-level
L2087: resources in the manifest from which reading systems can reach or utilize all other
L2088: resources in the set. The spine defines the default reading order.
L2089: Collections — a method of encapsulating and identifying
L2090: subcomponents within the EPUB publication.
L2091: Manifest fallback chains — a mechanism that defines an ordered list of top-level
L2092: resources as content equivalents. A reading system can then choose between the resources
L2093: based on which it is capable of rendering.
L2094: Note
L2095: An EPUB publication can reference more than one package document, allowing for alternative
L2096: representations of the content. For more information, refer to 4.2.6.3.1 Container file (container.xml)
L2097: Note
L2098: Refer to I.1 The application/oebps-package+xml media type for information about the file
L2099: properties of package documents.
L2100: 5.2 Parsing URLs in the package document
L2101: To parse a URL
L2102: string url used in the package document, the URL
L2103: parser [url] MUST be applied to url, with the content URL of the package
L2104: document as base.
L2105: 5.3 Shared attributes
L2106: This section provides definitions for shared attributes (i.e., attributes allowed on two or more
L2107: elements).
L2108: 5.3.1 The dir attribute (under-implemented)
L2109: Note
L2110: The dir attribute is marked under-implemented
L2111: as reading systems often only support a single default directionality for text display.
L2112: EPUB creators are still strongly encouraged to set the proper directionality of text
L2113: values in the package document to ensure proper rendering once this situation
L2114: improves.
L2115: Specifies the base direction [bidi] of the textual content and
L2116: attribute values of the carrying element and its descendants.
L2117: Allowed values are:
L2118: ltr — left-to-right base direction;
L2119: rtl — right-to-left base direction; and
L2120: auto — base direction is determined using the Unicode Bidi Algorithm
L2121: [bidi].
L2122: Reading systems will assume the
L2123: value auto when EPUB creators omit the attribute or use an invalid value.
L2124: Note
L2125: The base direction specified in the dir attribute does not affect the ordering
L2126: of characters within directional runs, only the relative ordering of those runs and the
L2127: placement of weak directional characters such as punctuation.
L2128: Example 11: Setting the global base direction for package document text
L2129: <package … dir="ltr">
L2130: …
L2131: </package>
L2132: Allowed on: collection, Dublin Core elements, meta, and
L2133: package.
L2134: 5.3.2 The href attribute
L2135: A valid URL string [url] that references a resource.
L2136: The URL string MUST NOT reference resources via elements in the package document (e.g., via a
L2137: manifest item or spine itemref declaration).
L2138: Example 12: Linking a metadata record
L2139: <package …>
L2140: <metadata …>
L2141: …
L2142: <link
L2143: rel="record"
L2144: href="meta/9780000000001.xml"
L2145: media-type="application/marc"/>
L2146: …
L2147: </metadata>
L2148: …
L2149: </package>
L2150: Allowed on: item and link.
L2151: 5.3.3 The id attribute
L2152: The ID [xml] of the element, which MUST be unique within the document
L2153: scope.
L2154: Example 13: Adding an identifier attribute
L2155: <dc:title id="pub-title">The Lord of the Rings</dc:title>
L2156: Allowed on: collection, Dublin Core elements, item,
L2157: itemref, link, manifest, meta, package, and spine.
L2158: 5.3.4 The media-type attribute
L2159: A media type [rfc2046] that specifies the type and format of the referenced resource.
L2160: Example 14: Adding the media type for a linked record
L2161: <package …>
L2162: <metadata …>
L2163: …
L2164: <link
L2165: rel="record"
L2166: href="http://example.org/meta/12389347?format=onix"
L2167: media-type="application/xml"
L2168: properties="onix"/>
L2169: …
L2170: </metadata>
L2171: …
L2172: </package>
L2173: Allowed on: item and link.
L2174: 5.3.5 The properties attribute
L2175: A space-separated list of property values.
L2176: Refer to each element's definition for the reserved vocabulary
L2177: for the attribute.
L2178: Example 15: Identifying the EPUB navigation document in the manifest
L2179: <package …>
L2180: …
L2181: <manifest>
L2182: …
L2183: <item
L2184: id="nav"
L2185: href="nav.xhtml"
L2186: properties="nav"
L2187: media-type="application/xhtml+xml"/>
L2188: …
L2189: </manifest>
L2190: …
L2191: </package>
L2192: Allowed on: item, itemref, and link.
L2193: 5.3.6 The refines attribute
L2194: Establishes an association between the current expression and the element or resource identified
L2195: by its value. EPUB creators MUST use as the value a path-relative-scheme-less-URL string, optionally followed by U+0023 (#) and a URL-fragment string that
L2196: references the resource or element they are describing.
L2197: Example 16: Specifying that a creator is the illustrator
L2198: <package …>
L2199: <metadata …>
L2200: …
L2201: <dc:creator id="creator02">
L2202: E.H. Shepard
L2203: </dc:creator>
L2204: <meta
L2205: refines="#creator02"
L2206: property="role"
L2207: scheme="marc:relators">
L2208: ill
L2209: </meta>
L2210: …
L2211: </metadata>
L2212: …
L2213: </package>
L2214: The refines attribute is OPTIONAL depending on the type of metadata expressed. When
L2215: omitted, the element defines a primary expression.
L2216: When creating expressions about a publication resource, the refines attribute
L2217: SHOULD specify a fragment identifier that references the ID [xml] of
L2218: the resource's manifest entry.
L2219: Refinement chains MUST NOT contain circular references or self-references.
L2220: Example 17: Setting the duration of a media overlay document
L2221: <package …>
L2222: <metadata …>
L2223: …
L2224: <meta
L2225: property="media:duration"
L2226: refines="#c01_overlay">
L2227: 0:32:29
L2228: </meta>
L2229: …
L2230: </metadata>
L2231: <manifest>
L2232: …
L2233: <item
L2234: id="c01_overlay"
L2235: href="overlays/chapter01.smil"
L2236: media-type="application/smil+xml"/>
L2237: …
L2238: </manifest>
L2239: …
L2240: </package>
L2241: Allowed on: link and meta.
L2242: 5.3.7 The xml:lang attribute
L2243: Specifies the language of the textual content and attribute values of the carrying element and
L2244: its descendants, as defined in section 2.12 Language
L2245: Identification of [xml]. The value of each xml:lang attribute MUST be a
L2246: well-formed language tag [bcp47].
L2247: Example 18: Setting the global language for package document text
L2248: <package … xml:lang="ja">
L2249: …
L2250: </package>
L2251: Allowed on: collection, Dublin Core elements, meta, and
L2252: package.
L2253: 5.4 The package element
L2254: The package element encapsulates all the information expressed in the package document.
L2255: Element Name:
L2256: package
L2257: Usage:
L2258: REQUIRED root element [xml] of the package document.
L2259: Attributes:
L2260: dir
L2261: [optional]
L2262: id
L2263: [optional]
L2264: prefix
L2265: [optional]
L2266: xml:lang
L2267: [optional]
L2268: unique-identifier
L2269: [required]
L2270: version
L2271: [required]
L2272: Content Model:
L2273: In this order:
L2274: metadata [exactly 1]
L2275: manifest [exactly 1]
L2276: spine [exactly 1]
L2277: guide
L2278: [0 or 1]
L2279: (legacy)
L2280: bindings
L2281: [0 or 1]
L2282: (deprecated)
L2283: collection [0 or more]
L2284: The version attribute specifies the EPUB specification
L2285: version to which the given EPUB publication conforms. The attribute MUST have the value
L2286: "3.0" to indicate conformance with EPUB 3.
L2287: Note
L2288: Updates to this specification do not represent new versions of EPUB 3 (i.e., each new 3.X
L2289: specification is a continuation of the EPUB 3 format). The Working Group is committed to
L2290: minimizing any changes that would invalidate existing content, allowing the version
L2291: attribute value to remain unchanged.
L2292: The unique-identifier attribute takes an IDREF
L2293: [xml] that identifies the dc:identifier element that provides the preferred, or primary,
L2294: identifier.
L2295: The prefix attribute provides a declaration mechanism for
L2296: prefixes not reserved by this specification. Refer to
L2297: D.1.4 The prefix attribute for more information.
L2298: 5.5 Metadata section
L2299: 5.5.1 The metadata element
L2300: The metadata element encapsulates meta information.
L2301: Element Name:
L2302: metadata
L2303: Usage:
L2304: REQUIRED first child of package.
L2305: Attributes:
L2306: None
L2307: Content Model:
L2308: In any order:
L2309: dc:identifier [1 or more]
L2310: dc:title [1 or more]
L2311: dc:language [1 or more]
L2312: Dublin Core Optional Elements
L2313: [0 or more]
L2314: meta [1 or more]
L2315: OPF2 meta
L2316: [0 or more]
L2317: (legacy)
L2318: link [0 or more]
L2319: The package document metadata element has two primary functions:
L2320: to provide a minimal set of meta information for reading systems to use to internally
L2321: catalogue an EPUB publication and make it available to a user (e.g., to present in a
L2322: bookshelf).
L2323: to provide access to all rendering metadata needed to control the layout and display of
L2324: the content (e.g., fixed-layout properties).
L2325: The package document does not provide complex metadata encoding capabilities. If EPUB creators need to provide more detailed information, they can associate metadata records (e.g.,
L2326: that conform to an international standard such as [onix] or are created for custom purposes)
L2327: using the link element. This approach allows reading systems to process the metadata in its
L2328: native form, avoiding the potential problems and information loss caused by translating to use
L2329: the minimal package document structure.
L2330: In keeping with this philosophy, the package document only has the
L2331: following minimal metadata requirements: it MUST contain the [dcterms] dc:title,
L2332: dc:identifier, and dc:language elements together with the [dcterms]
L2333: dcterms:modified property. All other metadata is OPTIONAL.
L2334: Example 19: The minimal set of metadata required in the package document
L2335: <package … unique-identifier="pub-id">
L2336: …
L2337: <metadata …>
L2338: <dc:identifier
L2339: id="pub-id">
L2340: urn:uuid:A1B0D67E-2E81-4DF5-9E67-A64CBE366809
L2341: </dc:identifier>
L2342: <dc:title>
L2343: Norwegian Wood
L2344: </dc:title>
L2345: <dc:language>
L2346: en
L2347: </dc:language>
L2348: <meta
L2349: property="dcterms:modified">
L2350: 2011-01-01T12:00:00Z
L2351: </meta>
L2352: </metadata>
L2353: …
L2354: </package>
L2355: The meta element provides a generic mechanism for including metadata properties from any vocabulary. Although EPUB creators MAY use this mechanism
L2356: for any metadata purposes, they will typically use it to include rendering metadata defined in
L2357: EPUB specifications.
L2358: Note
L2359: See [epub-a11y-11] for accessibility metadata recommendations.
L2360: 5.5.2 Metadata values
L2361: The Dublin Core elements [dcterms] and meta element have mandatory child text content
L2362: [dom]. In the descriptions for these elements, this specification refers to this content as
L2363: the element's value.
L2364: These elements MUST have non-empty values after leading and trailing ASCII
L2365: whitespace [infra] is stripped (i.e., they must consist of at least one
L2366: non-whitespace character).
L2367: Whitespace within these element values is not significant. Sequences of one or more whitespace
L2368: characters are collapsed to a single
L2369: space [infra] during processing .
L2370: 5.5.3 Dublin Core
L2371: 5.5.3.1 Required elements
L2372: 5.5.3.1.1 The dc:identifier element
L2373: The dc:identifier element [dcterms] contains an identifier such as a UUID, DOI or ISBN.
L2374: Element Name:
L2375: dc:identifier
L2376: Namespace:
L2377: http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/
L2378: Usage:
L2379: REQUIRED child of metadata. Repeatable.
L2380: Attributes:
L2381: id
L2382: [conditionally required]
L2383: Content Model:
L2384: Text
L2385: The EPUB creator MUST provide an identifier that is unique to one and only one EPUB publication — its unique identifier — in an dc:identifier element.
L2386: This dc:identifier element MUST specify an id attribute whose
L2387: value is referenced from the package element's unique-identifier
L2388: attribute.
L2389: Example 20: Specifying the element with the unique identifier
L2390: <package … unique-identifier="pub-id">
L2391: <metadata …>
L2392: <dc:identifier id="pub-id">
L2393: urn:uuid:A1B0D67E-2E81-4DF5-9E67-A64CBE366809
L2394: </dc:identifier>
L2395: …
L2396: </metadata>
L2397: </package>
L2398: Although not static, EPUB creators should make changes to the unique identifier for an
L2399: EPUB publication as infrequently as possible. Unique Identifiers should have maximal
L2400: persistence both for referencing and distribution purposes. EPUB creators should not
L2401: issue new identifiers when making minor revisions such as updating metadata, fixing
L2402: errata, or making similar minor changes.
L2403: EPUB creators MAY specify additional identifiers.
L2404: Note
L2405: EPUB creators are advised to use absolute-URL strings
L2406: [url] for identifiers whenever possible. The inclusion of a domain owned by the EPUB
L2407: creator can improve the uniqueness of the identifier, for example, while the use of
L2408: a URN with a namespace identifier [rfc8141] improves processing by reading
L2409: systems.
L2410: EPUB creators MAY use the identifier-type
L2411: property to indicate that the value of a dc:identifier element
L2412: conforms to an established system or an issuing authority granted it.
L2413: Example 21: Specifying the type of the identifier
L2414: In this example, the identifier-type property is used with the ONIX codelist 5 scheme to indicate
L2415: the product identifier type is a DOI (i.e., the
L2416: value 06 in codelist 5 is for DOIs).
L2417: <metadata …>
L2418: <dc:identifier
L2419: id="pub-id">
L2420: urn:doi:10.1016/j.iheduc.2008.03.001
L2421: </dc:identifier>
L2422: <meta
L2423: refines="#pub-id"
L2424: property="identifier-type"
L2425: scheme="onix:codelist5">
L2426: 06
L2427: </meta>
L2428: …
L2429: </metadata>
L2430: 5.5.3.1.2 The dc:title element
L2431: The dc:title element [dcterms] represents an instance of a name for the
L2432: EPUB publication.
L2433: Element Name:
L2434: dc:title
L2435: Namespace:
L2436: http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/
L2437: Usage:
L2438: REQUIRED child of metadata. Repeatable.
L2439: Attributes:
L2440: dir
L2441: [optional]
L2442: id
L2443: [optional]
L2444: xml:lang
L2445: [optional]
L2446: Content Model:
L2447: Text
L2448: The first dc:title element in document order is the main
L2449: title of the EPUB publication (i.e., the primary one reading systems present to
L2450: users).
L2451: Example 22: A basic title element
L2452: <metadata …>
L2453: <dc:title>
L2454: Norwegian Wood
L2455: </dc:title>
L2456: …
L2457: </metadata>
L2458: EPUB creators should use only a single dc:title element to ensure
L2459: consistent rendering of the title in reading systems.
L2460: Note
L2461: Although it is possible to include more than one dc:title element for
L2462: multipart titles, reading system support for additional dc:title
L2463: elements is inconsistent. Reading systems may ignore the additional segments or
L2464: combine them in unexpected ways.
L2465: For example, the following example shows a basic multipart title:
L2466: <metadata …>
L2467: <dc:title>
L2468: THE LORD OF THE RINGS
L2469: </dc:title>
L2470: <dc:title>
L2471: Part One: The Fellowship of the Ring
L2472: </dc:title>
L2473: …
L2474: </metadata>
L2475: The same title could instead be expressed using a single dc:title
L2476: element as follows:
L2477: <metadata …>
L2478: <dc:title>
L2479: THE LORD OF THE RINGS, Part One:
L2480: The Fellowship of the Ring
L2481: </dc:title>
L2482: …
L2483: </metadata>
L2484: Previous versions of this specification recommended using the title-type and display-seq properties to identify and
L2485: format the segments of multipart titles (see the Great
L2486: Cookbooks example). It is still possible to add these semantics, but they
L2487: are also not well supported.
L2488: 5.5.3.1.3 The dc:language element
L2489: The dc:language element [dcterms] specifies the language of the content of
L2490: the EPUB publication.
L2491: Element Name:
L2492: dc:language
L2493: Namespace:
L2494: http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/
L2495: Usage:
L2496: REQUIRED child of metadata. Repeatable.
L2497: Attributes:
L2498: id
L2499: [optional]
L2500: Content Model:
L2501: Text
L2502: The value of each dc:language element MUST be a well-formed language tag [bcp47].
L2503: Example 23: Specifying U.S. English as the language of the EPUB publication
L2504: <metadata …>
L2505: …
L2506: <dc:language>
L2507: en-US
L2508: </dc:language>
L2509: …
L2510: </metadata>
L2511: Although EPUB creators MAY specify additional dc:language elements for
L2512: multilingual Publications, reading systems will treat the first
L2513: dc:language element in document order as the primary language of the
L2514: EPUB publication.
L2515: Note
L2516: Publication resources do not inherit their language from the
L2517: dc:language element(s). EPUB creators must set the language of a
L2518: resource using the intrinsic methods of the format.
L2519: 5.5.3.2 Optional elements
L2520: 5.5.3.2.1 General definition
L2521: All [dcterms] elements except for dc:identifier, dc:language, and dc:title
L2522: are designated as OPTIONAL. These elements conform to the following generalized
L2523: definition:
L2524: Element Name:
L2525: dc:contributor | dc:coverage | dc:creator
L2526: | dc:date | dc:description | dc:format |
L2527: dc:publisher | dc:relation |
L2528: dc:rights | dc:source | dc:subject |
L2529: dc:type
L2530: Namespace:
L2531: http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/
L2532: Usage:
L2533: OPTIONAL child of metadata. Repeatable.
L2534: Attributes:
L2535: dir
L2536: [optional]
L2537: id
L2538: [optional]
L2539: xml:lang
L2540: [optional]
L2541: Content Model:
L2542: Text
L2543: This specification does not modify the [dcterms] element definitions except as noted in
L2544: the following sections.
L2545: 5.5.3.2.2 The dc:contributor element
L2546: The dc:contributor element [dcterms] is used to represent the
L2547: name of a person, organization, etc. that played a secondary role in the creation of the
L2548: content.
L2549: The requirements for the dc:contributor element are identical to those for
L2550: the dc:creator element in all other respects.
L2551: 5.5.3.2.3 The dc:creator element
L2552: The dc:creator element [dcterms] represents the name of a
L2553: person, organization, etc. responsible for the creation of the content. EPUB creators MAY associate a role property with the element to indicate the function the
L2554: creator played.
L2555: Example 24: Specifying that a creator is an author
L2556: In this example, the MARC
L2557: relators scheme is used to indicate the role (i.e., the value
L2558: aut indicates an author in MARC).
L2559: <metadata …>
L2560: …
L2561: <dc:creator
L2562: id="creator">
L2563: Haruki Murakami
L2564: </dc:creator>
L2565: <meta
L2566: refines="#creator"
L2567: property="role"
L2568: scheme="marc:relators"
L2569: id="role">
L2570: aut
L2571: </meta>
L2572: …
L2573: </metadata>
L2574: The dc:creator element should contain the name of the creator as EPUB
L2575: creators intend reading systems to display it to users.
L2576: EPUB creators MAY use the file-as property
L2577: to associate a normalized form of the creator's name, and
L2578: the alternate-script property to represent
L2579: the creator's name in another language or script.
L2580: Example 25: Expressing sorting and rendering information for a creator
L2581: <metadata …>
L2582: …
L2583: <dc:creator
L2584: id="creator">
L2585: Haruki Murakami
L2586: </dc:creator>
L2587: <meta
L2588: refines="#creator"
L2589: property="alternate-script"
L2590: xml:lang="ja">
L2591: 村上 春樹
L2592: </meta>
L2593: <meta
L2594: refines="#creator"
L2595: property="file-as">
L2596: Murakami, Haruki
L2597: </meta>
L2598: …
L2599: </metadata>
L2600: If an EPUB publication has more than one creator, EPUB creators should specify each
L2601: in a separate dc:creator element.
L2602: The document order of dc:creator elements in the metadata
L2603: section determines the display priority, where the first dc:creator element
L2604: encountered is the primary creator.
L2605: Example 26: Expressing the primary creator
L2606: In this example, Lewis Carroll is the primary creator because he is listed first.
L2607: <metadata …>
L2608: …
L2609: <dc:creator
L2610: id="creator01">
L2611: Lewis Carroll
L2612: </dc:creator>
L2613: <dc:creator
L2614: id="creator02">
L2615: John Tenniel
L2616: </dc:creator>
L2617: …
L2618: </metadata>
L2619: EPUB creators should represent secondary contributors using the dc:contributor
L2620: element.
L2621: 5.5.3.2.4 The dc:date element
L2622: The dc:date element [dcterms] defines the publication date of the
L2623: EPUB publication. The publication date is not the same as the last modified date (the last time the EPUB creator changed the EPUB publication).
L2624: It is RECOMMENDED that the date string conform to [iso8601-1], particularly the subset
L2625: expressed in W3C Date and Time Formats [datetime], as such strings are both human and
L2626: machine readable.
L2627: Example 27: Expressing the publication date
L2628: <metadata …>
L2629: …
L2630: <dc:date>
L2631: 2000-01-01T00:00:00Z
L2632: </dc:date>
L2633: …
L2634: </metadata>
L2635: EPUB creators should express additional dates using the specialized date properties
L2636: available in the [dcterms] vocabulary, or similar.
L2637: EPUB publications MUST NOT contain more than one dc:date element.
L2638: 5.5.3.2.5 The dc:subject element
L2639: The dc:subject element [dcterms] identifies the subject of the
L2640: EPUB publication. EPUB creators should set the value of the element to the
L2641: human-readable heading or label, but may use a code value if the subject taxonomy does
L2642: not provide a separate descriptive label.
L2643: EPUB creators MAY identify the system or scheme they drew the element's value from
L2644: using the authority property.
L2645: When a scheme is identified, EPUB creators MUST associate a
L2646: subject code using the term property.
L2647: Example 28: Specifying a BISAC code and heading
L2648: <metadata …>
L2649: <dc:subject id="subject01">
L2650: FICTION / Occult &amp; Supernatural
L2651: </dc:subject>
L2652: <meta
L2653: refines="#subject01"
L2654: property="authority">
L2655: BISAC
L2656: </meta>
L2657: <meta
L2658: refines="#subject01"
L2659: property="term">
L2660: FIC024000
L2661: </meta>
L2662: </metadata>
L2663: Example 29: Specifying a URL for the scheme
L2664: <metadata …>
L2665: <dc:subject id="sbj01">
L2666: Number Theory
L2667: </dc:subject>
L2668: <meta
L2669: refines="#sbj01"
L2670: property="authority">
L2671: http://www.ams.org/msc/msc2010.html
L2672: </meta>
L2673: <meta
L2674: refines="#sbj01"
L2675: property="term">
L2676: 11
L2677: </meta>
L2678: </metadata>
L2679: The term property MUST NOT be associated with a
L2680: dc:subject element that does not specify a scheme.
L2681: The values of the dc:subject element and term property are
L2682: case sensitive only when the designated scheme requires.
L2683: 5.5.3.2.6 The dc:type element
L2684: The dc:type element [dcterms] is used to indicate that the EPUB publication is of a specialized type (e.g., annotations or a dictionary packaged in
L2685: EPUB format).
L2686: EPUB creators MAY use any text string as a value.
L2687: Note
L2688: The former IDPF EPUB 3
L2689: Working Group maintained a non-normative registry of specialized EPUB publication types for use with
L2690: this element. This Working Group no longer maintains the registry and does not
L2691: anticipate developing new specialized publication types.
L2692: 5.5.4 The meta element
L2693: The meta element provides a generic means of including package metadata.
L2694: Element Name:
L2695: meta
L2696: Usage:
L2697: As child of the metadata element. Repeatable.
L2698: Attributes:
L2699: dir
L2700: [optional]
L2701: id
L2702: [optional]
L2703: property
L2704: [required]
L2705: refines
L2706: [optional]
L2707: scheme
L2708: [optional]
L2709: xml:lang
L2710: [optional]
L2711: Content Model:
L2712: Text
L2713: Each meta element defines a metadata expression. The
L2714: property attribute takes a property
L2715: data type value that defines the statement made in the expression, and the text content
L2716: of the element represents the assertion. (Refer to D.1 Vocabulary association mechanisms for more
L2717: information.)
L2718: This specification defines two types of metadata expressions that EPUB creators can define using the meta element:
L2719: A primary expression is one in which the expression defined
L2720: in the meta element establishes some aspect of the EPUB publication. A
L2721: meta element that omits a refines attribute defines a primary
L2722: expression.
L2723: A subexpression is one in which the expression defined in the
L2724: meta element is associated with another expression or resource using the
L2725: refines attribute to enhance its meaning. A subexpression might refine a
L2726: media clip, for example, by expressing its duration, or refine a creator or contributor
L2727: expression by defining the role of the person.
L2728: EPUB creators MAY use subexpressions to refine the meaning of other subexpressions, thereby
L2729: creating chains of information.
L2730: Note
L2731: All the [dcterms] elements represent primary expressions, and permit refinement by
L2732: meta element subexpressions.
L2733: The Meta Properties Vocabulary is the default vocabulary for use with the property
L2734: attribute.
L2735: EPUB creators MAY add terms from other vocabularies as defined in D.1 Vocabulary association mechanisms.
L2736: Example 30: Using properties with reserved prefixes
L2737: For the full list of reserved prefixes, refer to D.1.5 Reserved prefixes.
L2738: <metadata …>
L2739: …
L2740: <meta
L2741: property="dcterms:modified">
L2742: 2016-02-29T12:34:56Z
L2743: </meta>
L2744: <meta
L2745: property="rendition:layout">
L2746: pre-paginated
L2747: </meta>
L2748: <meta
L2749: property="media:active-class">
L2750: my-active-item
L2751: </meta>
L2752: …
L2753: </metadata>
L2754: The scheme attribute identifies the system or scheme the EPUB
L2755: creator obtained the element's value from. The value of the attribute MUST be a property data type value that resolves to the
L2756: resource that defines the scheme. The scheme attribute does not have a default vocabulary (i.e., all values require a prefix).
L2757: Example 31: Using values from a scheme
L2758: In this example, the scheme attribute indicates that the value of the tag is
L2759: from [onix] code list 5 (i.e., the value 15 indicates a 13 digit ISBN).
L2760: <metadata …>
L2761: …
L2762: <meta
L2763: refines="#isbn-id"
L2764: property="identifier-type"
L2765: scheme="onix:codelist5">
L2766: 15
L2767: </meta>
L2768: …
L2769: </metadata>
L2770: 5.5.5 Last modified date
L2771: The metadata section MUST contain exactly one dcterms:modified property [dcterms]
L2772: containing the last modification date. The value of this property MUST be an [iso8601-1]
L2773: complete representation of a date and time of day matching the extended format:
L2774: YYYY-MM-DDThh:mm:ssZ
L2775: EPUB creators MUST express the last modification date in Coordinated Universal Time (UTC) and
L2776: MUST terminate it with the "Z" (Zulu) time zone indicator.
L2777: Example 32: Expressing a last modification date
L2778: <metadata …>
L2779: …
L2780: <meta
L2781: property="dcterms:modified">
L2782: 2016-01-01T00:00:01Z
L2783: </meta>
L2784: …
L2785: </metadata>
L2786: EPUB creators should update the last modified date whenever they make changes to the EPUB publication.
L2787: EPUB creators MAY specify additional modified properties in the package document metadata,
L2788: but they MUST have a different subject (i.e., they require a refines attribute that
L2789: references an element or resource).
L2790: Note
L2791: The requirements for the last modification date are to ensure compatibility with earlier
L2792: versions of EPUB 3 that defined a release identifier [epubpackages-32] for EPUB publications.
L2793: 5.5.6 The link element
L2794: The link element associates resources with an EPUB publication, such as metadata
L2795: records.
L2796: Element Name:
L2797: link
L2798: Usage:
L2799: As a child of metadata. Repeatable.
L2800: Attributes:
L2801: href
L2802: [required]
L2803: hreflang
L2804: [optional]
L2805: id
L2806: [optional]
L2807: media-type
L2808: [conditionally required]
L2809: properties
L2810: [optional]
L2811: refines
L2812: [optional]
L2813: rel
L2814: [required]
L2815: Content Model:
L2816: Empty
L2817: The metadata element MAY contain zero or more link elements, each of which
L2818: identifies the location of a publication resource or a linked resource in its REQUIRED
L2819: href attribute.
L2820: Resources referenced from the link element are
L2821: publication resources only when they are:
L2822: referenced from the spine; or
L2823: included or embedded in an EPUB content document (e.g., a metadata record serialized
L2824: as RDFa [rdfa-core] or as JSON-LD [json-ld11] embedded in an
L2825: [html] script element).
L2826: In all other cases (e.g., when linking to standalone [onix] records), the resources referenced
L2827: are not publication resources (i.e., are not subject to core
L2828: media type requirements) and EPUB creators MUST NOT list them in the manifest.
L2829: Example 33: Reference to a record embedded in an XHTML content document
L2830: In this example, the metadata record is embedded in a script element. Note that
L2831: the media type of the embedded record (i.e., application/ld+json) is obtained
L2832: from the type attribute on the script element; it is not specified
L2833: in the link element.
L2834: Package document:
L2835: <package …>
L2836: <metadata …>
L2837: …
L2838: <link rel="record"
L2839: href="front.xhtml#meta-json"
L2840: media-type="application/xhtml+xml"
L2841: hreflang="en"/>
L2842: …
L2843: </metadata>
L2844: …
L2845: </package>
L2846: XHTML:
L2847: <html …>
L2848: <head>
L2849: …
L2850: <script id="meta-json" type="application/ld+json">
L2851: "@context" : "http://schema.org",
L2852: "name" : "…",
L2853: …
L2854: </script>
L2855: …
L2856: </head>
L2857: <body>
L2858: …
L2859: </body>
L2860: </html>
L2861: EPUB creators MAY locate linked resources within the EPUB container
L2862: or externally, but should consider that reading systems are not required to retrieve
L2863: resources outside the EPUB container.
L2864: The media-type
L2865: attribute is OPTIONAL when a linked resource is located outside the EPUB container, as
L2866: more than one media type could be served from the same URL [url]. EPUB creators MUST specify
L2867: the attribute for all linked resources within the EPUB container.
L2868: The OPTIONAL hreflang attribute identifies the language of the
L2869: linked resource. The value MUST be a well-formed language
L2870: tag [bcp47].
L2871: The REQUIRED rel attribute takes a space-separated list of property values that establish the relationship the linked
L2872: resource has with the EPUB publication.
L2873: Example 34: Linking to a MARC XML record
L2874: <metadata …>
L2875: …
L2876: <link
L2877: rel="record"
L2878: href="meta/9780000000001.xml"
L2879: media-type="application/marc"/>
L2880: …
L2881: </metadata>
L2882: The value of the media-type attribute is not always sufficient to identify the type
L2883: of linked resource (e.g., many XML-based record formats use the media type
L2884: "application/xml"). To aid reading systems in the identification of such
L2885: generic resources, EPUB creators MAY specify a semantic identifier in the
L2886: properties attribute.
L2887: Example 35: Identifying a record type via a property
L2888: In this example, the properties attribute identifies the link is to an ONIX
L2889: record.
L2890: <metadata …>
L2891: …
L2892: <link rel="record"
L2893: href="http://example.org/meta/12389347?format=onix"
L2894: media-type="application/xml"
L2895: properties="onix"/>
L2896: …
L2897: </metadata>
L2898: The Metadata Link Vocabulary is the default vocabulary for the rel and properties attributes.
L2899: EPUB creators MAY add relationships and properties from other vocabularies as defined in D.1 Vocabulary association mechanisms.
L2900: Example 36: Declaring a new link relationship
L2901: In this example, the link element is used to associate an author's home page
L2902: using the FOAF vocabulary. Note that as foaf is not a reserved prefix, it must be declared in the
L2903: prefix attribute.
L2904: <package
L2905: …
L2906: prefix="foaf: http://xmlns.com/foaf/spec/">
L2907: <metadata …>
L2908: …
L2909: <link
L2910: refines="#creator01"
L2911: rel="foaf:homepage"
L2912: href="http://example.org/book-info/12389347" />
L2913: …
L2914: </metadata>
L2915: …
L2916: </package>
L2917: EPUB creators MAY provide one or more linked metadata records.
L2918: Example 37: Specifying linked records
L2919: In this example, an ONIX record is hosted remotely while a JSON-LD record is included in the
L2920: EPUB container.
L2921: <metadata …>
L2922: <link rel="record"
L2923: href="http://example.org/onix/12389347"
L2924: media-type="application/xml"
L2925: properties="onix" />
L2926: <link rel="record"
L2927: href="meta/meta.jsonld"
L2928: media-type="application/ld+json" />
L2929: …
L2930: </metadata>
L2931: Note
L2932: Due to the variety of metadata record formats and serializations that an EPUB creator can
L2933: link to an EPUB publication, and the complexity of comparing metadata properties between
L2934: them, this specification does not require reading systems to process linked records.
L2935: In addition to full records, EPUB creators MAY also use the link element to identify
L2936: individual metadata properties available in an alternative format.
L2937: Example 38: Link to a description
L2938: In this example, the description of the EPUB publication is contained in an HTML
L2939: document.
L2940: <metadata …>
L2941: …
L2942: <link
L2943: rel="dcterms:description"
L2944: href="description.html"
L2945: media-type="text/html"/>
L2946: …
L2947: </metadata>
L2948: 5.6 Manifest section
L2949: 5.6.1 The manifest element
L2950: The manifest element provides an exhaustive list of publication resources used
L2951: in the rendering of the content.
L2952: Element Name:
L2953: manifest
L2954: Usage:
L2955: REQUIRED second child of package, following metadata.
L2956: Attributes:
L2957: id
L2958: [optional]
L2959: Content Model:
L2960: item [1 or more]
L2961: With the exception of the package document, the
L2962: manifest MUST list all publication resources regardless of whether they are
L2963: container resources or remote resources.
L2964: As the package document is already identified by the container.xml file, the
L2965: manifest MUST NOT specify an item element for it (i.e., a
L2966: self-reference serves no purpose).
L2967: Note
L2968: The manifest is only for listing publication resources. Linked resources and the special files for processing the OCF
L2969: Container (i.e., files in the META-INF directory, and the
L2970: mimetype file) are restricted from inclusion.
L2971: Failure to provide a complete manifest of publication resources may lead to rendering issues.
L2972: Reading systems might not unzip such resources or could prevent access to them for
L2973: security reasons.
L2974: 5.6.2 The item element
L2975: The item element represents a publication resource.
L2976: Element Name:
L2977: item
L2978: Usage:
L2979: As a child of manifest. Repeatable.
L2980: Attributes:
L2981: fallback
L2982: [conditionally required]
L2983: href
L2984: [required]
L2985: id
L2986: [required]
L2987: media-overlay
L2988: [optional]
L2989: media-type
L2990: [required]
L2991: properties
L2992: [optional]
L2993: Content Model:
L2994: Empty
L2995: Each item element identifies a publication resource by the
L2996: URL [url] in its href attribute. The value MUST be an
L2997: absolute- or path-relative-scheme-less-URL
L2998: string [url]. EPUB creators MUST ensure each URL is unique within the
L2999: manifest scope after parsing.
L3000: The publication resource identified by an item element
L3001: MUST conform to the applicable specification(s) as inferred from the MIME media type [rfc2046]
L3002: provided in the media-type attribute. For core media type resources, EPUB creators MUST use the media type designated in 3.2 Core media types.
L3003: The fallback attribute specifies the fallback for the
L3004: referenced publication resource. The fallback attribute's IDREF [xml] value MUST resolve to another item in the
L3005: manifest.
L3006: The fallback for one item MAY specify a fallback to another item, and
L3007: so on, creating a chain of fallback options. Refer to 3.5.1 Manifest fallbacks for
L3008: additional requirements related to the use of fallback chains.
L3009: The media-overlay attribute takes an IDREF [xml]
L3010: that identifies the media overlay document for the resource described by this
L3011: item. Refer to 9.3.5 Media overlays packaging for more information.
L3012: Note
L3013: The order of item elements in the manifest is not significant. The
L3014: spine element provides the presentation sequence of content documents.
L3015: 5.6.2.1 Resource properties
L3016: The properties attribute provides information
L3017: to reading systems about the content of a resource. This information enables discovery
L3018: of key resources, such as the cover image and EPUB navigation document. It also allows
L3019: reading systems to optimize rendering by indicating, for example, whether the resource
L3020: contains embedded scripting, MathML, or SVG.
L3021: The Manifest Properties
L3022: Vocabulary is the default vocabulary for the
L3023: properties attribute.
L3024: EPUB creators MUST set the following properties whenever a resource referenced by an
L3025: item element matches their respective definitions:
L3026: mathml
L3027: remote-resources
L3028: scripted
L3029: svg
L3030: switch
L3031: Example 39: Identifying a scripted content document with embedded MathML
L3032: <item
L3033: properties="scripted mathml"
L3034: id="c2"
L3035: href="c2.xhtml"
L3036: media-type="application/xhtml+xml" />
L3037: These properties do not apply recursively to content included into a resource (e.g., via the
L3038: [html] iframe element). For example, if a non-scripted XHTML content document
L3039: embeds a scripted content document, only the embedded document's manifest
L3040: item
L3041: properties attribute will have the scripted value.
L3042: EPUB creators MUST declare exactly one item as the EPUB navigation document
L3043: using the nav property.
L3044: Example 40: Identifying the EPUB navigation document
L3045: <item
L3046: properties="nav"
L3047: id="c1"
L3048: href="c1.xhtml"
L3049: media-type="application/xhtml+xml" />
L3050: If an EPUB publication contains a cover image, it is recommended to set the cover-image property, but setting this property
L3051: is OPTIONAL.
L3052: Example 41: Identifying the cover image
L3053: <item
L3054: properties="cover-image"
L3055: id="ci"
L3056: href="cover.svg"
L3057: media-type="image/svg+xml" />
L3058: EPUB creators MAY add terms from other vocabularies as defined in D.1 Vocabulary association mechanisms.
L3059: 5.6.2.2 Examples
L3060: Example 42: A manifest with only core media type resources
L3061: <package …>
L3062: …
L3063: <manifest>
L3064: <item
L3065: id="nav"
L3066: href="nav.xhtml"
L3067: properties="nav"
L3068: media-type="application/xhtml+xml"/>
L3069: <item
L3070: id="intro"
L3071: href="intro.xhtml"
L3072: media-type="application/xhtml+xml"/>
L3073: <item
L3074: id="c1"
L3075: href="chap1.xhtml"
L3076: media-type="application/xhtml+xml"/>
L3077: <item
L3078: id="c1-answerkey"
L3079: href="chap1-answerkey.xhtml"
L3080: media-type="application/xhtml+xml"/>
L3081: <item
L3082: id="c2"
L3083: href="chap2.xhtml"
L3084: media-type="application/xhtml+xml"/>
L3085: <item
L3086: id="c2-answerkey"
L3087: href="chap2-answerkey.xhtml"
L3088: media-type="application/xhtml+xml"/>
L3089: <item
L3090: id="c3"
L3091: href="chap3.xhtml"
L3092: media-type="application/xhtml+xml"/>
L3093: <item
L3094: id="c3-answerkey"
L3095: href="chap3-answerkey.xhtml"
L3096: media-type="application/xhtml+xml"/>
L3097: <item
L3098: id="notes"
L3099: href="notes.xhtml"
L3100: media-type="application/xhtml+xml"/>
L3101: <item
L3102: id="cover"
L3103: href="./images/cover.svg"
L3104: properties="cover-image"
L3105: media-type="image/svg+xml"/>
L3106: <item
L3107: id="f1"
L3108: href="./images/fig1.jpg"
L3109: media-type="image/jpeg"/>
L3110: <item
L3111: id="f2"
L3112: href="./images/fig2.jpg"
L3113: media-type="image/jpeg"/>
L3114: <item
L3115: id="css"
L3116: href="./style/book.css"
L3117: media-type="text/css"/>
L3118: </manifest>
L3119: …
L3120: </package>
L3121: Example 43: Foreign content document in spine with fallback
L3122: The following example shows the manifest fallback chain allowing a foreign content document (JPEG) to be listed in the spine with fallback to an SVG content
L3123: document.
L3124: <package …>
L3125: …
L3126: <manifest>
L3127: …
L3128: <item
L3129: id="page-001"
L3130: href="images/page-001.jpg"
L3131: media-type="image/jpeg"
L3132: fallback="page-001-svg"/>
L3133: <item
L3134: id="page-001-svg"
L3135: href="images/page-001.svg"
L3136: media-type="image/svg+xml"/>
L3137: …
L3138: …
L3139: </manifest>
L3140: <spine>
L3141: …
L3142: <itemref idref="page-001"/>
L3143: …
L3144: </spine>
L3145: </package>
L3146: Example 44: Embedded core media type resource with Link to View as top-level content document
L3147: The following example shows a JPEG embedded in an EPUB content document (via the
L3148: img tag) with a hyperlink that allows it to open as a separate page
L3149: (e.g., for easier zooming). Although embedding the image using the img tag
L3150: does not require it to be listed in the spine or have a fallback,
L3151: adding the hyperlink causes the document to open as a top-level content document. As
L3152: its use in the spine makes it a foreign content document, the EPUB creator must
L3153: include a fallback to an EPUB content document.
L3154: XHTML:
L3155: <html …>
L3156: …
L3157: <body>
L3158: …
L3159: <img
L3160: src="images/infographic.jpg"
L3161: alt="…"/>
L3162: <a
L3163: href="images/infographic.jpg">
L3164: Expand Image
L3165: </a>
L3166: …
L3167: </body>
L3168: </html>
L3169: Package document:
L3170: <package …>
L3171: …
L3172: <manifest>
L3173: …
L3174: <item
L3175: id="img01"
L3176: href="images/infographic.jpg"
L3177: media-type="image/jpeg"
L3178: fallback="infographic-svg"/>
L3179: <item
L3180: id="infographic-svg"
L3181: href="images/infographic.svg"

L3190: linear="no"/>
L3191: …
L3192: </spine>
L3193: </package>
L3194: Example 45: Link to View foreign resource as top-level content document
L3195: The following example shows a link to the raw CSV data file. As the data will open in the
L3196: reading system as a top-level content document, the EPUB creator must list
L3197: it in the spine. As its use in the spine makes it a foreign content document, the
L3198: EPUB creator must also provide a fallback to an EPUB content document. Because there
L3199: is no guarantee users will be able to access the data in its raw form, instructions on
L3200: how to extract the file from the EPUB container are also provided.
L3201: XHTML:
L3202: <html …>
L3203: …
L3204: <body>
L3205: …
L3206: <p>
L3207: <a href="../data/raw.csv">
L3208: [Open the raw CSV data for this project.]
L3209: </a>
L3210: </p>
L3211: <p class="small">To extract the data file
L3212: from this publication, unzip the EPUB file.
L3213: The data is located in the
L3214: <code>/EPUB/data/raw.csv</code> file.
L3215: </p>
L3216: …
L3217: </body>
L3218: </html>
L3219: Package document:
L3220: <package …>
L3221: …

L3223: …
L3224: <item
L3225: id="data01"
L3226: href="data/raw.csv"
L3227: media-type="text/csv"
L3228: fallback="data-html"/>
L3229: <item
L3230: id="data-html"
L3231: href="xhtml/data-table.html"
L3232: media-type="application/xhtml+xml"/>
L3233: …
L3234: </manifest>
L3235: <spine>
L3236: …
L3237: <itemref
L3238: idref="data01"
L3239: linear="no"/>
L3240: …
L3241: </spine>
L3242: </package>
L3243: Example 46: Remote resources that are publication resources
L3244: The following example shows a reference to a remote audio file. Because the
L3245: audio element embeds the audio in its EPUB content document, the
L3246: file is considered a publication resource. The EPUB creator therefore must list
L3247: the audio file in the manifest and indicate that its host EPUB content document contains
L3248: a remote resource.
L3249: XHTML:
L3250: <html …>
L3251: …

L3256: controls="controls"/>
L3257: …

L3259: </html>
