L3276: …
L3277: </package>
L3278: Example 47: External Resources that are not publication resources
L3279: The following example shows a hyperlink to an audio file hosted on the web. Reading systems will open such external content in a new browser window; the audio file is not
L3280: rendered within the publication. In this case, the EPUB creator does not list the
L3281: file in the manifest because it is not a publication resource.
L3282: XHTML:
L3283: <html …>
L3284: …
L3285: <body>
L3286: …
L3287: <a
L3288: href="http://www.example.com/book/audio/ch01.mp4">
L3289: Listen to audio
L3290: </a>
L3291: …
L3292: </body>
L3293: </html>
L3294: Manifest:
L3295: No Entry
L3296: 5.6.3 The bindings element (deprecated)
L3297: The bindings element defines a set of custom handlers for media types not supported
L3298: by this specification.
L3299: Use of the element is deprecated.
L3300: Refer to the bindings element definition in [epubpublications-301] for more
L3301: information.
L3302: 5.7 Spine section
L3303: 5.7.1 The spine element
L3304: The spine element defines an ordered list of manifest
L3305: item references that represent the default reading order.
L3306: Element Name:
L3307: spine
L3308: Usage:
L3309: REQUIRED third child of package, following manifest.
L3310: Attributes:
L3311: id
L3312: [optional]
L3313: page-progression-direction
L3314: [optional]
L3315: toc
L3316: [optional]
L3317: (legacy)
L3318: Content Model:
L3319: itemref [1 or more]
L3320: The spine MUST specify at least one EPUB content document or foreign content document.
L3321: EPUB creators MUST list in the spine all EPUB and
L3322: foreign content documents that are hyperlinked to from publication resources in the
L3323: spine, where hyperlinking encompasses any linking mechanism that requires the
L3324: user to navigate away from the current resource. Common hyperlinking mechanisms include the
L3325: href attribute of the [html] a and area elements and scripted links (e.g., using
L3326: DOM Events and/or form elements). The requirement to list hyperlinked resources applies
L3327: recursively (i.e., EPUB creators must list all EPUB and foreign content documents hyperlinked to
L3328: from hyperlinked documents, and so on.).
L3329: EPUB creators also MUST list in the spine all EPUB and foreign content documents
L3330: hyperlinked to from the EPUB navigation document, regardless of whether EPUB creators
L3331: include the EPUB navigation document in the spine.
L3332: Note
L3333: As hyperlinks to resources outside the EPUB container are not publication resources, they
L3334: are not subject to the requirement to include in the spine (e.g., web pages and web-hosted
L3335: resources).
L3336: Publication resources used in the rendering of spine items (e.g., referenced from [html]
L3337: embedded content) similarly do not have to be included in the spine.
L3338: The page-progression-direction
L3339: attribute sets the global direction in which the content flows. Allowed values are
L3340: ltr (left-to-right), rtl (right-to-left) and default.
L3341: When EPUB creators specify the default value, they are expressing no preference and
L3342: the reading system can choose the rendering direction.
L3343: Although the page-progression-direction attribute sets the global flow direction,
L3344: individual EPUB content documents and parts of EPUB content documents MAY override this setting
L3345: (e.g., via the writing-mode CSS property). Reading systems may also provide
L3346: mechanisms to override the default direction (e.g., buttons or settings that allow the
L3347: application of alternate style sheets).
L3348: The legacy
L3349: toc attribute takes an IDREF [xml] that identifies
L3350: the manifest item that represents the NCX.
L3351: 5.7.2 The itemref element
L3352: The itemref element identifies an EPUB content document or foreign content document in the default reading order.
L3353: Element Name:
L3354: itemref
L3355: Usage:
L3356: As a child of spine. Repeatable.
L3357: Attributes:
L3358: id
L3359: [optional]
L3360: idref
L3361: [required]
L3362: linear
L3363: [optional]
L3364: properties
L3365: [optional]
L3366: Content Model:
L3367: Empty
L3368: Each itemref element MUST reference the ID [xml] of an item in the manifest via the
L3369: IDREF [xml] in its idref attribute.
L3370: item element IDs MUST NOT be referenced more than once.
L3371: Each referenced manifest item MUST be either a) an
L3372: EPUB content document or b) a foreign content document that includes an EPUB content
L3373: document in its manifest fallback chain.
L3374: Note
L3375: Although EPUB publications require an EPUB navigation
L3376: document, it is not mandatory to include it in the spine.
L3377: The linear attribute indicates whether the referenced
L3378: item contains content that contributes to the primary reading order and that
L3379: reading systems must read sequentially ("yes"), or auxiliary content that
L3380: enhances or augments the primary content that reading systems can access out of sequence
L3381: ("no"). Examples of auxiliary content include notes, descriptions, and answer
L3382: keys.
L3383: The linear attribute allows reading systems to distinguish content that a user
L3384: should access as part of the default reading order from supplementary content which a reading
L3385: system might, for example, present in a popup window or omit from an aural rendering.
L3386: Specifying that content is non-linear does not require reading systems to present it in a
L3387: specific way, however; it is only a hint to the purpose. Reading systems may present non-linear
L3388: content where it occurs in the spine, for example, or may skip it until users reach the end of
L3389: the spine.
L3390: Note
L3391: EPUB creators should list non-linear content at the end of the spine except when it makes
L3392: sense for users to encounter it between linear spine items.
L3393: A linear itemref element is one whose linear
L3394: attribute value is explicitly set to "yes" or that omits the
L3395: attribute — reading systems will assume the value "yes" for
L3396: itemref elements without the attribute. The spine MUST contain at least one
L3397: linear itemref element.
L3398: EPUB creators MUST provide a means
L3399: of accessing all non-linear content (e.g., hyperlinks in the content or from the EPUB navigation document).
L3400: The Spine Properties
L3401: Vocabulary is the default vocabulary for the
L3402: properties attribute.
L3403: EPUB creators MAY add terms from other vocabularies as defined in D.1 Vocabulary association mechanisms.
L3404: Example 48: A basic spine
L3405: In this example, the spine entries correspond to the manifest
L3406: example above.
L3407: <spine
L3408: page-progression-direction="ltr">
L3409: <itemref
L3410: idref="intro"/>
L3411: <itemref
L3412: idref="c1"/>
L3413: <itemref
L3414: idref="c1-answerkey"
L3415: linear="no"/>
L3416: <itemref
L3417: idref="c2"/>
L3418: <itemref
L3419: idref="c2-answerkey"
L3420: linear="no"/>
L3421: <itemref
L3422: idref="c3"/>
L3423: <itemref
L3424: idref="c3-answerkey"
L3425: linear="no"/>
L3426: <itemref
L3427: idref="notes"
L3428: linear="no"/>
L3429: </spine>
L3430: 5.8 Collections
L3431: 5.8.1 The collection element
L3432: The collection element defines a related group of resources.
L3433: Element Name:
L3434: collection
L3435: Usage:
L3436: OPTIONAL sixth element of package. Repeatable.
L3437: Attributes:
L3438: dir
L3439: [optional]
L3440: id
L3441: [optional]
L3442: role
L3443: [required]
L3444: xml:lang
L3445: [optional]
L3446: Content Model:
L3447: In this order: metadata
L3448: [0 or 1], ( collection [1 or more] or ( collection
L3449: [0 or more], link
L3450: [1 or more] ))
L3451: The collection element allows EPUB creators to assemble resources into logical
L3452: groups for a variety of potential uses: enabling reassembly into a meaningful unit of content
L3453: split across multiple EPUB content documents (e.g., an index split across multiple
L3454: documents), identifying resources for specialized purposes (e.g., preview content), or
L3455: collecting together resources that present additional information about the EPUB publication.
L3456: EPUB creators MUST identify the role of each collection
L3457: element in its role attribute, whose value MUST be one or more NMTOKENs
L3458: [xmlschema-2] and/or absolute-URL-with-fragment strings [url].
L3459: The requirements for authoring specialized collections are defined by their respective
L3460: specifications.
L3461: Note
L3462: The former IDPF EPUB 3 Working
L3463: Group maintained both a registry of role
L3464: extensions and a list of custom
L3465: extension roles. This Working Group no longer maintains these registries.
L3466: Example 49: A multi-document index
L3467: <collection role="index">
L3468: <link href="subjectIndex01.xhtml"/>
L3469: <link href="subjectIndex02.xhtml"/>
L3470: <link href="subjectIndex03.xhtml"/>
L3471: </collection>
L3472: 5.8.2 Defining collection types (deprecated)
L3473: The creation of new collection element roles is now deprecated.
L3474: Refer to the collection element definition in [epubpackages-32] for more
L3475: information about the creation of specialized collections, including the requirements and
L3476: restrictions on their use.
L3477: 5.9 Legacy features
L3478: 5.9.1 Introduction
L3479: The package document legacy features are retained from EPUB 2 only to allow
L3480: EPUB creators to author content that can function, to some degree, in reading systems
L3481: that only support EPUB 2 publications.
L3482: These features were added primarily to address the overlap period as EPUB 3 reading systems were
L3483: developed, as there was still a high probability at that time that users would be opening EPUB 3
L3484: publications on EPUB 2 reading systems.
L3485: As reading systems that only handle EPUB 2 publications are now rare, EPUB creators should
L3486: consider the likelihood of their publications still being opened on these types of older devices
L3487: before making the effort to add these legacy features.
L3488: 5.9.2 Support
L3489: EPUB creators MAY include the legacy features defined in this section for compatibility
L3490: purposes with EPUB 2 reading systems.
L3491: EPUB 3 reading systems will not use these features when presenting publications to users.
L3492: Note
L3493: EPUB conformance checkers should not alert EPUB creators about the presence of legacy
L3494: features in an EPUB publication, as their inclusion is valid for backwards
L3495: compatibility. EPUB conformance checkers must alert EPUB creators if a legacy feature does
L3496: not conform to its definition or otherwise breaks a usage requirement.
L3497: 5.9.3 The meta element
L3498: The meta
L3499: element [opf-201] provides a means of including generic metadata for EPUB 2 reading systems.
L3500: Refer to the meta element definition in [opf-201] for more information.
L3501: Note
L3502: The EPUB 3 meta element, which uses different attributes and requires text content,
L3503: provides metadata capabilities for EPUB 3 reading systems.
L3504: The [opf-201] meta element also allows EPUB creators to identify a cover
L3505: image for EPUB 2 reading systems. In EPUB 3, the cover image must be identified using the cover-image property on the manifest item
L3506: for the image.
L3507: 5.9.4 The guide element
L3508: The guide
L3509: element [opf-201] provides machine-processable navigation to key structures in
L3510: EPUB 2 reading systems.
L3511: Refer to the guide element definition in [opf-201] for more information.
L3512: Note
L3513: The landmarks nav in the EPUB navigation document
L3514: provides this functionality in EPUB 3 reading systems.
L3515: 5.9.5 NCX
L3516: The NCX [opf-201]
L3517: provides a table of contents for EPUB 2 reading systems.
L3518: Refer to the NCX
L3519: definition in [opf-201] for more information.
L3520: Note
L3521: The EPUB navigation document replaces the NCX for EPUB 3 reading
L3522: systems.
L3523: 6. EPUB content documents
L3524: 6.1 XHTML content documents
L3525: 6.1.1 Introduction
L3526: This section is non-normative.
L3527: This section defines a profile of [html] for creating XHTML content documents. An instance
L3528: of an XML document that conforms to this profile is a core media type resource and is
L3529: referred to in this specification as an XHTML content document.
L3530: 6.1.2 XHTML requirements
L3531: An XHTML content document:
L3532: MUST be an [html] document that conforms to the XML syntax.
L3533: MUST conform to the conformance criteria for all
L3534: document constructs defined by [html] unless explicitly overridden in 6.1.4 HTML deviations and constraints.
L3535: MAY include extensions to the [html] grammar as
L3536: defined in 6.1.3 HTML extensions, and MUST conform to all content
L3537: conformance constraints defined therein.
L3538: Unless specified otherwise, XHTML content documents inherit all definitions of semantics,
L3539: structure, and processing behaviors from the [html] specification.
L3540: Note
L3541: The recommendation that EPUB publications follow the accessibility requirements in
L3542: [epub-a11y-11] applies to XHTML content documents. See Accessibility.
L3543: 6.1.3 HTML extensions
L3544: This section defines EPUB 3 XHTML content document extensions to the underlying [html]
L3545: document model.
L3546: Note
L3547: Although [html] allows user agents to support vendor-neutral extensions, unless such extensions are listed in this section, they
L3548: are not supported features of EPUB 3.
L3549: 6.1.3.1 Structural semantics
L3550: EPUB creators MAY use the epub:type attribute in XHTML content documents to
L3551: express structural semantics.
L3552: The attribute MUST NOT be used on the head element or metadata content [html].
L3553: 6.1.3.2 RDFa
L3554: The [html-rdfa] specification defines a set of attributes that EPUB creators MAY use in
L3555: XHTML content documents to semantically enrich the content. The use of these attributes
L3556: MUST conform to the requirements defined in [html-rdfa].
L3557: The [html-rdfa] specification defines changes to the [html] content model when authors
L3558: use RDFa attributes. This modified content model is valid in XHTML content documents.
L3559: Note
L3560: The listing of RDFa does not express a preference on the part of the Working Group, only
L3561: that these attributes represent an extension of the HTML grammar. EPUB creators can also
L3562: specify microdata attributes [html] and linked data [json-ld11] in XHTML content documents as
L3563: both are natively supported.
L3564: 6.1.3.3 Content switching (deprecated)
L3565: The switch element provides a simple mechanism through which EPUB creators
L3566: can tailor the content displayed to users, one that is not dependent on the scripting
L3567: capabilities of the EPUB reading system.
L3568: Use of the element is deprecated.
L3569: Refer to the switch element definition in [epubcontentdocs-301] for more
L3570: information.
L3571: 6.1.3.4 The epub:trigger element (deprecated)
L3572: The trigger element enables the creation of markup-defined user interfaces for
L3573: controlling multimedia objects, such as audio and video playback, in both scripted and
L3574: non-scripted contexts.
L3575: Use of the element is deprecated.
L3576: Refer to the epub:trigger element definition in [epubcontentdocs-301]
L3577: for more information.
L3578: 6.1.3.5 Custom attributes
L3579: XHTML content documents MAY contain custom attributes, which are prefixed [xml-names] attributes whose namespace
L3580: URL does not include either of the following strings in its domain [url]:
L3581: w3.org
L3582: idpf.org
L3583: When using custom attributes, the content MUST remain consumable by a user without any
L3584: information loss or other significant deterioration, regardless of the reading system it
L3585: is rendered on.
L3586: Note
L3587: Custom attributes are usually defined in a reading system-specific manner and are not
L3588: intended for use by other reading systems. This specification should be extended to
L3589: provide extensions that multiple independent reading systems can use.
L3590: 6.1.4 HTML deviations and constraints
L3591: This section defines deviations from, and constraints on, the underlying [html] document model
L3592: applicable to EPUB 3 XHTML content documents.
L3593: 6.1.4.1 Embedded MathML
L3594: XHTML content documents support embedded [mathml3]. Occurrences of MathML markup MUST
L3595: conform to the constraints expressed in the MathML specification [mathml3], with the
L3596: following additional restrictions:
L3597: Presentation MathML
L3598: The math element MUST contain only Presentation MathML, except within the
L3599: annotation-xml element.
L3600: Content MathML
L3601: EPUB creators MAY include Content MathML within MathML markup in
L3602: XHTML content documents, and, when present, MUST include it within an
L3603: annotation-xml child element of a semantics
L3604: element.
L3605: When EPUB creators include Content MathML per
L3606: the previous condition, they MUST set the given annotation-xml
L3607: element's encoding attribute to either of the functionally-equivalent
L3608: values MathML-Content or application/mathml-content+xml,
L3609: and the name attribute to contentequiv.
L3610: This subset eases the implementation burden on reading systems and promotes
L3611: accessibility, while retaining compatibility with [html] user agents.
L3612: Note
L3613: The mathml property of the manifest item element indicates that an XHTML content document contains
L3614: embedded MathML.
L3615: 6.1.4.2 Embedded SVG
L3616: XHTML content documents support the embedding of SVG:
L3617: by reference — for example, from an [html]
L3618: img or object element. SVGs embedded by reference are SVG
L3619: core media types so their requirements are already defined in 6.2 SVG content documents.
L3620: by inclusion — via direct inclusion of an SVG document
L3621: fragment [svg] in an XHTML content document. SVGs embedded by inclusion have
L3622: the same content conformance constraints as those defined for SVG content documents
L3623: in 6.2.3 Restrictions on SVG.
L3624: Note
L3625: The svg property of the manifest
L3626: item element indicates that an XHTML content document contains embedded SVG (either
L3627: by reference or by inclusion).
L3628: 6.1.4.3 Discouraged constructs
L3629: This section is non-normative.
L3630: 6.1.4.3.1 The base element
L3631: The [html] base
L3632: element can be used to specify the document base URL for the purposes of
L3633: parsing URLs. When using it in an EPUB publication, the interpretation of the
L3634: base element may inadvertently result in references to remote resources. It may also cause reading systems to misinterpret the location of
L3635: hyperlinks (e.g., relative links to other documents in the publication might appear as
L3636: links to a web site if the base element specifies an absolute URL). To
L3637: avoid significant interoperability issues, EPUB creators should not use the
L3638: base element.
L3639: 6.1.4.3.2 The rp element
L3640: The [html] rp element is intended to provide a fallback
L3641: for older reading systems that do not recognize ruby markup (i.e., a parenthesis
L3642: display around ruby markup). As EPUB 3 reading systems are ruby-aware, and
L3643: can provide fallbacks, EPUB creators should not use rp elements.
L3644: 6.1.4.3.3 The embed element
L3645: Since the [html] embed
L3646: element element does not include intrinsic facilities to provide fallback
L3647: content for reading systems that do not support scripting, EPUB creators are
L3648: discouraged from using the element when the referenced resource includes scripting. The
L3649: [html] object element is a better alternative, as it includes intrinsic fallback
L3650: capabilities.
L3651: 6.2 SVG content documents
L3652: Caution
L3653: Reading systems may not support all the features of [svg] or support them across all
L3654: platforms that reading systems run on. When utilizing such features, EPUB creators should
L3655: consider the inherent risks on interoperability and document longevity.
L3656: 6.2.1 Introduction
L3657: This section is non-normative.
L3658: The Scalable Vector Graphics (SVG) specification [svg] defines a format for representing
L3659: final-form vector graphics and text.
L3660: Although EPUB creators typically use XHTML content documents as the
L3661: top-level document type, the use of SVG content documents
L3662: is also permitted. EPUB creators will typically only need SVGs for certain special cases, such
L3663: as when final-form page images are the only suitable representation of the content (e.g., for
L3664: cover art or in the context of manga or comic books).
L3665: This section defines a profile for [svg] documents. An instance of an XML document that
L3666: conforms to this profile is a core media type resource and is referred to in this
L3667: specification as an SVG content document.
L3668: Note
L3669: This section defines conformance requirements for SVG content documents. Refer to 6.1.4.2 Embedded SVG for the conformance requirements for SVG embedded in XHTML
L3670: content documents.
L3671: 6.2.2 SVG requirements
L3672: An SVG content document MUST be a conforming SVG
L3673: stand-alone file [svg] and conform to all content conformance constraints expressed in
L3674: 6.2.3 Restrictions on SVG.
L3675: Note
L3676: The recommendation that EPUB publications follow the accessibility requirements in
L3677: [epub-a11y-11] applies to SVG content documents. See Accessibility.
L3678: 6.2.3 Restrictions on SVG
L3679: This specification restricts the content model of SVG content documents and SVG embedded by inclusion in XHTML content documents as
L3680: follows:
L3681: The [svg] foreignObject element:
L3682: MUST contain either [html] flow content or exactly one [html] body element.
L3683: Note
L3684: In the case of SVGs embedded by
L3685: inclusion, a body element is not permitted per the restrictions on SVG defined in [html].
L3686: MUST contain a valid document fragment
L3687: that conforms to the XHTML content document model defined in 6.1.2 XHTML requirements.
L3688: If the [svg] title element contains
L3689: marked-up text, the markup MUST contain only elements declared in the HTML namespace [infra].
L3690: Note
L3691: Although the [svg] title element allows markup elements, support for
L3692: this feature is limited. EPUB creators are advised to use text-only titles for
L3693: maximum interoperability.
L3694: When specified, the epub:type attribute MUST
L3695: only be included on renderable elements [svg].
L3696: Note
L3697: The SVG content model allows authors to include
L3698: namespaced attributes, so this specification does not need to allow the
L3699: epub:type attribute or vocabulary association
L3700: mechanisms.
L3701: One key difference between SVGs embedded by reference and by inclusion, however, is
L3702: that SVGs embedded by inclusion cannot have an epub:prefix attribute on their root svg element
L3703: [svg]. For more information, refer to D.1.4 The prefix attribute.
L3704: 6.3 Common resource requirements
L3705: This section defines requirements for technologies usable in both XHTML
L3706: and SVG content documents.
L3707: 6.3.1 Cascading Style Sheets (CSS)
L3708: 6.3.1.1 Introduction
L3709: This section is non-normative.
L3710: CSS is an integral part of the Open Web Platform. Readers, publishers, and document authors
L3711: expect CSS to "just work," as they expect HTML to just work.
L3712: In the past, EPUB defined a profile of CSS that mandated support for certain properties and
L3713: provided prefixed versions of numerous other properties. Although the CSS Working Group no
L3714: longer recommends the use of prefixed properties, this specification maintains some prefixed
L3715: properties to avoid breaking existing content. But with the minor exceptions defined in this
L3716: section, EPUB defers to the W3C to define CSS.
L3717: Note
L3718: Keep in mind that some reading systems will not support all desired features of CSS.
L3719: The following are known to be particularly problematic:
L3720: Reading system-induced pagination can interact poorly with style sheets as
L3721: reading systems sometimes paginate using columns. This may result in incorrect
L3722: values for viewport sizes. Fixed and absolute positioning are particularly
L3723: problematic.
L3724: Some types of screens will render animations and transitions poorly (e.g., those
L3725: with high latency).
L3726: 6.3.1.2 CSS requirements
L3727: A CSS style sheet:
L3728: MAY include any CSS properties, with the following
L3729: exceptions:
L3730: It MUST NOT include the direction
L3731: property [css-writing-modes-3].
L3732: It MUST NOT include the unicode-bidi
L3733: property [css-writing-modes-3].
L3734: MAY include the prefixed properties defined in 6.3.1.3 Prefixed properties.
L3735: MUST be encoded in UTF-8 or UTF-16 [unicode], with
L3736: UTF-8 as the RECOMMENDED encoding.
L3737: Note
L3738: This specification restricts the use of the direction and
L3739: unicode-bidi properties because reading systems might not
L3740: implement, or might switch off, CSS processing. EPUB creators must use the following
L3741: format-specific methods when they need control over these aspects of the rendering:
L3742: the dir attribute [html] and direction attribute [svg] for inline base
L3743: directionality.
L3744: the bdo element with the dir attribute [html] and the
L3745: presentation attribute alternative for unicode-bidi
L3746: [svg] for bidirectionality.
L3747: 6.3.1.3 Prefixed properties
L3748: Earlier version of EPUB included prefixed CSS properties, as many CSS features related to
L3749: world languages were not yet mature. To ensure backwards compatibility for content authored
L3750: using these prefixes, they have been retained in this specification. Unless otherwise noted,
L3751: prefixed properties and values behave exactly as their unprefixed equivalents as described
L3752: in the appropriate CSS specification. The prefixed properties are documented in E. Prefixed CSS properties.
L3753: Caution
L3754: EPUB creators should use unprefixed properties and reading systems should support
L3755: current CSS specifications. This specification retains the widely used prefixed
L3756: properties from [epubcontentdocs-301] but removes support for the less-used ones. EPUB
L3757: creators should use CSS-native solutions for the removed properties whenever
L3758: available.
L3759: The Working Group recommends that EPUB creators currently using these prefixed properties
L3760: move to unprefixed versions as soon as support allows, as the Working Group does not
L3761: anticipate supporting them in the next major version of EPUB.
L3762: 6.3.2 Scripting
L3763: 6.3.2.1 Script inclusion
L3764: EPUB content documents MAY contain scripting using the facilities defined for this in the
L3765: respective underlying specifications ([html] and [svg]). When an EPUB content document
L3766: contains scripting, this specification refers to it as a scripted content document. This
L3767: label also applies to XHTML content documents that contain [html] form
L3768: elements.
L3769: The scripted property of the manifest item element is used to indicate that an EPUB content document is a
L3770: scripted content document.
L3771: When an [html] script element contains a data
L3772: block [html], it does not represent scripted content.
L3773: Note
L3774: [svg] does not define data blocks as of publication, but the same exclusion would apply
L3775: if a future update adds the concept.
L3776: EPUB creators should note that reading systems are required to behave as though a
L3777: unique origin [html] has been assigned to each EPUB publication. In practice, this means that it is not possible for scripts to share data
L3778: between EPUB publications.
L3779: Which context a script is used in also determines the
L3780: rights and restrictions that a reading system places on it (refer to Scripting [epub-rs-33] for more
L3781: information).
L3782: Note
L3783: Reading systems may render scripted content documents in a manner that disables other
L3784: EPUB capabilities and/or provides a different rendering and user experience (e.g., by
L3785: disabling pagination).
L3786: 6.3.2.2 Scripting contexts
L3787: EPUB 3 defines two contexts for script execution:
L3788: container constrained — when the
L3789: execution of a script occurs within an [html] iframe element; and
L3790: spine level — when the execution of a script occurs
L3791: directly within a top-level content document.
L3792: Note
L3793: Scripts may execute in other contexts, but reading system support for these contexts
L3794: is optional. For example, a scripted SVG document may be referenced from an [html]
L3795: object element.
L3796: Refer to the processing of scripts
L3797: [epub-rs-33] for more information.
L3798: Whether EPUB creators embed the code directly in a script element or
L3799: reference it via the element's src attribute makes no difference to its
L3800: executing context.
L3801: Which context EPUB creators use for their scripts affects both what actions the scripts can
L3802: perform and the likelihood of support in reading systems, as described in the following
L3803: subsections.
L3804: Note
L3805: Refer to H.2 Scripting contexts for an example of the difference
L3806: between the two contexts.
L3807: 6.3.2.2.1 Container-constrained scripts
L3808: A container-constrained script is either of the following:
L3809: An instance of the [html] script element contained in an XHTML content document that is embedded in an XHTML content document using the [html]
L3810: iframe element.
L3811: An instance of the [svg] script element contained in an SVG content document that is embedded in a XHTML content document using the [html]
L3812: iframe element.
L3813: A container-constrained script MUST NOT contain
L3814: instructions for modifying the DOM of the EPUB content document that embeds it
L3815: (i.e., the one that contains the iframe element). It also MUST NOT contain
L3816: instructions for manipulating the size of its containing rectangle.
L3817: EPUB creators should note that support
L3818: for container-constrained scripting in reading systems is only recommended in
L3819: reflowable documents [epub-rs-33]. Furthermore, reading system support in
L3820: fixed-layout documents is optional.
L3821: EPUB creators should ensure container-constrained scripts degrade gracefully in reading
L3822: systems without scripting support (see 6.3.2.5 Scripting fallbacks).
L3823: Note
L3824: EPUB creators choosing to restrict the usage of scripting to the
L3825: container-constrained model will ensure a more consistent user experience between
L3826: scripted and non-scripted content (e.g., consistent pagination behavior).
L3827: 6.3.2.2.2 Spine-level scripts
L3828: A spine-level script is an instance of the [html] script or [svg] script
L3829: element contained in a top-level content document.
L3830: EPUB creators should note that support for spine-level scripting in reading systems is only recommended in fixed-layout documents
L3831: and reflowable documents set to
L3832: scroll [epub-rs-33]. Furthermore, reading system support in all other contexts
L3833: is optional.
L3834: Top-level content documents that include spine-level
L3835: scripting SHOULD remain consumable by the user without any information loss or other
L3836: significant deterioration when scripting is disabled or not available (e.g., by
L3837: employing progressive enhancement techniques or fallbacks). Failing to account for non-scripted environments in top-level
L3838: content documents can result in EPUB publications being unreadable.
L3839: 6.3.2.3 Event model
L3840: This section is non-normative.
L3841: EPUB creators should consider the wide variety of possible reading system
L3842: implementations when adding scripting functionality to their EPUB publications (e.g.,
L3843: not all devices have physical keyboards, and in many cases a soft keyboard is activated only
L3844: for text input elements). Consequently, EPUB creators should not rely on keyboard events
L3845: alone; they should always provide alternative ways to trigger a desired action.
L3846: 6.3.2.4 Scripting accessibility
L3847: EPUB content documents that contain scripting SHOULD employ
L3848: relevant [wai-aria] accessibility techniques to ensure that the content remains consumable
L3849: by all users.
L3850: 6.3.2.5 Scripting fallbacks
L3851: EPUB content documents that contain scripting MAY
L3852: provide fallbacks for such content, either by using intrinsic fallback mechanisms (such as
L3853: those available for the [html] object and canvas elements) or, when an intrinsic
L3854: fallback is not applicable, by using a manifest-level
L3855: fallback.
L3856: EPUB creators MUST ensure that scripts only
L3857: generate core media type resources or fragments
L3858: thereof.
L3859: 7. EPUB navigation document
L3860: 7.1 Introduction
L3861: This section is non-normative.
L3862: The EPUB navigation document is a mandatory component of an EPUB publication. It allows EPUB creators to include a human- and machine-readable global
L3863: navigation layer, thereby ensuring increased usability and accessibility for the user.
L3864: The EPUB navigation document is a special type of XHTML content document that defines the table of contents for reading systems. It may also include other
L3865: specialized navigation elements, such as a page list and a list of
L3866: key landmarks. These navigation elements have additional restrictions on their content to facilitate their
L3867: processing.
L3868: The EPUB navigation document is not exclusively for machine processing, however. There are no
L3869: restrictions on the structure or content of the EPUB navigation document outside of the specialized
L3870: navigation elements (i.e., EPUB creators can mark the rest of the document up like any other XHTML
L3871: content document). As a result, it can also be part of the linear reading order, avoiding the need
L3872: for duplicate tables of contents. EPUB creators can hide navigation elements that are only for
L3873: machine processing (e.g., the page list) with the hidden attribute.
L3874: Note that reading systems may strip scripting, styling, and HTML formatting as they generate
L3875: navigational interfaces from information found in the EPUB navigation document, and this may make
L3876: the result difficult to read. If EPUB creators require such formatting and functionality, then they
L3877: should also include the EPUB navigation document in the spine. The use of
L3878: progressive enhancement techniques for scripting and styling of the navigation document will help
L3879: ensure the content will retain its integrity when rendered in a non-browser context.
L3880: 7.2 Navigation document requirements
L3881: A valid EPUB navigation document:
L3882: MUST conform to the content conformance constraints for XHTML content documents defined in 6.1.2 XHTML requirements;
L3883: MUST conform to the nav element constraints defined 7.3 The nav element: restrictions;
L3884: MUST include exactly one toc nav element as defined in
L3885: 7.4.2 The toc nav element.
L3886: 7.3 The nav element: restrictions
L3887: When a nav element carries the epub:type attribute in an EPUB navigation document, this specification restricts the content model of the element and its descendants as
L3888: follows:
L3889: Content Model:
L3890: nav
L3891: In this order:
L3892: h1-h6
L3893: [0 or 1]
L3894: ol [exactly 1]
L3895: ol
L3896: In this order:
L3897: li [1 or more]
L3898: li
L3899: In this order:
L3900: (span or a) [exactly 1]
L3901: ol [conditionally required]
L3902: span and a
L3903: In any order:
L3904: HTML Phrasing content [1 or more]
L3905: Note that there are no restrictions on the attributes allowed on these elements.
L3906: Refer the definition below for additional requirements.
L3907: The following elaboration of the content model of the nav element explains the purpose
L3908: and restrictions of the various elements:
L3909: The ol child of the nav element represents the
L3910: primary level of content navigation.
L3911: Each list item of the ordered list represents a heading, structure, or
L3912: other item of interest. A child a element describes the target that the link
L3913: points to, while a span element serves as a heading for breaking down lists
L3914: into distinct groups (for example, an EPUB creator could segment a large list of
L3915: illustrations into several lists, one for each chapter).
L3916: The child a or span element MUST provide a
L3917: non-zero-length text label after concatenation of all child content and application of
L3918: whitespace normalization rules. When determining compliance with this requirement, the
L3919: concatenated label MUST include text content contained in title or
L3920: alt attributes for non-textual descendant elements.
L3921: If an a or span element contains instances
L3922: of HTML embedded content [html] that do not provide intrinsic text
L3923: alternatives, the element MUST also contain a title attribute with an alternate
L3924: text rendering of the link label.
L3925: The URL [url] reference provided in the href attribute
L3926: of the a element:
L3927: MUST, in the case of the toc nav, landmarks
L3928: nav and page-list
L3929: nav, resolve to a top-level content document or fragment
L3930: therein.
L3931: MAY, for all other nav types, also
L3932: reference content outside the EPUB container (e.g., web-hosted resources).
L3933: An ol (ordered list) element representing a subsidiary
L3934: content level (e.g., all the subsection headings of a section) MAY follow an a
L3935: element.
L3936: An ol (ordered list) element MUST follow a
L3937: span element (span elements cannot occur in "leaf"
L3938: li elements).
L3939: Regardless of whether an a or span element
L3940: precedes it, every sublist MUST adhere to the content requirements defined in this section
L3941: for constructing the primary navigation list.
L3942: Example 50: Basic patterns of a navigation element
L3943: <nav epub:type="…">
L3944: <h1>…</h1>
L3945: <ol>
L3946: <li>
L3947: <a href="chap1.xhtml">
L3948: A basic leaf node
L3949: </a>
L3950: </li>
L3951: <li>
L3952: <a href="chap2.xhtml">
L3953: A linked heading
L3954: </a>
L3955: <ol>
L3956: …
L3957: </ol>
L3958: </li>
L3959: <li>
L3960: <span>An unlinked heading</span>
L3961: <ol>
L3962: …
L3963: </ol>
L3964: </li>
L3965: <li>
L3966: <a href="appendix.xhtml">
L3967: <img src="app1.jpg" alt="An image-based heading"/>
L3968: </a>
L3969: </li>
L3970: </ol>
L3971: </nav>
L3972: Caution
L3973: Although the headings and links in nav elements allow any [html] phrasing content, app-based reading systems often only support simple text labels. Because these apps
L3974: create their own navigation widgets that are not based on HTML rendering, they often cannot
L3975: retain embedded images and multimedia, MathML, inline styling and other element- and
L3976: attribute-based rendering instructions. EPUB creators should avoid using these types of elements
L3977: where their absence may lead to usability issues.
L3978: As a conforming XHTML content document, EPUB creators MAY
L3979: include the EPUB navigation document in the spine.
L3980: In the context of this specification, the default display style of list
L3981: items within nav elements is equivalent to the list-style:
L3982: none property [csssnapshot]. EPUB creators MAY specify alternative list
L3983: styling using CSS for rendering of the document in the spine.
L3984: 7.4 The nav element: types
L3985: 7.4.1 Introduction
L3986: This section is non-normative.
L3987: The nav elements defined in an EPUB navigation document are distinguished
L3988: semantically by the value of their epub:type attribute.
L3989: This specification defines three types of navigation aid:
L3990: toc
L3991: Identifies the nav element that contains the table of contents. The
L3992: toc nav is the only navigation aid that EPUB creators must include
L3993: in the EPUB navigation document.
L3994: page-list
L3995: Identifies the nav element that contains a list of pages for a print or
L3996: other statically paginated source.
L3997: landmarks
L3998: Identifies the nav element that contains a list of points of interest.
L3999: An EPUB navigation document may contain at most one navigation aid for each of these types.
L4000: The EPUB navigation document may include additional navigation types. See 7.4.5 Other nav elements for more information.
L4001: 7.4.2 The toc nav element
L4002: The primary navigational hierarchy of an EPUB publication is defined in a nav element
L4003: [html] whose epub:type attribute set to the value "toc" [epub-ssv-11] (i.e., the toc nav element). This element
L4004: conceptually corresponds to a table of contents in a printed work — it provides navigation
L4005: to the major structural sections of the publication.
L4006: EPUB creators SHOULD order the references in the toc nav element such that they
L4007: reflect both:
L4008: the order of the referenced EPUB content documents in
L4009: the spine; and
L4010: the order of the targeted elements within their respective EPUB content documents.
L4011: 7.4.3 The page-list nav element
L4012: The page list provides navigation to static page boundaries in the content. These boundaries may
L4013: correspond to a statically paginated source such as print or may be defined exclusively for the
L4014: EPUB publication.
L4015: The page list is defined in a nav element [html] whose epub:type attribute is set to
L4016: the value "page-list" [epub-ssv-11] (i.e., the
L4017: page-list nav element).
L4018: The page-list nav element is OPTIONAL in EPUB navigation documents and MUST NOT
L4019: occur more than once.
L4020: The page-list nav element SHOULD contain only a single ol descendant
L4021: (i.e., no nested sublists).
L4022: EPUB creators MAY identify the destinations of the page-list references in their
L4023: respective EPUB content documents using the pagebreak term [epub-ssv-11].
L4024: 7.4.4 The landmarks nav element
L4025: Landmarks identify fundamental structural components of the content to enable reading systems
L4026: to provide the user efficient access to them (e.g., through a dedicated button in the user
L4027: interface).
L4028: Landmarks are defined in a nav element [html] whose epub:type attribute is set to the
L4029: value "landmarks" [epub-ssv-11] (i.e., the
L4030: landmarks nav element).
L4031: The landmarks nav element is OPTIONAL in EPUB navigation documents and MUST NOT
L4032: occur more than once.
L4033: The landmarks nav element SHOULD contain only a single ol descendant
L4034: (i.e., no nested sublists).
L4035: The epub:type attribute is REQUIRED on a element descendants of the
L4036: landmarks nav element. The structural semantics of each link target within the
L4037: landmarks nav element is determined by the value of this attribute.
L4038: Example 51: A basic landmarks nav
L4039: In this example, the epub:type attribute value are drawn from structural
L4040: semantics drawn from [epub-ssv-11].
L4041: <nav epub:type="landmarks">
L4042: <h2>Guide</h2>
L4043: <ol>
L4044: <li>
L4045: <a epub:type="toc"
L4046: href="#toc">
L4047: Table of Contents
L4048: </a>
L4049: </li>
L4050: <li>
L4051: <a epub:type="loi"
L4052: href="content.html#loi">
L4053: List of Illustrations
L4054: </a>
L4055: </li>
L4056: <li>
L4057: <a epub:type="bodymatter"
L4058: href="content.html#bodymatter">
L4059: Start of Content
L4060: </a>
L4061: </li>
L4062: </ol>
L4063: </nav>
L4064: The landmarks nav MUST NOT include multiple entries with the same
L4065: epub:type value that reference the same resource, or fragment thereof.
L4066: EPUB creators should limit the number of items they define in the landmarks nav
L4067: to only items that a reading system is likely to use in its user interface. The element is not
L4068: meant to repeat the table of contents.
L4069: The following landmarks are recommended to include when available:
L4070: bodymatter [epub-ssv-11] — Reading
L4071: systems often use this landmark to automatically jump users past the front matter when they
L4072: begin reading.
L4073: toc [epub-ssv-11] — If the table
L4074: of contents is available in the spine, reading systems may use this landmark to take users
L4075: to the document containing it.
L4076: Other possibilities for inclusion in the landmarks nav are key reference sections
L4077: such as indexes and glossaries.
L4078: Although the landmarks nav is intended for reading system use, EPUB creators should
L4079: still ensure that the labels for the landmarks nav are human readable. Reading
L4080: systems may expose the links directly to users.
L4081: 7.4.5 Other nav elements
L4082: EPUB navigation documents MAY contain one or more nav elements in addition to
L4083: the toc, page-list, and landmarks nav elements defined in
L4084: the preceding sections. If these nav elements are intended for reading system
L4085: processing, they MUST have an epub:type attribute and are subject to the content model
L4086: restrictions defined in 7.3 The nav element: restrictions.
L4087: This specification imposes no restrictions on the semantics of any additional nav
L4088: elements: they MAY represent navigational semantics for any information domain, and they MAY
L4089: contain link targets with homogeneous or heterogeneous semantics.
L4090: Example 52: Adding a custom navigation element
L4091: In this example, the lot semantic indicates that the EPUB creator is adding a
L4092: "list of tables" navigation element.
L4093: <nav
L4094: epub:type="lot"
L4095: aria-labelledby="lot">
L4096: <h2 id="lot">List of tables</h2>
L4097: <ol>
L4098: <li>
L4099: <span>Tables in Chapter 1</span>
L4100: <ol>
L4101: <li>
L4102: <a href="chap1.xhtml#table-1.1">
L4103: Table 1.1
L4104: </a>
L4105: </li>
L4106: <li>
L4107: <a href="chap1.xhtml#table-1.2">
L4108: Table 1.2
L4109: </a>
L4110: </li>
L4111: </ol>
L4112: </li>
L4113: …
L4114: </ol>
L4115: </nav>
L4116: 7.5 Using in the spine
L4117: This section is non-normative.
L4118: Although it is possible to reuse the EPUB navigation document in the spine, it
L4119: is often the case that not all of the navigation structures, or branches within them, are needed.
L4120: EPUB creators will often want to hide the page list and landmarks navigation elements or trim the branches of the table of
L4121: contents for books that have many levels of subsections.
L4122: While the display
L4123: property [csssnapshot] controls the visual rendering of EPUB navigation documents in
L4124: reading systems with viewports, reading systems without viewports may not support CSS. To
L4125: better ensure the proper rendering in these reading systems, EPUB creators should use the [html]
L4126: hidden attribute to indicate which (if any) portions of the navigation data are
L4127: excluded from rendering in the content flow.
L4128: The hidden attribute has no effect on how reading systems render the navigation data
L4129: outside of the content flow (such as in dedicated navigation user interfaces provided by reading
L4130: systems).
L4131: Note
L4132: The hidden attribute can be used together with the display property to
L4133: maximize interoperability across all reading systems.
L4134: Example 53: Hiding a nav element in spine
L4135: In this example, the presence of the hidden attribute on the nav
L4136: element indicates the page list will be excluded from rendering in the content flow when the
L4137: document is rendered in the spine.
L4138: <nav
L4139: epub:type="page-list"
L4140: hidden="hidden">
L4141: <h2>Pagebreaks of the print version, third edition</h2>
L4142: <ol>
L4143: <li>
L4144: <a href="frontmatter.xhtml#pi">
L4145: I
L4146: </a>
L4147: </li>
L4148: …
L4149: </ol>
L4150: </nav>
L4151: Example 54: Hiding branches of a nav element
L4152: In this example, the branch (ol element) not wanted for rendering in the spine has
L4153: the hidden attribute on it. When rendered, this limits the table of content to the
L4154: two top-most hierarchical levels.

L4173: </a>

L4184: 8. Layout rendering control
L4185: 8.1 Introduction
L4186: This section is non-normative.
L4187: Not all rendering information can be expressed through the underlying technologies that EPUB is built
L4188: upon. For example, although HTML with CSS provides powerful layout capabilities, those capabilities
L4189: are limited to the scope of the document being rendered.
L4190: This section defines properties that allow EPUB creators to express package-level rendering
L4191: intentions (i.e., functionality that can only be implemented by the EPUB reading system). If a
L4192: reading system supports the desired rendering, these properties enable the user to be presented the
L4193: content as the EPUB creator optimally designed it.
L4194: 8.2 Fixed layouts
L4195: 8.2.1 Introduction
L4196: This section is non-normative.
L4197: EPUB publications, unlike print books or PDF files, are designed to change. The content
L4198: flows, or reflows, to fit the screen and to fit the needs of the user. As noted in Rendering and CSS "content presentation
L4199: adapts to the user, rather than the user having to adapt to a particular presentation of
L4200: content." [epub-overview-33]
L4201: But this principle does not work for all types of documents. Sometimes content and design are so
L4202: intertwined it is not possible to separate them. Any change in appearance risks changing the
L4203: meaning or losing all meaning. Fixed-layout documents give EPUB creators greater control
L4204: over presentation when a reflowable EPUB is not suitable for the content.
L4205: EPUB creators define fixed layouts using a set of package document
L4206: properties to control the rendering in reading systems. In addition, they set the dimensions of each fixed-layout document in its respective
L4207: EPUB content document.
L4208: Note
L4209: EPUB 3 affords multiple mechanisms for representing fixed-layout content. When fixed-layout
L4210: content is necessary, the EPUB creator's choice of mechanism will depend on many factors
L4211: including desired degree of precision, file size, accessibility, etc. This section does not
L4212: attempt to dictate the EPUB creator's choice of mechanism.
L4213: 8.2.2 Fixed-layout package settings
L4214: 8.2.2.1 Layout
L4215: The rendition:layout property specifies whether the content is reflowable or
L4216: pre-paginated.
L4217: When the rendition:layout
L4218: property is specified on a meta element, it indicates that the
L4219: paginated or reflowable layout style applies globally (i.e., for all spine items).
L4220: EPUB creators MUST use one of the following values with the rendition:layout
L4221: property:
L4222: reflowable
L4223: The content is not pre-paginated (i.e., reading systems apply dynamic pagination
L4224: when rendering). Default value.
L4225: pre-paginated
L4226: The content is pre-paginated (i.e., reading systems produce exactly one page per
L4227: spine itemref when rendering).
L4228: Note
L4229: Reading systems typically restrict or deny the application of user or user agent style
L4230: sheets to pre-paginated documents because dynamic style changes are likely to have
L4231: unintended consequence on the intrinsic properties of such documents. EPUB creators
L4232: should consider the negative impact on usability and accessibility that these
L4233: restrictions have when choosing to use pre-paginated instead of reflowable content.
L4234: Refer to Guideline 1.4 - Provide text
L4235: configuration [uaag20] for related information.
L4236: When the property is set
L4237: to pre-paginated for a spine item, its content dimensions MUST be set as
L4238: defined in 8.2.2.6 Content document dimensions.
L4239: EPUB creators MUST NOT declare the rendition:layout property more than once.
L4240: They also MUST NOT declare the property using the refines attribute. Refer to 8.2.2.1.1 Layout overrides for
L4241: setting the property for individual EPUB content documents.
L4242: Example 55: Fixed Layout Document with media queries
L4243: In this example, the document's layout is set to pre-paginated (i.e., it is
L4244: defined to be a fixed-layout document). Furthermore, media queries
L4245: [mediaqueries-3] are used to apply different style sheets for three different device
L4246: categories. Note that the media queries only affect the style sheet applied to the
L4247: document; the size of the content area set in the viewport meta tag is
L4248: static.
L4249: Package document:
L4250: <package …>
L4251: <metadata …>
L4252: …
L4253: <meta
L4254: property="rendition:layout">
L4255: pre-paginated
L4256: </meta>
L4257: …
L4258: </metadata>
L4259: …
L4260: </package>
L4261: XHTML
L4262: <html …>
L4263: <head>
L4264: <meta
L4265: name="viewport"
L4266: content="width=1200,
L4267: height=900"/>
L4268: <link
L4269: rel="stylesheet"
L4270: href="eink-style.css"
L4271: media="(max-monochrome: 3)"/>
L4272: <link
L4273: rel="stylesheet"
L4274: href="skinnytablet-style.css"
L4275: media="((color) and (max-height:600px) and (orientation:landscape),
L4276: (color) and (max-width:600px) and (orientation:portrait))"/>
L4277: <link
L4278: rel="stylesheet"
L4279: href="fattablet-style.css"
L4280: media="((color) and (min-height:601px) and (orientation:landscape),
L4281: (color) and (min-width:601px) and (orientation:portrait))"/>
L4282: </head>
L4283: …
L4284: </html>
L4285: 8.2.2.1.1 Layout overrides
L4286: EPUB creators MAY specify the following properties locally
L4287: on spine itemref elements to override the global value for the given spine item:
L4288: rendition:layout-pre-paginated
L4289: Specifies that the given spine item is pre-paginated.
L4290: rendition:layout-reflowable
L4291: Specifies that the given spine item is reflowable.
L4292: EPUB creators MUST NOT use more than one of these overrides on any given spine item.
L4293: 8.2.2.2 Orientation
L4294: The rendition:orientation property specifies which orientation the EPUB creator intends the content to be rendered in.
L4295: When the rendition:orientation property is specified on a meta element,
L4296: it indicates that the intended orientation applies globally (i.e., for all spine items).
L4297: EPUB creators MUST use one of the following values with the
L4298: rendition:orientation property:
L4299: landscape
L4300: Reading systems should render the content in landscape orientation.
L4301: portrait
L4302: Reading systems should render the content in portrait orientation.
L4303: auto
L4304: The content is not orientation constrained. Default value.
L4305: EPUB creators MUST NOT declare the
L4306: rendition:orientation property more than once.
L4307: They also MUST NOT declare the property using the refines attribute. Refer to 8.2.2.2.1 Orientation overrides
L4308: for setting the property for individual EPUB content documents.
L4309: Example 56: Specifying global landscape orientation
L4310: In this example, items in the spine are to be rendered in landscape mode.
L4311: <package …>
L4312: <metadata …>
L4313: …
L4314: <meta
L4315: property="rendition:layout">
L4316: pre-paginated
L4317: </meta>
L4318: <meta
L4319: property="rendition:orientation">
L4320: landscape
L4321: </meta>
L4322: </metadata>
L4323: …
L4324: </package>
L4325: 8.2.2.2.1 Orientation overrides
L4326: EPUB creators MAY specify the following properties
L4327: locally on spine itemref elements to override the global value for the given spine item:
L4328: rendition:orientation-auto
L4329: Specifies that the reading system determines the orientation to render the spine
L4330: item in.
L4331: rendition:orientation-landscape
L4332: Specifies that reading systems should render the given spine item in landscape
L4333: orientation.
L4334: rendition:orientation-portrait
L4335: Specifies that reading systems should render the given spine item in portrait
L4336: orientation.
L4337: EPUB creators MUST NOT use more than one of these overrides on any given spine item.
L4338: 8.2.2.3 Synthetic spreads
L4339: The rendition:spread property specifies the intended reading system
L4340: synthetic spread behavior.
L4341: When the rendition:spread property is specified on a
L4342: meta element, it indicates that the intended synthetic spread behavior
L4343: applies globally (i.e., for all spine items).
L4344: EPUB creators MUST use one of the following values with the rendition:spread
L4345: property:
L4346: none
L4347: Do not incorporate spine items in a synthetic spread. Reading systems should display
L4348: the items in a single viewport positioned at the center of the screen.
L4349: landscape
L4350: Render a synthetic spread for spine items only when the device is in landscape
L4351: orientation.
L4352: portrait (deprecated)
L4353: The use of spreads only in portrait orientation is deprecated.
L4354: EPUB creators should use the value "both" instead, as spreads that are
L4355: readable in portrait orientation are also readable in landscape.
L4356: both
L4357: Render a synthetic spread regardless of device orientation.
L4358: auto
L4359: The EPUB creator is not defining an explicit synthetic spread behavior. Default
L4360: value.
L4361: EPUB creators MUST NOT declare the rendition:spread property more than once.
L4362: They also MUST NOT declare the property using the refines attribute. Refer to 8.2.2.3.1 Synthetic spread overrides for
L4363: setting the property for individual EPUB content documents.
L4364: Note
L4365: When synthetic spreads are used in the context of XHTML and
L4366: SVG content documents, the dimensions given via the viewport meta element and viewBox attribute represents the size of one page in the
L4367: spread, respectively.
L4368: Note
L4369: Refer to the spine element for information about declaration of global flow
L4370: directionality using the page-progression-direction attribute and that of
L4371: local page-progression-direction within content documents.
L4372: Example 57: A fixed-layout EPUB publication without synthetic spread
L4373: <package …>
L4374: <metadata …>
L4375: …
L4376: <meta
L4377: property="rendition:layout">
L4378: pre-paginated
L4379: </meta>
L4380: <meta
L4381: property="rendition:spread">
L4382: none
L4383: </meta>
L4384: </metadata>
L4385: …
L4386: </package>
L4387: Figure 1 Rendering of three fixed-layout documents without synthetic spread.

L4389: 2.5.)

L4391: Two rows of schematic views of tablets (three in each row). The tablets in the top
L4392: row are in portrait mode, and in landscape mode in the bottom one. The schematic
L4393: views of the tablets within a row are linked with left-to-right arrows.
L4394: In the tablets of each row the consecutive panels of a comics are displayed; the
L4395: panels are centered in their respective tablets.
L4396: Example 58: Specifying the usage of synthetic spreads in landscape orientation only

L4402: pre-paginated
L4403: </meta>
L4404: <meta
L4405: property="rendition:spread">
L4406: landscape
L4407: </meta>

L4453: In this example, the EPUB creator overrides the global reflowable setting in the spine
L4454: for the introductory page. The intention is for reading systems to render it as a

L4479: 2.5.)
L4480: Image description
L4481: A row of schematic views of three tablets in portrait mode, and linked with
L4482: left-to-right arrows.
L4483: The first tablet views includes a single, column-like strip (i.e., a rectangle
L4484: without a bottom edge following beyond the bottom of the tablet) with a text flowing
L4485: down the strip, and starting with the word "Introduction". This is followed by two
L4486: schematic tablets with three panels of comics displayed. The first tablet in the row
L4487: contains the first and second panels of the comics side by side; the second tablet
L4488: contains the third panel of the comics on the left.
L4489: 8.2.2.3.1 Synthetic spread overrides
L4490: EPUB creators MAY specify the following properties locally
L4491: on spine itemref elements to override the global value for the given spine item:
L4492: rendition:spread-auto
L4493: Specifies the reading system determines when to render a synthetic spread for
L4494: the spine item.
L4495: rendition:spread-both
L4496: Specifies the reading system should render a synthetic spread for the spine item in
L4497: both portrait and landscape orientations.
L4498: rendition:spread-landscape
L4499: Specifies the reading system should render a synthetic spread for the spine item
L4500: only when in landscape orientation.
L4501: rendition:spread-none
L4502: Specifies the reading system should not render a synthetic spread for the spine
L4503: item.
L4504: rendition:spread-portrait
L4505: The rendition:spread-portrait property is deprecated.
L4506: Refer to the spread-portrait property definition
L4507: in [epubpublications-301] for more information.
L4508: EPUB creators MUST NOT use more than one of these overrides on any given spine item.
L4509: 8.2.2.4 Spread placement
L4510: When a reading system renders a synthetic spread, the default behavior is to populate
L4511: the spread by rendering the next EPUB content document in the next available unpopulated
L4512: viewport, where the next available viewport is determined by the given page progression direction or by
L4513: local declarations within EPUB content documents. An EPUB creator MAY override this
L4514: automatic population behavior and force reading systems to place a document in a particular
L4515: viewport by specifying one of the following properties on its spine itemref
L4516: element:
L4517: rendition:page-spread-center
L4518: The rendition:page-spread-center property is an alias of the spread-none property for centering a spine
L4519: item.
L4520: rendition:page-spread-left
L4521: The rendition:page-spread-left property is an alias of the page-spread-left property for placing a
L4522: spine item in the left-hand slot of a two-page spread.
L4523: rendition:page-spread-right
L4524: The rendition:page-spread-right property is an alias of the page-spread-right property for placing a
L4525: spine item in the right-hand slot of a two-page spread.
L4526: The rendition:page-spread-center, rendition:page-spread-left, and
L4527: rendition:page-spread-right properties apply to both pre-paginated and
L4528: reflowable content. They only apply when the reading system is creating synthetic
L4529: spreads.
L4530: Although EPUB creators often indicate to use a spread in certain device orientations, the
L4531: content itself does not represent true spreads (i.e., two consecutive pages that reading
L4532: systems must render side-by-side for readability, such as a two-page map). To indicate that
L4533: two consecutive pages represent a true spread, EPUB creators SHOULD use the
L4534: rendition:page-spread-left and rendition:page-spread-right
L4535: properties on the spine items for the two adjacent EPUB content documents,
L4536: and omit the properties on spine items where one-up or two-up presentation is equally
L4537: acceptable.
L4538: EPUB creators MUST NOT declare more than one rendition:page-spread-* property,
L4539: and/or their unprefixed equivalents, on any given spine item (e.g., it is valid to specify
L4540: both "rendition:page-spread-left page-spread-left" in case reading systems only
L4541: support one of properties).
L4542: Note
L4543: The rendition:page-spread-left and rendition:page-spread-right
L4544: properties were created to allow the use of a single vocabulary for all fixed-layout
L4545: properties. EPUB creators can use either property set, but older reading systems might
L4546: only recognize the unprefixed versions.
L4547: The rendition:page-spread-center was created to make it easier for EPUB
L4548: creators to understand the process of switching between two-page spreads and single
L4549: centered pages. EPUB creators can use either rendition:page-spread-center
L4550: or spread-none to disable spread behavior in reading systems.
L4551: Example 61: Starting the first document on the right
L4552: <package …>
L4553: <metadata …>
L4554: …
L4555: <meta
L4556: property="rendition:layout">
L4557: reflowable
L4558: </meta>
L4559: <meta
L4560: property="rendition:spread">
L4561: landscape
L4562: </meta>
L4563: …
L4564: </metadata>
L4565: <spine page-progression-direction="ltr">
L4566: …
L4567: <itemref
L4568: idref="first-panel"
L4569: properties="rendition:page-spread-right"/>
L4570: …
L4571: </spine>
L4572: </package>
L4573: Figure 5 Rendering of three fixed-layout documents, with synthetic spread in
L4574: landscape orientation starting on the right.
L4575: (Comics
L4576: courtesy of xkcd, licensed under cc by-nc
L4577: 2.5.)
L4578: Image description
L4579: A row of schematic views of two tablets in landscape mode, and linked with a
L4580: left-to-right arrow.
L4581: Three panels of a comics are displayed in the tablets. The first tablet in the row
L4582: contains the first panels of the comics on the right hand of the tablet, with the
L4583: left side empty; the second tablet contains the second and third panels of the
L4584: comics side-by-side.
L4585: Example 62: Placing individual spine items in a spread
L4586: In this example, the EPUB creator intends the reading system to create a two-page
L4587: fixed-layout center plate using synthetic spreads in any device orientation. Note that
L4588: the EPUB creator has left spread behavior for the other (reflowable) parts undefined,
L4589: since the global value of rendition:spread initializes to auto
L4590: by default.
L4591: <package …>
L4592: …

L4594: …
L4595: <itemref
L4596: idref="center-plate-left"
L4597: properties="rendition:spread-both rendition:page-spread-left"/>
L4598: <itemref
L4599: idref="center-plate-right"
L4600: properties="rendition:spread-both rendition:page-spread-right"/>
L4601: …
L4602: </spine>
L4603: </package>
L4604: Example 63: Creating a centered layout
L4605: <package …>
L4606: <metadata …>
L4607: …
L4608: <meta
L4609: property="rendition:layout">
L4610: pre-paginated
L4611: </meta>
L4612: <meta
L4613: property="rendition:spread">
L4614: auto
L4615: </meta>
L4616: </metadata>
L4617: <spine>
L4618: …
L4619: <itemref
L4620: idref="center-plate"
L4621: properties="rendition:page-spread-center"/>
L4622: …
L4623: </spine>
L4624: …
L4625: </package>
L4626: 8.2.2.5 Viewport dimensions (deprecated)
L4627: The rendition:viewport property allows EPUB creators to express the CSS
L4628: initial containing block (ICB) [css2] for XHTML and SVG content documents whose rendition:layout property has been set to
L4629: pre-paginated.
L4630: Use of the property is deprecated.
L4631: Refer to the rendition:viewport property definition
L4632: in [epubpublications-301] for more information.
L4633: 8.2.2.6 Content document dimensions
L4634: This section defines rules for the expression and interpretation of dimensional properties of
L4635: fixed-layout documents.
L4636: Fixed-layout documents specify their initial
L4637: containing block [css2] in the manner applicable to their format:
L4638: Expressing in XHTML
L4639: For XHTML fixed-layout documents, the initial
L4640: containing block [css2] is obtained from the REQUIRED height
L4641: and width definitions in a viewport
L4642: meta tag, where:
L4643: the height
L4644: property MUST have as its value a positive number [css2] or the keyword
L4645: device-height; and
L4646: the width property MUST have as its value a positive number [css2] or the keyword
L4647: device-width.
L4648: The device-width and device-height values refer to the 100%
L4649: of the width and height, respectively, of the reading system's viewport.
L4650: The height and width definitions MUST be specified in the
L4651: first viewport meta tag in document order in the [html] head element. Reading systems
L4652: will ignore subsequent viewport meta tags.
L4653: EPUB creators MUST NOT specify more than one height or
L4654: width definition within a viewport meta tag.
L4655: Example 64: Specifying the initial containing block in a viewport meta tag
L4656: <html …>
L4657: <head>
L4658: …
L4659: <meta
L4660: name="viewport"
L4661: content="width=1200, height=600"/>
L4662: …
L4663: </head>
L4664: …
L4665: </html>
L4666: Expressing in SVG
L4667: For SVG fixed-layout documents, the initial containing block [css2]
L4668: dimensions MUST be expressed using the viewBox attribute [svg].
L4669: Example 65: Specifying the initial containing block in the viewBox attribute
L4670: In this example, the viewBox attribute sets the ICB to an aspect
L4671: ratio of 844 pixels wide by 1200 pixels high.
L4672: <svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"
L4673: version="1.1"
L4674: viewBox="0 0 844 1200">
L4675: …
L4676: </svg>
L4677: Note
L4678: The initial containing block definition affects only the document where it is
L4679: defined. The dimensions of the containing blocks in the other content documents within the
L4680: same publication may be different.
L4681: 8.3 Reflowable layouts
L4682: Although control over the rendering of EPUB content documents to create fixed layouts is an obvious need not handled by other
L4683: technologies, there are also considerations for reflowable content that are unique to EPUB publications (e.g., how to handle the flow of content in the viewport). This section defines
L4684: properties that allow EPUB creators to control presentation aspects of reflowable content.
L4685: 8.3.1 The rendition:flow property
L4686: The rendition:flow property specifies the EPUB creator preference for how
L4687: reading systems should handle content overflow.
L4688: When the rendition:flow property is
L4689: specified on a meta element, it indicates the EPUB creator's global preference for
L4690: overflow content handling (i.e., for all spine items). EPUB creators MAY
L4691: indicate a preference for dynamic pagination or scrolling. For scrolled content, it is also
L4692: possible to specify whether consecutive EPUB content documents are to be rendered as a
L4693: continuous scrolling view or whether each is to be rendered separately (i.e., with a dynamic
L4694: page break between each).
L4695: EPUB creators MUST use one of the following values with the rendition:flow
L4696: property:
L4697: paginated
L4698: Dynamically paginate all overflow content.
L4699: scrolled-continuous
L4700: Render all EPUB content documents such that overflow content is scrollable, and the
L4701: EPUB publication is presented as one continuous scroll from spine item to spine item
L4702: (except where locally overridden).
L4703: Note that EPUB creators SHOULD NOT create publications in which different resources have
L4704: different block flow directions, as continuous scrolled rendition in EPUB reading
L4705: systems would be problematic.
L4706: scrolled-doc
L4707: Render all EPUB content documents such that overflow content is scrollable, and each
L4708: spine item is presented as a separate scrollable document.
L4709: auto
L4710: Render overflow content using the reading system default method or a user preference,
L4711: whichever is applicable. Default value.
L4712: Note that when two reflowable EPUB content documents occur
L4713: sequentially in the spine, the default rendering for their [html] body elements is consistent with the page-break-before property [csssnapshot] having been set to
L4714: always. In addition to using the rendition:flow property, EPUB
L4715: creators MAY override this behavior through an appropriate style sheet declaration, if the
L4716: reading system supports such overrides.
L4717: EPUB creators MUST NOT declare the rendition:flow property more than once.
L4718: They also MUST NOT declare the property using the refines
L4719: attribute. Refer to 8.3.1.1 Spine overrides for setting the property for
L4720: individual EPUB content documents.
L4721: Figure 6 Rendering of an EPUB publication with a single spine item, and with the
L4722: rendition:flow set to paginated.
L4723: Image description
L4724: Three column-like rectangles linked left-to-middle and middle-to-right with respective
L4725: arrows, with a text flowing from one rectangle to the next one. The text is sectioned with
L4726: headers figuring 'Chapter 1', '2', and '3'. The leftmost rectangle is enclosed in a
L4727: schematic view of a tablet.
L4728: Figure 7 Rendering of an EPUB publication with multiple spine items, and with the
L4729: rendition:flow set to paginated.
L4730: Image description
L4731: Three column-like rectangles linked left-to-middle and middle-to-right with respective
L4732: arrows, with a text flowing from one rectangle to the next one. The text is sectioned with
L4733: headers figuring 'Chapter 1', '2'. The section with 'Chapter 2' starts at the top of the
L4734: rightmost rectangle, leaving an empty space at the bottom of the middle rectangle. The
L4735: leftmost rectangle is enclosed in a schematic view of a tablet.
L4736: Figure 8 Rendering of an EPUB publication with a single spine item, and with the
L4737: rendition:flow set to scrolled-continuous.
L4738: Image description
L4739: A single, column-like strip (i.e., a rectangle without a bottom edge) with a text flowing
L4740: down the strip. The text is sectioned with headers figuring 'Chapter 1', '2'. The top part
L4741: of the strip is enclosed in a schematic view of a tablet.
L4742: Figure 9 Rendering of an EPUB publication with multiple spine items, and with the
L4743: rendition:flow set to scrolled-doc.
L4744: Image description
L4745: Three column-like strips (i.e., a rectangles without bottom edges) linked left-to-middle and
L4746: middle-to-right with respective arrows, each containing a text flowing down the strip. The
L4747: text is sectioned with headers figuring 'Chapter 1', '2' and '3'. Each strip starts with a
L4748: chapter header and flows down the strip. The top part of the leftmost strip is enclosed in a
L4749: schematic view of a tablet.
L4750: 8.3.1.1 Spine overrides
L4751: EPUB creators MAY specify the following properties
L4752: locally on spine itemref elements to override the global value for the given spine item:
L4753: rendition:flow-auto
L4754: Indicates no preference for overflow content handling by the EPUB creator.
L4755: rendition:flow-paginated
L4756: Indicates the EPUB creator preference is to dynamically paginate content overflow.
L4757: rendition:flow-scrolled-continuous
L4758: Indicates the EPUB creator preference is to provide a scrolled view for overflow
L4759: content, and that consecutive spine items with this property are to be rendered as a
L4760: continuous scroll.
L4761: rendition:flow-scrolled-doc
L4762: Indicates the EPUB creator preference is to provide a scrolled view for overflow
L4763: content, and each spine item with this property is to be rendered as a separate
L4764: scrollable document.
L4765: EPUB creators MUST NOT use more than one of these overrides on any given spine item.
L4766: Example 66: Overriding a global paginated flow declaration
L4767: In this example, the EPUB creator's intent is to have a paginated EPUB publication
L4768: with a scrollable table of contents.
L4769: <package …>
L4770: <metadata …>
L4771: …
L4772: <meta
L4773: property="rendition:flow">
L4774: paginated
L4775: </meta>
L4776: …
L4777: </metadata>
L4778: …
L4779: <spine>
L4780: <itemref
L4781: idref="toc"
L4782: properties="rendition:flow-scrolled-doc"/>
L4783: <itemref
L4784: idref="c01"/>
L4785: </spine>
L4786: </package>
L4787: 8.3.2 The rendition:align-x-center property
L4788: The rendition:align-x-center property specifies that the given spine item should be centered horizontally in the viewport or spread.
L4789: The property MUST NOT be set globally for all EPUB content documents (i.e., in a meta
L4790: element without a refines attribute). It is only
L4791: available as a spine override for individual EPUB content documents via the itemref
L4792: element's properties attribute.
L4793: Note
L4794: This property was developed primarily to handle "Naka-Tobira (中扉)" (sectional title pages),
L4795: in the absence of reliable centering control within the content rendering. As support for
L4796: paged media evolves in CSS, however, this property is expected to be deprecated. EPUB creators are encouraged to use CSS solutions when effective.
L4797: 9. Media overlays
L4798: 9.1 Introduction
L4799: This section is non-normative.
L4800: Mainstream ebooks, educational tools and ebooks formatted for persons with print disabilities are
L4801: some examples of works that contain synchronized audio narration. In EPUB 3, EPUB creators can
L4802: create these types of books using media overlay documents to describe the timing for the
L4803: pre-recorded audio narration and how it relates to the EPUB content document markup. The
L4804: specification defines the file format for media overlays as a subset of [smil3], a W3C
L4805: recommendation for representing synchronized multimedia information in XML.
L4806: The text and audio synchronization enabled by media overlays provides enhanced accessibility for any
L4807: user who has difficulty following the text of a traditional book. Media overlays also provide a
L4808: continuous listening experience for readers who are unable to read the text for any reason,
L4809: something that traditional audio embedding techniques cannot offer. They are even useful for
L4810: purposes not traditionally considered accessibility concerns (e.g., for language learning).
L4811: The media overlays feature is transparent to EPUB reading systems that do not support the
L4812: feature. The inclusion of media overlays in an EPUB publication has no impact on the ability of
L4813: media overlay-unaware reading systems to render the EPUB publication as though the media overlays
L4814: are not present.
L4815: Media overlays in EPUB are not an equivalent to audiobooks, as audiobooks are primarily audio-based
L4816: with text occasionally provided as an alternate format. The W3C [audiobooks] recommendation is for
L4817: building audio publications.
L4818: Although future versions of this specification might incorporate support for video media (e.g.,
L4819: synchronized text/sign-language books), this version supports only synchronizing audio media with
L4820: the EPUB content document.
L4821: 9.2 Media overlay documents
L4822: 9.2.1 Media overlay document requirements
L4823: A media overlay document:
L4824: MUST be valid to the media overlays schema as defined in
L4825: G.3 Media overlays schema and conform to all content conformance
L4826: constraints expressed in 9.2.2 Media overlay document definition.
L4827: MAY refer to more than one EPUB content document,
L4828: but more than one media overlay document MUST NOT reference the same EPUB content
L4829: document.
L4830: 9.2.2 Media overlay document definition
L4831: All elements [xml] defined in this section are in the https://www.w3.org/ns/SMIL
L4832: namespace [xml-names] unless otherwise specified.
L4833: 9.2.2.1 The smil element
L4834: The smil element encapsulates all the information in an media overlay document.
L4835: Element Name:
L4836: smil
L4837: Usage:
L4838: REQUIRED root element [xml] of the media overlay
L4839: document.
L4840: Attributes:
L4841: version
L4842: [required]
L4843: Specifies the version number of the [smil3] specification to which the
L4844: media overlay document adheres.
L4845: This attribute MUST have the value "3.0".
L4846: id
L4847: [optional]
L4848: The ID [xml] of the element, which MUST be unique
L4849: within the document scope.
L4850: epub:prefix
L4851: [optional]
L4852: Declares additional metadata vocabulary prefixes.
L4853: Refer to 9.3.3 Structural semantics in overlays for more
L4854: information.
L4855: Content Model:
L4856: In this order:
L4857: head [0 or 1]
L4858: body [exactly 1]
