L4859: 9.2.2.2 The head element
L4860: The head element is the container for metadata in the media overlay document.
L4861: Element Name:
L4862: head
L4863: Usage:
L4864: The head element is the OPTIONAL first child of the smil
L4865: element.
L4866: Attributes:
L4867: None
L4868: Content Model:
L4869: metadata [0 or 1]
L4870: As this specification does not define any metadata properties that must occur in the media
L4871: overlay document, the head element is OPTIONAL.
L4872: 9.2.2.3 The metadata element
L4873: The metadata element represents metadata for the media overlay document. The
L4874: metadata element is an extension point that allows the inclusion of
L4875: metadata from any metainformation structuring language.
L4876: Element Name:
L4877: metadata
L4878: Usage:
L4879: As a child of the head element.
L4880: Attributes:
L4881: None
L4882: Content Model:
L4883: [0 or more] elements from any namespace
L4884: This specification does not require any metadata properties in the media overlay document;
L4885: the metadata element is provided for custom metadata requirements.
L4886: 9.2.2.4 The body element
L4887: The body element is the starting point for the presentation contained in the
L4888: media overlay document. It contains the main sequence of par and seq
L4889: elements.
L4890: Element Name:
L4891: body
L4892: Usage:
L4893: The body element is a REQUIRED child of the smil element. It follows
L4894: the head element, when that element is present.
L4895: Attributes:
L4896: epub:type
L4897: [optional]
L4898: An expression of the structural semantics of the corresponding element in the
L4899: EPUB content document.
L4900: The value is a whitespace-separated list of property types. Refer to 9.3.3 Structural semantics in overlays for more information.
L4901: id
L4902: [optional]
L4903: The ID [xml] of the element, which MUST be unique
L4904: within the document scope.
L4905: epub:textref
L4906: [optional]
L4907: Refers to the associated EPUB content document and, optionally, identifies a
L4908: specific part of it.
L4909: The value MUST be a path-relative-scheme-less-URL string, optionally
L4910: followed by U+0023 (#) and a URL-fragment string.
L4911: Content Model:
L4912: In any order:
L4913: seq [0 or more]
L4914: par [0 or more]
L4915: MUST include at least one par or seq.
L4916: 9.2.2.5 The seq element
L4917: The seq element is a sequential time container for media objects and/or child
L4918: time containers.
L4919: Element Name:
L4920: seq
L4921: Usage:
L4922: One or more seq elements MAY occur as children of the body element
L4923: and of the seq element.
L4924: Attributes:
L4925: epub:type
L4926: [optional]
L4927: An expression of the structural semantics of the corresponding element in the
L4928: EPUB content document.
L4929: The value is a whitespace-separated list of property types. Refer to 9.3.3 Structural semantics in overlays for more information.
L4930: id
L4931: [optional]
L4932: The ID [xml] of the element, which MUST be unique
L4933: within the document scope.
L4934: epub:textref
L4935: [required]
L4936: Refers to the associated EPUB content document and, optionally,
L4937: identifies a specific part of it.
L4938: The value MUST be a path-relative-scheme-less-URL string, optionally
L4939: followed by U+0023 (#) and a URL-fragment string.
L4940: Refer to 9.3.2.1 Overlay structure for more
L4941: information.
L4942: Content Model:
L4943: In any order:
L4944: seq [0 or more]
L4945: par [0 or more]
L4946: MUST include at least one par or seq.
L4947: 9.2.2.6 The par element
L4948: The par element is a parallel time container for media objects.
L4949: Element Name:
L4950: par
L4951: Usage:
L4952: One or more par elements MAY occur as children of the body and
L4953: seq elements.
L4954: Attributes:
L4955: epub:type
L4956: [optional]
L4957: An expression of the structural semantics of the corresponding element in the
L4958: EPUB content document.
L4959: The value is a whitespace-separated list of property types. Refer to 9.3.3 Structural semantics in overlays for more information.
L4960: id
L4961: [optional]
L4962: The ID [xml] of the element, which MUST be unique
L4963: within the document scope.
L4964: Content Model:
L4965: In any order:
L4966: text [exactly 1]
L4967: audio [0 or 1]
L4968: 9.2.2.7 The text element
L4969: The text element references an element in an EPUB content document. A
L4970: text element typically refers to a textual element but can also refer to
L4971: other EPUB content document media elements. In the absence of a sibling audio
L4972: element, textual content referred to by this element may be rendered via text-to-speech.
L4973: Element Name:
L4974: text
L4975: Usage:
L4976: As a REQUIRED child of the par element.
L4977: Attributes:
L4978: src
L4979: [required]
L4980: Refers to the associated EPUB content document and, optionally, identifies a
L4981: specific part of it.
L4982: The value MUST be a path-relative-scheme-less-URL string, optionally
L4983: followed by U+0023 (#) and a URL-fragment string.
L4984: id
L4985: [optional]
L4986: The ID [xml] of the element, which MUST be unique
L4987: within the document scope.
L4988: Content Model:
L4989: Empty
L4990: Note
L4991: This specification places no restriction on the src attribute of a
L4992: text element. EPUB creators should, however, refer to a content that
L4993: can be styled with CSS to make the association with style
L4994: information effective (i.e., palpable content for XHTML or paths, basic shapes, or text elements in SVG).
L4995: Note
L4996: [epub-rs-33] no longer provides guidance for reading systems on the playback
L4997: of timed media (i.e., the automatic starting of the referenced media). Although the
L4998: src attribute of a text element may refer to embedded timed
L4999: media (e.g., via an [html] video element), referencing such media may have
L5000: unpredictable results.
L5001: 9.2.2.8 The audio element
L5002: The audio element represents a clip of audio media.
L5003: Element Name:
L5004: audio
L5005: Usage:
L5006: An OPTIONAL child of the par element.
L5007: Attributes:
L5008: id
L5009: [optional]
L5010: The ID [xml] of the element, which MUST be unique
L5011: within the document scope.
L5012: src
L5013: [required]
L5014: The relative- or absolute-URL string [url] reference to an audio file. The audio
L5015: file MUST be one of the audio formats listed in the core media type resources table.
L5016: clipBegin
L5017: [optional]
L5018: A clock value that specifies the offset into the physical media corresponding
L5019: to the start point of an audio clip.
L5020: MUST be a [smil3] clock
L5021: value.
L5022: See H.4 Clock values.
L5023: clipEnd
L5024: [optional]
L5025: A clock value that specifies the offset into the physical media corresponding
L5026: to the end point of an audio clip.
L5027: MUST be a [smil3] clock
L5028: value.
L5029: See H.4 Clock values.
L5030: The chronological offset of the terminating position MUST be after the
L5031: starting offset specified in the clipBegin attribute.
L5032: Content Model:
L5033: Empty
L5034: 9.3 Creating media overlays
L5035: 9.3.1 Introduction
L5036: This section is non-normative.
L5037: EPUB creators can represent a pre-recorded narration of a publication as a series of audio
L5038: clips, each corresponding to part of an EPUB content document. A single audio clip, for
L5039: example, typically represents a single phrase or paragraph, but infers no order relative to the
L5040: other clips or to the text of a document. Media overlays solve this problem of synchronization
L5041: by tying the structured audio narration to its corresponding text (or other media) in the EPUB
L5042: content document using [smil3] markup. Media overlays are, in fact, a simplified subset of
L5043: SMIL 3.0 that define the playback sequence of these clips.
L5044: The SMIL elements primarily used for structuring media overlays are body (used for the main
L5045: sequence), seq (sequence) and par (parallel). (Refer to 9.2.2 Media overlay document definition
L5046: for more information on these and other SMIL elements.)
L5047: The par element is the basic building block of a media overlay and corresponds to a
L5048: phrase in the EPUB content document. The element provides two key pieces of information for
L5049: synchronizing content: 1) the audio clip containing the narration for the phrase; and 2) a
L5050: pointer to the associated EPUB content document fragment. The par element uses two
L5051: media element children to represent this information: an audio element and a text
L5052: element. Because par elements' media object children are timed in parallel, reading
L5053: systems render the audio clip and EPUB content document fragment at the same time, resulting in
L5054: a synchronized presentation.
L5055: The text element src attribute references the associated phrase,
L5056: sentence, or other segment of the EPUB content document by its URL [url] reference. The
L5057: audio element src attribute similarly references the location of
L5058: the corresponding audio clip and adds the OPTIONAL clipBegin and clipEnd
L5059: attributes to indicate a specific offset within the clip.
L5060: Example 67: Media overlays markup for a single phrase or sentence
L5061: <par>
L5062: <text
L5063: src="chapter1.xhtml#sentence1"/>
L5064: <audio
L5065: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5066: clipBegin="23s"
L5067: clipEnd="30s"/>
L5068: </par>
L5069: EPUB creators place par elements together sequentially to form a series of phrases
L5070: or sentences. Not every element of the EPUB content document will have a corresponding
L5071: par element in a media overlay document, only those relevant to the audio
L5072: narration.
L5073: Example 68: A basic media overlay document containing a sequence of phrases
L5074: In this example, the body element acts as the main sequence for the whole
L5075: document.
L5076: <smil
L5077: xmlns="http://www.w3.org/ns/SMIL"
L5078: version="3.0">
L5079: <body>
L5080: <par id="par1">
L5081: <text
L5082: src="chapter1.xhtml#sentence1"/>
L5083: <audio
L5084: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5085: clipBegin="0s"
L5086: clipEnd="10s"/>
L5087: </par>
L5088: <par id="par2">
L5089: <text
L5090: src="chapter1.xhtml#sentence2"/>
L5091: <audio
L5092: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5093: clipBegin="10s"
L5094: clipEnd="20s"/>
L5095: </par>
L5096: <par id="par3">
L5097: <text
L5098: src="chapter1.xhtml#sentence3"/>
L5099: <audio
L5100: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5101: clipBegin="20s"
L5102: clipEnd="30s"/>
L5103: </par>
L5104: </body>
L5105: </smil>
L5106: EPUB creators can also add par elements to seq elements to define more
L5107: complex structures such as parts and chapters (see 9.3.2.1 Overlay structure).
L5108: 9.3.2 Relationship to the EPUB content document
L5109: Note
L5110: In this section, the EPUB content document is assumed to be an XHTML content document. While EPUB creators may use media overlays with SVG content documents,
L5111: playback behavior might not be consistent and therefore interoperability is not
L5112: guaranteed.
L5113: EPUB creators should also be aware that reading system support for playback of both
L5114: reflowable and fixed-layout EPUB content documents is not guaranteed. Differences in reading
L5115: system pagination strategies mean that some reading systems will only support media overlays
L5116: in one or the other layout format.
L5117: 9.3.2.1 Overlay structure
L5118: The body of a media overlay document consists of two elements: the par element
L5119: and the seq element. The ordering of these elements represents how reading systems
L5120: render the content in the corresponding EPUB content documents during playback.
L5121: The par element represents a segment of content, such as a word, phrase,
L5122: sentence, table cell, list item, image, or other identifiable piece of content in the
L5123: markup. Each element identifies both the content to display (in the text element) and
L5124: audio to synchronize (in the audio element) during playback.
L5125: The seq element represents sequences — sets of seq and/or
L5126: par elements that together represent a logical component of the content.
L5127: EPUB creators can use it to represent nested containers such as sections, asides,
L5128: headers, tables, lists, and footnotes. It allows EPUB creators to retain the structure
L5129: inherent in these containers in the media overlay document.
L5130: The seq element MUST contain an epub:textref attribute. As seq elements do not
L5131: provide synchronization instructions, this attribute allows a reading system to match
L5132: the fragment to a location in the text.
L5133: Note
L5134: The reason for grouping structures like sections, figures, tables, and footnotes in a
L5135: seq element is so that reading systems can identify their start and end
L5136: positions during playback. Reading systems can then offer playback options tailored to
L5137: the layout of the content, such as jumping past a long figure, turning off rendering of
L5138: page break announcements (see 9.4 Skippability and escapability), or customizing
L5139: the reading mode to suit structures such as tables.
L5140: Example 69: A media overlay document with nested seq
L5141: elements
L5142: This example shows a chapter with both a section header and a figure.
L5143: Media overlay document:
L5144: <smil
L5145: xmlns="http://www.w3.org/ns/SMIL"
L5146: xmlns:epub="http://www.idpf.org/2007/ops"
L5147: version="3.0">
L5148: <body>
L5149: <!-- a chapter -->
L5150: <seq
L5151: id="id1"
L5152: epub:textref="chapter1.xhtml#sectionstart"
L5153: epub:type="chapter">
L5154: <!-- the section title -->
L5155: <par id="id2">
L5156: <text
L5157: src="chapter1.xhtml#section1_title"/>
L5158: <audio
L5159: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5160: clipBegin="0:23:23.84"
L5161: clipEnd="0:23:34.221"/>
L5162: </par>
L5163: <!-- some sentences in the chapter -->
L5164: <par id="id3">
L5165: <text
L5166: src="chapter1.xhtml#text1"/>
L5167: <audio
L5168: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5169: clipBegin="0:23:34.221"
L5170: clipEnd="0:23:59.003"/>
L5171: </par>
L5172: <par id="id4">
L5173: <text
L5174: src="chapter1.xhtml#text2"/>
L5175: <audio
L5176: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5177: clipBegin="0:23:59.003"
L5178: clipEnd="0:24:15.000"/>
L5179: </par>
L5180: <!-- a figure -->
L5181: <seq
L5182: id="id7"
L5183: epub:textref="chapter1.xhtml#figure">
L5184: <par id="id8">
L5185: <text
L5186: src="chapter1.xhtml#photo"/>
L5187: <audio
L5188: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5189: clipBegin="0:24:18.123"
L5190: clipEnd="0:24:28.764"/>
L5191: </par>
L5192: <par id="id9">
L5193: <text
L5194: src="chapter1.xhtml#caption"/>
L5195: <audio
L5196: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5197: clipBegin="0:24:28.764"
L5198: clipEnd="0:24:50.010"/>
L5199: </par>
L5200: </seq>
L5201: <!-- more sentences in the chapter (outside the figure) -->
L5202: <par id="id12">
L5203: <text
L5204: src="chapter1.xhtml#text3"/>
L5205: <audio
L5206: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5207: clipBegin="0:25:45.515"
L5208: clipEnd="0:26:30.203"/>
L5209: </par>
L5210: <par id="id13">
L5211: <text
L5212: src="chapter1.xhtml#text4"/>
L5213: <audio
L5214: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5215: clipBegin="0:26:30.203"
L5216: clipEnd="0:27:15.000"/>
L5217: </par>
L5218: </seq>
L5219: </body>
L5220: </smil>
L5221: XHTML content document:
L5222: <html …>
L5223: <head>
L5224: <title>
L5225: Media Overlays Example of
L5226: EPUB content document
L5227: </title>
L5228: </head>
L5229: <body id="sec1">
L5230: <section
L5231: id="sectionstart"
L5232: epub:type="chapter">
L5233: <h1 id="section1_title">
L5234: The Section Title
L5235: </h1>
L5236: <p id="text1">
L5237: The first phrase of the main text body.
L5238: </p>
L5239: <p id="text2">
L5240: The second phrase of the main text body.
L5241: </p>
L5242: <figure id="figure">
L5243: <img id="photo"
L5244: src="photo.png"
L5245: alt="a photograph for which there is a caption" />
L5246: <figcaption id="caption">
L5247: The photo caption
L5248: </figcaption>
L5249: </figure>
L5250: <p id="text3">
L5251: The third phrase of the main text body.
L5252: </p>
L5253: <p id="text4">
L5254: The fourth phrase of the main text body.
L5255: </p>
L5256: </section>
L5257: </body>
L5258: </html>
L5259: 9.3.2.2 Referencing document fragments
L5260: Both the epub:textref attribute and the text element's src
L5261: attribute may contain a URL-fragment string that references a specific part (e.g., an
L5262: element via its ID) of the associated EPUB content document.
L5263: For XHTML and SVG content documents, the URL-fragment string
L5264: SHOULD be a reference to a specific element via its ID, or an SVG Fragment
L5265: Identifier [svg], respectively.
L5266: EPUB creators MAY use other fragment identifier schemes, but reading systems may not
L5267: support such identifiers.
L5268: 9.3.2.3 Overlay granularity
L5269: This section is non-normative.
L5270: The granularity level of the media overlay depends on how EPUB creators mark up the
L5271: EPUB content document and the type of fragment identifier they use in the text
L5272: elements' src attributes and the seq elements' epub:textref
L5273: attributes. For example, when referencing [html] elements, if the finest level of markup
L5274: is at the paragraph level, then that is the finest possible level for media overlay
L5275: synchronization. Likewise, if sub-paragraph markup is available, such as [html] span
L5276: element representing phrases or sentences, then finer granularity is possible in the media
L5277: overlay. Finer granularity gives users more precise results for synchronized playback when
L5278: navigating by word or phrase and when searching the text but increases the file size of the
L5279: media overlay documents. Fragment identifier schemes that do not rely on the presence of
L5280: elements could provide even finer granularity, where supported.
L5281: 9.3.2.4 Text-to-speech rendering
L5282: This specification allows the use of text-to-speech (TTS) — the rendering of the textual
L5283: content of an EPUB publication as artificial human speech using a synthesized voice — in
L5284: addition to pre-recorded audio clips.
L5285: When a media overlay par element omits its audio element, its text element may be
L5286: rendered in reading systems via TTS. If the text fragment is not appropriate for TTS
L5287: rendering (e.g., is not a text element and/or has no text fallback), this may produce
L5288: unexpected results.
L5289: Note
L5290: See EPUB 3 Text-to-Speech Support [epub-tts-10] for
L5291: more information about using TTS technologies in EPUB publications.
L5292: 9.3.3 Structural semantics in overlays
L5293: To express structural semantics in media overlay documents, EPUB creators MAY specify the epub:type attribute on par, seq, and
L5294: body elements.
L5295: The epub:type attribute facilitates reading system behavior appropriate for the
L5296: semantic type(s) indicated. Examples of these behaviors are skippability and escapability and table reading mode [epub-rs-33].
L5297: Media overlay documents MAY use the applicable vocabulary
L5298: association mechanisms for the epub:type attribute to define additional
L5299: semantics.
L5300: Example 70: Semantic markup for a media overlay document containing a figure
L5301: <smil
L5302: xmlns="http://www.w3.org/ns/SMIL"
L5303: xmlns:epub="http://www.idpf.org/2007/ops"
L5304: version="3.0">
L5305: <body>
L5306: <seq
L5307: id="id1"
L5308: epub:textref="chapter1.xhtml#figure"
L5309: epub:type="figure">
L5310: <par id="id2">
L5311: <text
L5312: src="chapter1.xhtml#figuretitle"/>
L5313: <audio
L5314: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5315: clipBegin="0:24:15.000"
L5316: clipEnd="0:24:18.123"/>
L5317: </par>
L5318: <par id="id3">
L5319: <text
L5320: src="chapter1.xhtml#figurecaption"/>
L5321: <audio
L5322: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5323: clipBegin="0:24:18.123"
L5324: clipEnd="0:24:38.530"/>
L5325: </par>
L5326: <par id="id4">
L5327: <text
L5328: src="chapter1.xhtml#figuretext1"/>
L5329: <audio
L5330: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5331: clipBegin="0:24:38.530"
L5332: clipEnd="0:25:00.515"/>
L5333: </par>
L5334: </seq>
L5335: </body>
L5336: </smil>
L5337: 9.3.4 Associating style information
L5338: EPUB creators MAY express visual rendering information for the currently playing EPUB content document element in a CSS Style Sheet using author-defined classes.
L5339: When used, EPUB creators MUST declare the class names in the package document using the active-class and playback-active-class properties.
L5340: EPUB creators MUST define exactly one CSS class name in each property they define. Each property
L5341: MUST define a valid CSS class
L5342: name not including any selectors
L5343: [css2]. This specification does not reserve names for use with these
L5344: properties.
L5345: EPUB creators MAY define any CSS properties for the specified CSS classes but must ensure that
L5346: each EPUB content document with an associated media overlay document includes a CSS
L5347: stylesheet (either embedded or linked) containing the class definitions. In the absence of such
L5348: definitions reading systems might provide their own styling, or no styling at all.
L5349: EPUB creators MUST NOT use the active-class and playback-active-class
L5350: properties in conjunction with a refines attribute
L5351: as they always apply to the entire EPUB publication.
L5352: Example 71: Associating style information with the
L5353: currently playing EPUB content document
L5354: The author-defined CSS class names are declared using the metadata properties active-class and playback-active-class in the package document:
L5355: <package …>
L5356: <metadata …>
L5357: …
L5358: <meta
L5359: property="media:active-class">
L5360: my-active-item
L5361: </meta>
L5362: <meta
L5363: property="media:playback-active-class">
L5364: my-document-playing
L5365: </meta>
L5366: …
L5367: </metadata>
L5368: …
L5369: </package>
L5370: The CSS Style Sheet containing the author-defined class names:
L5371: /* emphasize the active element */
L5372: .my-active-item {
L5373: background-color: yellow;
L5374: color: black !important;
L5375: }
L5376: /* fade out the inactive text */
L5377: html.my-document-playing * {
L5378: color: gray;
L5379: }
L5380: The relevant EPUB content document excerpt:
L5381: <html>
L5382: …
L5383: <body>
L5384: …
L5385: <span id="txt1">
L5386: This is the first phrase.
L5387: </span>
L5388: <span id="txt2">
L5389: This is the second phrase.
L5390: </span>
L5391: <span id="txt3">
L5392: This is the third phrase.
L5393: </span>
L5394: …
L5395: </body>
L5396: </html>
L5397: In this example, the reading system would apply the author-defined
L5398: my-active-item class to each text element in the EPUB content document as
L5399: it became active during playback. Conversely, reading systems would remove the class name
L5400: when the element is no longer active. The user would see each EPUB content document element
L5401: styled with a yellow background for the duration of that element's playback.
L5402: The reading system would also apply the author-defined my-document-playing class
L5403: to the document element of the EPUB content document when media overlays playback begins.
L5404: The reading system would remove the class name when playback stops. In the case of an
L5405: XHTML content document, the reading system would apply the class name to the html
L5406: element [html]. In the case of an SVG content document, the reading system would
L5407: apply the class name to the svg element [svg]. The user would see all the inactive
L5408: text elements turn gray during media overlays playback. When playback stopped, the elements'
L5409: colors would return to their defaults.
L5410: 9.3.5 Media overlays packaging
L5411: 9.3.5.1 Including media overlays
L5412: If an EPUB content document is wholly or partially referenced by a media overlay document, then its manifest item element MUST specify a
L5413: media-overlay attribute. The attribute MUST reference the ID [xml] of the manifest item for the
L5414: corresponding media overlay document.
L5415: EPUB creators MUST only specify the media-overlay attribute on manifest
L5416: item elements that reference EPUB content documents.
L5417: Manifest items for media overlay documents MUST have the media type
L5418: application/smil+xml.
L5419: Example 72: Entries for an EPUB content document and its associated
L5420: media overlay document in the package document manifest
L5421: <package …>
L5422: …
L5423: <manifest>
L5424: <item
L5425: id="ch1"
L5426: href="chapter1.xhtml"
L5427: media-type="application/xhtml+xml"
L5428: media-overlay="ch1_audio"/>
L5429: <item
L5430: id="ch1_audio"
L5431: href="chapter1_audio.smil"
L5432: media-type="application/smil+xml"/>
L5433: …
L5434: </manifest>
L5435: </package>
L5436: 9.3.5.2 Overlays package metadata
L5437: EPUB creators MUST specify the duration of the entire EPUB publication in the package document using a meta element with the duration property.
L5438: In addition, EPUB creators MUST provide the duration of each media overlay document. EPUB
L5439: creators MUST use the refines attribute to
L5440: associate each duration declaration to the corresponding manifest
L5441: item.
L5442: The sum of the durations for each media overlay document SHOULD equal the total duration plus or minus one second.
L5443: Note
L5444: Although the sum of individual durations may not exactly match the total due to rounding
L5445: the times to nearest fraction of a second, a difference of greater than one second
L5446: indicates a mismatch arising from other issues.
L5447: EPUB creators MAY also specify narrator information
L5448: in the package document, as well as author-defined CSS class
L5449: names to apply to the currently playing EPUB content document element.
L5450: Note
L5451: The media: prefix is reserved
L5452: for inclusion of these properties in package metadata.
L5453: Example 73: Media overlays metadata in the package document
L5454: <package …>
L5455: <metadata …>
L5456: …
L5457: <meta
L5458: property="media:duration"
L5459: refines="#ch1_audio">
L5460: 0:32:29
L5461: </meta>
L5462: <meta
L5463: property="media:duration"
L5464: refines="#ch2_audio">
L5465: 0:34:02
L5466: </meta>
L5467: <meta
L5468: property="media:duration"
L5469: refines="#ch3_audio">
L5470: 0:29:49
L5471: </meta>
L5472: <meta
L5473: property="media:duration">
L5474: 1:36:20
L5475: </meta>
L5476: <meta
L5477: property="media:narrator">
L5478: Joe Speaker
L5479: </meta>
L5480: <meta
L5481: property="media:active-class">
L5482: my-active-item
L5483: </meta>
L5484: <meta
L5485: property="media:playback-active-class">
L5486: my-document-playing
L5487: </meta>
L5488: …
L5489: </metadata>
L5490: </package>
L5491: 9.4 Skippability and escapability
L5492: 9.4.1 Skippability
L5493: While reading, users may want to turn on or off certain features of the content, such as
L5494: footnotes, page numbers, or other types of secondary content. This feature is called
L5495: skippability. Reading systems use the semantic information provided by media overlay
L5496: elements' epub:type attribute to
L5497: determine when to offer users the option of skippable features.
L5498: EPUB creators MAY use the following semantics to enable skippability:
L5499: footnote [epub-ssv-11]
L5500: endnote [epub-ssv-11]
L5501: pagebreak [epub-ssv-11]
L5502: This list is non-exhaustive, however. It represents terms from the Structural Semantics
L5503: Vocabulary [epub-ssv-11] for which reading systems are most likely to offer the option
L5504: of skippability.
L5505: Example 74: Media overlay document with a page break
L5506: In this example, a reading system could offer the user the option of turning on and off the
L5507: page break/page number announcements, which are often cumbersome to listen to.
L5508: Media overlay document:
L5509: <smil
L5510: xmlns="http://www.w3.org/ns/SMIL"
L5511: xmlns:epub="http://www.idpf.org/2007/ops"
L5512: version="3.0">
L5513: <body>
L5514: <!-- a paragraph -->
L5515: <par id="id1">
L5516: <text
L5517: src="chapter1.xhtml#para1"/>
L5518: <audio
L5519: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5520: clipBegin="0:23:22.000"
L5521: clipEnd="0:24:15.000"/>
L5522: </par>
L5523: <!-- a page number -->
L5524: <par
L5525: id="id2"
L5526: epub:type="pagebreak">
L5527: <text
L5528: src="chapter1.xhtml#pgbreak1"/>
L5529: <audio
L5530: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5531: clipBegin="0:24:15.000"
L5532: clipEnd="0:24:18.123"/>
L5533: </par>
L5534: <!-- another paragraph -->
L5535: <par id="id3">
L5536: <text
L5537: src="chapter1.xhtml#para2"/>
L5538: <audio
L5539: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5540: clipBegin="0:24:18.123"
L5541: clipEnd="0:25:28.530"/>
L5542: </par>
L5543: </body>
L5544: </smil>
L5545: EPUB content document:
L5546: <html … >
L5547: …
L5548: <body>
L5549: <p id="para1">
L5550: This is the paragraph before the page break …
L5551: </p>
L5552: <span
L5553: id="pgbreak1"
L5554: role="doc-pagebreak"
L5555: aria-label="14"/>
L5556: <p id="para2">
L5557: This is the paragraph after the page break …
L5558: </p>
L5559: </body>
L5560: </html>
L5561: 9.4.2 Escapability
L5562: Escapable items are nested structures, such as tables and lists, that users might wish to skip
L5563: over, continuing to read from the point immediately after the nested structure. The escapability
L5564: feature differs from the skippability feature in that it does not enable or disable entire types
L5565: of items, but provides an exit from them (e.g., a user can listen to some of the content before
L5566: choosing to escape).
L5567: EPUB creators MAY use the following semantics to enable escapability:
L5568: table [epub-ssv-11]
L5569: list [epub-ssv-11]
L5570: figure [epub-ssv-11]
L5571: aside [epub-ssv-11]
L5572: This list is non-exhaustive list, however. It represents terms from the Structural Semantics
L5573: Vocabulary [epub-ssv-11] for which reading systems are most likely to offer the
L5574: option of escapability.
L5575: Note
L5576: Sometimes escapable structures may contain escapable structures. For example, tables are
L5577: composed of many rows and cells that users may want to separately escape from. Reading
L5578: system support for escaping from such structures is complex and not well supported at this
L5579: time. EPUB creators should avoid identifying nested escapable structures until better
L5580: support is available.
L5581: Example 75: Escapable structures
L5582: In this example, the media overlay document for an EPUB content document contains a
L5583: paragraph, a table, and another paragraph. A reading system that supported escapability
L5584: would give the user the option to interrupt playback of the table to continue playing the
L5585: next paragraph.
L5586: <smil
L5587: xmlns="http://www.w3.org/ns/SMIL"
L5588: xmlns:epub="http://www.idpf.org/2007/ops"
L5589: version="3.0">
L5590: <body>
L5591: <!-- a paragraph, part of the regular document text -->
L5592: <par id="id1">
L5593: <text
L5594: src="c01.xhtml#para1"/>
L5595: <audio
L5596: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5597: clipBegin="0:23:22.000"
L5598: clipEnd="0:24:15.000"/>
L5599: </par>
L5600: <!-- a table with two nested rows -->
L5601: <seq
L5602: id="id2"
L5603: epub:textref="c01.xhtml#t1"
L5604: epub:type="table">
L5605: <seq
L5606: id="id3"
L5607: epub:textref="c01.xhtml#tr1"
L5608: epub:type="table-row">
L5609: <par
L5610: id="id4"
L5611: epub:type="table-cell">
L5612: <text
L5613: src="c01.xhtml#td1"/>
L5614: <audio
L5615: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5616: clipBegin="0:24:15.000"
L5617: clipEnd="0:24:18.123"/>
L5618: </par>
L5619: <par
L5620: id="id5"
L5621: epub:type="table-cell">
L5622: <text
L5623: src="c01.xhtml#td2"/>
L5624: <audio
L5625: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5626: clipBegin="0:24:18.123"
L5627: clipEnd="0:25:28.530"/>
L5628: </par>
L5629: <par
L5630: id="id6"
L5631: epub:type="table-cell">
L5632: <text
L5633: src="c01.xhtml#td3"/>
L5634: <audio
L5635: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5636: clipBegin="0:25:28.530"
L5637: clipEnd="0:25:45.515"/>
L5638: </par>
L5639: </seq>
L5640: <seq
L5641: id="id7"
L5642: epub:textref="c01.xhtml#tr2"
L5643: epub:type="table-row">
L5644: <par
L5645: id="id8"
L5646: epub:type="table-cell">
L5647: <text
L5648: src="c01.xhtml#td4"/>
L5649: <audio
L5650: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5651: clipBegin="0:25:45.515"
L5652: clipEnd="0:26:45.700"/>
L5653: </par>
L5654: <par
L5655: id="id9"
L5656: epub:type="table-cell">
L5657: <text
L5658: src="chapter1.xhtml#td5"/>
L5659: <audio
L5660: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5661: clipBegin="0:26:45.700"
L5662: clipEnd="0:28:02.033"/>
L5663: </par>
L5664: <par
L5665: id="id10"
L5666: epub:type="table-cell">
L5667: <text
L5668: src="chapter1.xhtml#td6"/>
L5669: <audio
L5670: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5671: clipBegin="0:28:02.033"
L5672: clipEnd="0:28:52.207"/>
L5673: </par>
L5674: </seq>
L5675: </seq>
L5676: <!-- another paragraph -->
L5677: <par id="id11">
L5678: <text
L5679: src="c01.xhtml#para2"/>
L5680: <audio
L5681: src="chapter1_audio.mp3"
L5682: clipBegin="0:28:52.207"
L5683: clipEnd="0:30:01.000"/>
L5684: </par>
L5685: </body>
L5686: </smil>
L5687: 9.5 Navigation document overlays
L5688: This section is non-normative.
L5689: As the EPUB navigation document is an XHTML content document, EPUB creators may associate
L5690: a media overlay document with it. Unlike traditional XHTML content documents, however, reading systems must present the EPUB navigation document to users even when it is not included in the
L5691: spine (see Navigation document processing
L5692: [epub-rs-33]). As a result, the method in which an associated media overlay behaves can change
L5693: depending on the context:
L5694: When included in the spine, playback of the EPUB navigation document's media overlay document
L5695: obeys the same conformance requirements as with any other XHTML content document.
L5696: When exposed in a presentation context that allows users to access and activate the links,
L5697: reading systems may implement additional presentation behaviors to expose audio feedback
L5698: when user access navigation links.
L5699: Note
L5700: Specific implementation details are beyond the scope of this specification. The DAISY Media Overlays Playback Requirements document describes best practices for EPUB creators and provides recommendations for reading system developers.
L5701: 10. Accessibility
L5702: This section is non-normative.
L5703: EPUB 3 builds upon the Open Web Platform expressly so that it can leverage the structure, semantics and,
L5704: by extension, accessibility built into its underlying technologies.
L5705: The requirements and practices for creating accessible web content have already been documented in the
L5706: W3C's Web Content Accessibility Guidelines (WCAG) [wcag2].
L5707: These guidelines also form the basis for defining accessibility in EPUB publications.
L5708: As the current WCAG guidelines (version 2) are heavily focused on web pages, a separate specification, EPUB Accessibility [epub-a11y-11], defines how to apply the standard
L5709: to EPUB publications. It also adds EPUB-specific requirements and recommendations for metadata,
L5710: pagination, and media overlays.
L5711: This specification recommends that EPUB publications conform to the accessibility
L5712: requirements defined in [epub-a11y-11]. A benefit of following this recommendation is
L5713: that it helps to ensure that EPUB publications meet the accessibility requirements legislated in
L5714: jurisdictions around the world.
L5715: EPUB creators, however, should look beyond legal imperatives and treat accessibility as a requirement
L5716: for all their content. The more accessible that EPUB publications are, the greater the potential
L5717: audience for them.
L5718: Note
L5719: This specification does not integrate the accessibility requirements to allow them to adapt and
L5720: evolve independent of the EPUB specification — accessibility practices often need more frequent
L5721: updating. The accessibility specification is also intended for use with past, present, and future
L5722: versions of EPUB. The approach of a separate specification ensures that the evolution of EPUB does
L5723: not lock accessibility in time (i.e., it allows producers of older versions of EPUB to reference the
L5724: latest accessibility requirements).
L5725: 11. Security and privacy
L5726: 11.1 Overview
L5727: This section is non-normative.
L5728: The particularity of an EPUB publication is its structure. The EPUB format provides a means of
L5729: representing, packaging, and encoding structured and semantically enhanced web
L5730: content — including HTML, CSS, SVG, JavaScript, and other resources — for
L5731: distribution in a single-file container.
L5732: This means that EPUB 3's security and privacy issues are primarily linked to the features of those
L5733: formats, and closely mirror the threats presented by web content.
L5734: Although content risks are often equated with deliberately malicious authoring intent, EPUB creators need to be aware that many practices followed with the best of intentions may expose
L5735: users to privacy and security issues. The rest of this section explores the risk model of EPUB 3
L5736: with the aim of helping EPUB creators recognize and mitigate these risks.
L5737: Note
L5738: For the risks associated with reading systems, refer to the security and privacy section of
L5739: [epub-rs-33].
L5740: 11.2 Threat model
L5741: This section is non-normative.
L5742: EPUB publications pose a variety of privacy and security threats to unsuspecting users. Many of
L5743: these threats intersect with web content, but EPUB also introduces its own unique methods of attack
L5744: that can be used to trick users into accessing malicious content or into providing sensitive
L5745: information. Some of the more important attack vectors that EPUB creators and users need to be
L5746: aware of include:
L5747: Embedding of remote resources
L5748: EPUB 3 allows some publication resources to be remotely
L5749: hosted, specifically resources whose sizes can negatively affect the downloading and
L5750: opening of the EPUB publication (e.g., audio, video, and fonts). Although helpful for users
L5751: when used as intended, these exemptions can also be used to inject malicious content into a
L5752: publication.
L5753: This threat is not limited to accessing content created by a bad actor. If EPUB creators
L5754: embed content from untrustworthy sources (e.g., third party audio and video), there is
L5755: always the possibility that users may receive compromised resources.
L5756: Checking for malware and exploits at distribution time is not always reliable, either, as the
L5757: malicious content can be swapped in any time after publication, unlike resources that come
L5758: embedded in the EPUB container.
L5759: The origin of an EPUB is both unknown to the EPUB creator
L5760: and specific to each reading system implementation. Consequently, if the EPUB creator hosts
L5761: remote resources on a web server they control, the server effectively cannot use
L5762: security features that require specifying allowable origins, such as headers for CORS, Content-Security-Policy, or X-Frame-Options.
L5763: Linking to external resources
L5764: Whether intentional or not, links to external web sites and resources expose users to
L5765: potential exploits that can compromise their reading system or operating system. Although
L5766: external links will typically open in a web browser, and be subject to the browser security
L5767: model, this does not protect users from all exploits.
L5768: Even if the intentions of the EPUB creator are not malicious, adding tracking information to
L5769: external links is problematic for user privacy as it can allow a user's activity to be
L5770: tracked without their consent.
L5771: Broken-link hijacking — when a domain expires and is bought by another party to exploit the
L5772: links to it — can also lead to users being taken to resources the EPUB creator did not
L5773: intend.
L5774: Including malicious content
L5775: Resources embedded in the EPUB container are not immune to malicious actors, especially when
L5776: EPUB publications are obtained from untrusted sources. Resources may contain exploits or
L5777: forms that may submit sensitive information to unintended parties. Such actors may also try
L5778: to gain access to remote resources using file indirection techniques, such as symbolic
L5779: links or file aliases.
L5780: The use of third-party content, such as games and quizzes, may also lead to security and
L5781: privacy issues if the EPUB creator is not able to fully vet the content.
L5782: Allowing scripts network access
L5783: When scripts can access a device's network, it provides a variety channels to exploit the
L5784: user:
L5785: collecting information about the user and their activities, whether malicious or
L5786: not;
L5787: attempting to access the file system and local storage to harvest information;
L5788: phishing attempts (e.g., making an EPUB content document appear like a trusted web
L5789: site to get the user to submit login information); and
L5790: injecting malicious content from external sites into the EPUB publication.
L5791: Network access may allow third-party content to exploit the user even if it was not the EPUB
L5792: creator's intent.
L5793: Securing content with digital rights management
L5794: The encryption and decryption of EPUB publications using digital rights management schemes
L5795: may allow personally identifiable information about the user, what vendors they use, and
L5796: their reading choices to be relayed to third parties.
L5797: The effectiveness of these attacks also often depends on tricking users into believing that the
L5798: publication they are interacting with is from a trustworthy source. These deceptions can take the
L5799: following forms:
L5800: Falsified publication information
L5801: The EPUB publication may include false information about itself to trick users into believing
L5802: that it comes from a legitimate source. A malicious EPUB creator might, for example, fake
L5803: the title, authors, identifiers, and publisher for the work.
L5804: Although this misinformation itself does not present an immediate harm, it could lead users
L5805: to trust malicious forms, links, and other content within the EPUB publication believing it
L5806: comes from a reliable source.
L5807: Spoofed platforms
L5808: Malicious EPUB creators may also design their content to imitate or replicate a platform's
L5809: experience to trick users into trusting their content.
L5810: 11.2.1 EPUB-specific features
L5811: EPUB 3 tries to avoid extending the underlying technologies it builds on, but it has introduced
L5812: some new features. The restricted scope of these features limits the threats they might pose,
L5813: however:
L5814: Content switching and multimedia control elements only allow hiding of
L5815: content and script-less control of playback in HTML. Moreover, these features,
L5816: introduced in the first release of EPUB 3.0, are deprecated
L5817: and no longer recommended for use.
L5818: The expression of structural semantics in HTML and
L5819: SVG only allows the annotation of elements.
L5820: The one potential exception is the epubReadingSystem object [epub-rs-33] that allows EPUB creators
L5821: to query information about the current reading system. EPUB creators need to be mindful that
L5822: they only use the information exposed by this object to improve the rendering of their content
L5823: (i.e., avoid using the information to profile the user and their environment).
L5824: 11.3 Recommendations
L5825: Although EPUB creators cannot prevent every method of exploiting users, they are ultimately
L5826: responsible for the secure construction of their content. That means that they need to take
L5827: precautions to limit the exposure of their EPUB publications to the types of malicious exploits described in the previous section.
L5828: Some practical steps include:
L5829: Ensuring the use of stable links to remote resources.
L5830: Avoiding third-party resources, especially those hosted on servers outside the control of the
L5831: EPUB creator.
L5832: Avoiding links to untrustworthy web sites (e.g., that browsers do not recognize as safe).
L5833: Using secure connections to external sites and resources (i.e., using the HTTPS protocol).
L5834: Not using scripts to send or receive data over the network without the consent of the user.
L5835: Obtaining user consent for the use of Web APIs with privacy implications, such as Geolocation [geolocation] and Push Notifications [push-api].
L5836: Avoiding embedding content not provided by reputable organizations or individuals.
L5837: Avoiding deprecated features of EPUB due to the potential for undiscovered bugs in
L5838: implementations.
L5839: EPUB creators also need to consider the privacy rights of users and avoid situations where they are
L5840: intentionally collecting data. Ideally, EPUB creators SHOULD NOT track their users, but this is not
L5841: realistic for all types of publishing.
L5842: When EPUB creators have to track users, they SHOULD obtain the approval of the user to collect
L5843: information prior to opening the EPUB publication (e.g., in educational course work). If this is not
L5844: possible, they SHOULD obtain permission when users access the EPUB publication for the first time.
L5845: EPUB creators SHOULD also allow users to opt out of tracking, and provide users the ability to
L5846: manage and delete any data that is collected about them.
L5847: EPUB creators also need to consider the inadvertent collection of information about users. Linking to
L5848: content on a publisher's web site, or remotely hosting resources on their servers, can lead to
L5849: profiling users, especially if unique tracking identifiers are added to the URLs.
L5850: When collecting and storing user information within an EPUB publication (e.g., through the use of cookies and web
L5851: storage [html]), EPUB creators need to consider to potential for data theft by other EPUB
L5852: publications on a reading system. Although [epub-rs-33] introduces a unique origin requirement for EPUB publications,
L5853: which limits the potential for attacks, there is still a risk that reading systems will allow EPUB
L5854: publications access to shared persistent storage (e.g., older reading systems that have not been
L5855: updated and non-conforming newer reading systems). Consequently, EPUB creators SHOULD NOT store
L5856: sensitive user data in persistent storage. If EPUB creators must store sensitive data, they SHOULD
L5857: encrypt the data to prevent trivial access to it in the case of an exploit.
L5858: When publishers and vendors must use digital rights management schemes, they should prefer schemes
L5859: that do not utilize or transmit information about the user or their content to external parties to
L5860: perform encryption or decryption.
L5861: To maximally reduce security and privacy risks, EPUB creators SHOULD produce their EPUB publications
L5862: with the goal of long-term preservation. EPUB publications created this way are normally
L5863: self-contained, not dependent on network access, and not encrypted with digital rights management,
L5864: removing many of the possible attack vectors. [iso22424] is an example of such a preservation
L5865: format for EPUB publications. While it is understood that not all EPUB creators can achieve these
L5866: levels of self-containment, following as many of these practices as possible will still benefit
L5867: overall user privacy and security.
L5868: A. Unsupported features
L5869: This specification contains certain features that are not yet fully supported in reading systems,
L5870: that the Working Group no longer recommends for use, or that are only retained for interoperability with
L5871: EPUB 2 reading systems. This section defines the meanings of the designations attached to these features
L5872: and their support expectations.
L5873: A.1 Under-implemented features
L5874: A under-implemented feature is a feature introduced prior to EPUB 3.3 for which the
L5875: Working Group has not been able to establish enough implementation
L5876: experience.
L5877: These features are considered important to retain despite this limitation because they are known to
L5878: be implemented by EPUB creators (i.e., their deprecation would invalidate existing content)
L5879: and/or they are integral to the content model on which EPUB is built.
L5880: If this specification designates a feature as under-implemented, EPUB creators MAY use the features
L5881: as described.
L5882: Note
L5883: EPUB conformance checkers should alert EPUB creators to the presence of under-implemented
L5884: features when encountered in EPUB publications but must not treat their inclusion as a violation
L5885: of the standard (i.e., not emit errors or warnings).
L5886: Caution
L5887: Whether under-implemented labels are removed or replaced by deprecation in a future version of
L5888: the standard cannot be determined at this time. EPUB creators should strongly consider the
L5889: interoperability problems that may arise both now and in the future when using these
L5890: features.
L5891: Note
L5892: The marking of features as under-implemented is a one-time event to account for the different
L5893: process under which EPUB was developed prior to being brought into W3C. This label will not be
L5894: used for new features developed under W3C processes.
L5895: A.2 Deprecated features
L5896: A deprecated feature is one the Working Group no longer recommends for use in this
L5897: version of the specification. Deprecated features typically have limited or no support in reading systems and/or usage in EPUB publications.
L5898: If this specification designates a feature as deprecated, EPUB creators SHOULD NOT use the
L5899: feature in their EPUB publications.
L5900: Note
L5901: EPUB conformance checkers should alert EPUB creators to the presence of deprecated features
L5902: when encountered in EPUB publications.
L5903: B. Allowed external identifiers
L5904: The following table lists the public and
L5905: system identifiers [xml] allowed in document type declarations. [xml]
L5906: EPUB creators MAY use these external identifiers only in publication resources with the listed
L5907: media types [rfc2046] specified in their manifest declarations. (Refer to 3.9 XML conformance for more information.)
L5908: Media Type(s)
L5909: Public Identifier
L5910: System Identifier
L5911: application/mathml+xml
L5912: application/mathml-presentation+xml
L5913: application/mathml-content+xml
L5914: -//W3C//DTD MathML 3.0//EN
L5915: http://www.w3.org/Math/DTD/mathml3/mathml3.dtd
L5916: application/x-dtbncx+xml
L5917: -//NISO//DTD ncx 2005-1//EN
L5918: http://www.daisy.org/z3986/2005/ncx-2005-1.dtd
L5919: image/svg+xml
L5920: -//W3C//DTD SVG 1.1//EN
L5921: http://www.w3.org/Graphics/SVG/1.1/DTD/svg11.dtd
L5922: C. Expressing structural semantics
L5923: C.1 Introduction
L5924: This section is non-normative.
L5925: Structural semantics add additional meaning about the specific structural purpose an element plays.
L5926: The epub:type attribute is used to express domain-specific semantics in EPUB content documents and media overlay documents, with the structural information it carries
L5927: complementing the underlying vocabulary.
L5928: The applied semantics refine the meaning of their containing elements without changing their nature
L5929: for assistive technologies, as happens when using the similar role attribute [html]. The
L5930: attribute does not enhance the accessibility of the content, in other words; it only provides hints
L5931: about the purpose.
L5932: Semantic metadata enriches content for use in publishing workflows and for author-defined purposes.
L5933: It also allows reading systems to learn more about the structure and content of a document
L5934: (e.g., to enable skippability and escapability in media
L5935: overlays).
L5936: This specification defines a method for adding structural semantics using the attribute
L5937: axis: instead of adding new elements, EPUB creators can append the epub:type
L5938: attribute to existing elements to add the desired semantics.
L5939: C.2 The epub:type attribute
L5940: Attribute Name:
L5941: epub:type
L5942: Namespace:
L5943: http://www.idpf.org/2007/ops
L5944: Usage:
L5945: Refer to the requirements for XHTML, SVG, and media
L5946: overlays.
L5947: Value:
L5948: A whitespace-separated list of property values, with
L5949: restrictions as defined in D.1 Vocabulary association mechanisms.
L5950: Whitespace is the set of characters as defined in [xml].
L5951: Caution
L5952: Although the epub:type attribute is similar in nature to the role
L5953: attribute [html], the attributes serve different purposes. The values of the
L5954: epub:type attribute do not enhance access through assistive technologies like
L5955: screen readers as they do not map to the accessibility APIs used by these technologies. This
L5956: means that adding epub:type values to semantically neutral elements like [html]
L5957: div and span does not make them any more accessible to assistive technologies. Only ARIA
L5958: roles influence how assistive technologies understand such elements.
L5959: The epub:type attribute is consequently only intended for publishing semantics and
L5960: reading system enhancements. Reading systems may use epub:type values to
L5961: provide accessibility enhancements like built-in read aloud or media overlays functionality
L5962: where interaction with assistive technologies is not essential.
L5963: Refer to Digital Publishing WAI-ARIA
L5964: Module [dpub-aria] for more information about accessible publishing roles.
L5965: The epub:type attribute inflects semantics on the element on which it appears. Its value
L5966: is one or more whitespace-separated terms stemming from external vocabularies associated with the
L5967: document instance.
L5968: The default vocabulary for the epub:type attribute is
L5969: the EPUB 3 Structural Semantics Vocabulary [epub-ssv-11]. EPUB creators MAY include
L5970: unprefixed terms that are not part of this vocabulary, but the preferred method for adding custom
L5971: semantics is to use prefixes for them. Refer to D.1 Vocabulary association mechanisms for more information.
L5972: Example 76: Identifying a preamble
L5973: <html
L5974: …
L5975: xmlns:epub="http://www.idpf.org/2007/ops">
L5976: …
L5977: <body>
L5978: …
L5979: <section epub:type="preamble">
L5980: …
L5981: </section>
L5982: …
L5983: </body>
L5984: </html>
L5985: Example 77: Identifying a glossary
L5986: <html
L5987: …
L5988: xmlns:epub="http://www.idpf.org/2007/ops">
L5989: …
L5990: <body>
L5991: …
L5992: <dl epub:type="glossary">
L5993: …
L5994: </dl>
L5995: …
L5996: </body>
L5997: </html>
L5998: Example 78: Adding page break semantics
L5999: <html
L6000: …
L6001: xmlns:epub="http://www.idpf.org/2007/ops">
L6002: …
L6003: <body>
L6004: …
L6005: <p>
L6006: …
L6007: <span
L6008: epub:type="pagebreak"
L6009: id="p234"
L6010: role="doc-pagebreak"
L6011: aria-label="234"/>
L6012: …
L6013: </p>
L6014: …
L6015: </body>
L6016: </html>
L6017: D. Vocabularies
L6018: This appendix defines a general set of mechanisms by which attributes in this specification can reference
L6019: terms from vocabularies. It also defines EPUB-specific vocabularies for use with the attributes.
L6020: D.1 Vocabulary association mechanisms
L6021: D.1.1 Introduction
L6022: This section is non-normative.
L6023: EPUB defines a formal method of referencing terms and properties defined in metadata and semantic
L6024: vocabularies using the property data type. The
L6025: epub:type attribute uses this data type in EPUB content documents and
L6026: media overlay documents to add structural semantics,
L6027: for example, while the property and rel attributes use the data type
L6028: to define properties and relationships in the package document.
L6029: A property value is like a CURIE [rdfa-core] — it represents a URL [url] in
L6030: compact form. The expression consists of a prefix and a reference, where the prefix — whether
L6031: literal or implied — is a shorthand mapping of a URL that typically resolves to a term
L6032: vocabulary. When a reading system converts the prefix to its URL representation and combines
L6033: with the reference, the resulting URL normally resolves to a fragment within that vocabulary
L6034: that contains human- and/or machine-readable information about the term.
L6035: To reduce the complexity for authoring, each attribute that takes a property data type
L6036: also defines a default vocabulary. Terms and properties
L6037: referenced from the default vocabularies do not include a prefix as the mapping reading systems
L6038: use to map to a URL is predefined.
L6039: The power of the property data type lies in its easy extensibility. To incorporate new
L6040: terms and properties, EPUB creators only need to declare a prefix. In another authoring convenience, this specification also reserves prefixes for many commonly used
L6041: publishing vocabularies (i.e., their declaration is optional).
L6042: The following sections provide additional details on the property data type and
L6043: vocabulary association mechanism.
L6044: D.1.2 The property data type
L6045: The property data type is a compact means of expressing a URL [url] and consists of
L6046: an OPTIONAL prefix separated from a reference by a colon.
L6047: (EBNF productions ISO/IEC
L6048: 14977)
L6049: All terminal symbols are in the Unicode Block 'Basic Latin' (U+0000 to
L6050: U+007F).
L6051: property
L6052: =
L6053: [ prefix , ":" ] , reference;
L6054: prefix
L6055: =
L6056: ? xsd:NCName ? ;
L6057: reference
L6058: =
L6059: ? path-relative-scheme-less-URL string [url] ? ;
L6060: /* as defined in [url] */
L6061: This specification derives the property data type from the CURIE data type defined in
L6062: [rdfa-core]. A property represents a subset of CURIEs.
L6063: There are two key differences from CURIEs, however:
L6064: an empty reference does not represent a valid property value even
L6065: though it is valid to the definition above (i.e., a property value that only
L6066: consists of a prefix and colon is invalid).
L6067: an empty string does not represent a valid property even though it is valid to
L6068: the definition above.
L6069: Example 79: Expanding a metadata property value
L6070: In this example, the property value is composed of the prefix dcterms
L6071: and the reference modified.
L6072: <meta property="dcterms:modified">2011-01-01T12:00:00Z</meta>
L6073: After processing [epub-rs-33], this
L6074: property would expand to the following URL:
L6075: http://purl.org/dc/terms/modified
L6076: as the dcterms: prefix is a reserved
L6077: prefix that maps to the URL "http://purl.org/dc/terms/".
L6078: When an EPUB creator omits a prefix from a property value, the expressed reference
L6079: represents a term from the default vocabulary for that
L6080: attribute.
L6081: Example 80: Expanding a manifest property value
L6082: In this example, the mathml property is specified on a
L6083: manifest item element:
L6084: <item … properties="mathml"/>
L6085: This property expands to:
L6086: http://idpf.org/epub/vocab/package/item/#mathml
L6087: when the prefix URL for the vocabulary is concatenated with the reference.
L6088: D.1.3 Default vocabularies
L6089: A default vocabulary is one that EPUB creators do not have to declare a prefix for in order to use its terms and properties where a property value is expected. EPUB creators MUST NOT add a prefix to terms and properties from a default vocabulary.
L6090: EPUB creators MUST NOT assign a prefix to the URLs associated with these vocabularies using the
L6091: prefix attribute.
L6092: Note
L6093: Refer to the definition of each attribute that takes a property data type for more information about its default
L6094: vocabulary.
L6095: D.1.4 The prefix attribute
L6096: The prefix attribute defines prefix mappings for use in property values.
L6097: The value of the prefix attribute is a whitespace-separated list of one or more
L6098: prefix-to-URL mappings of the form:
L6099: (EBNF productions ISO/IEC
L6100: 14977)
L6101: All terminal symbols are in the Unicode Block 'Basic Latin' (U+0000 to
L6102: U+007F).
L6103: prefixes
L6104: =
L6105: mapping , { whitespace, { whitespace } , mapping } ;
L6106: mapping
L6107: =
L6108: prefix , ":" , space , { space } , ? xsd:anyURI ? ;
L6109: prefix
L6110: =
L6111: ? xsd:NCName ? ;
L6112: space
L6113: =
L6114: #x20 ;
L6115: whitespace
L6116: =
L6117: (#x20 | #x9 | #xD | #xA) ;
L6118: With the exception of reserved prefixes, EPUB creators
L6119: MUST declare all prefixes used in a document. EPUB creators MUST only specify the
L6120: prefix attribute on the root element [xml] of
L6121: the respective format.
L6122: The attribute is not namespaced when used in the package document.
L6123: Example 81: Declaring prefixes in the package document
L6124: In this example, the EPUB creator declares prefixes for the Friend of a Friend
L6125: (foaf) and DBPedia (dbp) vocabularies.
L6126: <package
L6127: …
L6128: prefix="foaf: http://xmlns.com/foaf/spec/
L6129: dbp: http://dbpedia.org/ontology/">
L6130: …
L6131: </package>
L6132: EPUB creators MUST declare the attribute in the namespace
L6133: http://www.idpf.org/2007/ops in EPUB content documents and media overlay documents.
L6134: Example 82: Declaring prefixes in an XHTML content document
L6135: In this example, the EPUB creator declares a prefix for the Z39.98 Structural Semantics
L6136: Vocabulary.
L6137: <html …
L6138: xmlns:epub="http://www.idpf.org/2007/ops"
L6139: epub:prefix="z3998: https://www.daisy.org/z3998/2012/vocab/structure/">
L6140: …
L6141: </html>
L6142: Note
L6143: Although the prefix attribute is modeled on the identically named
L6144: prefix attribute in [rdfa-core], EPUB creators cannot use the attributes
L6145: interchangeably. The prefix attribute without a namespace in EPUB content
L6146: documents is the RDFa attribute.
L6147: It is common for both attributes to appear in EPUB content documents that also specify RDFa
L6148: expressions.
L6149: <html … prefix="…"
L6150: xmlns:epub="http://www.idpf.org/2007/ops"
L6151: epub:prefix="…"> …
L6152: </html>
L6153: Note that for SVG embedded by inclusion, prefixes MUST be
L6154: declared on the [html] root html element.
L6155: To avoid conflicts, EPUB creators MUST NOT use the prefix attribute to declare a
L6156: prefix that maps to a default vocabulary.
L6157: EPUB creators MUST NOT declare the prefix '_' as this specification reserves this prefix for
L6158: future compatibility with RDFa [rdfa-core] processing.
L6159: For future compatibility with alternative serializations of the package document, EPUB creators
L6160: MUST NOT declare a prefix for the Dublin Core /elements/1.1/ namespace [dcterms].
L6161: EPUB creators MUST use only the [dcterms] elements allowed in
L6162: the package document metadata.
L6163: D.1.5 Reserved prefixes
L6164: Caution
L6165: Although reserved prefixes are an authoring convenience, EPUB creators should avoid
L6166: relying on them as they may cause interoperability issues. EPUB conformance checkers
L6167: will often reject new prefixes until their developers update the tools to the latest version
L6168: of the specification, for example. EPUB creators should declare all prefixes they use to
L6169: avoid such issues.
L6170: EPUB creators MAY use reserved prefixes in attributes that expect a property value without declaring them in a prefix attribute.
L6171: EPUB creators SHOULD NOT override reserved prefixes in the prefix attribute.
L6172: The reserved prefixes an EPUB creators can use depends on the context:
L6173: Package document
L6174: EPUB creators MAY use the following prefixes in package document attributes without
L6175: having to declare them.
L6176: Prefix
L6177: URL
L6178: a11y
L6179: http://www.idpf.org/epub/vocab/package/a11y/#
L6180: dcterms
L6181: http://purl.org/dc/terms/
L6182: marc
L6183: http://id.loc.gov/vocabulary/
L6184: media
L6185: http://www.idpf.org/epub/vocab/overlays/#
L6186: onix
L6187: http://www.editeur.org/ONIX/book/codelists/current.html#
L6188: rendition
L6189: http://www.idpf.org/vocab/rendition/#
L6190: schema
L6191: http://schema.org/
L6192: xsd
L6193: http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#
L6194: Structural Semantics
L6195: EPUB creators MAY use the following reserved prefixes in the epub:type attribute without having
L6196: to declare them.
L6197: Prefix
L6198: URL
L6199: msv
L6200: http://www.idpf.org/epub/vocab/structure/magazine/#
L6201: prism
L6202: http://www.prismstandard.org/specifications/3.0/PRISM_CV_Spec_3.0.htm#
L6203: D.2 Property field definitions
L6204: The fields in the vocabulary definition tables have the following implicit requirements:
L6205: Allowed Values
L6206: Specifies the REQUIRED type of value using [xmlschema-2] datatypes.
L6207: Applies To
L6208: Specifies which publication resource type(s) EPUB creators MAY specify the property
L6209: on.
L6210: This field appears for properties used in the properties attribute.
L6211: Cardinality
L6212: Specifies the number of times EPUB creators MAY specify the property, whether globally or
L6213: attached to another element or property.
L6214: Properties with a minimum cardinality of one MUST be specified.
L6215: Description
L6216: Describes the purpose of the property and specifies any additional usage requirements that
L6217: EPUB creators must follow.
L6218: Example
L6219: Provides non-normative usage examples.
L6220: Extends
L6221: Identifies what EPUB creators MAY associate the property with.
L6222: This field appears for properties that define primary expressions
L6223: and subexpressions and relationships.
L6224: Name
L6225: Specifies the name of the property as it MUST appear in the metadata.
L6226: D.3 Meta properties vocabulary
L6227: The properties in this vocabulary are usable in the meta
L6228: element's property attribute.
L6229: Unless indicated otherwise in its "Extends" field, the properties defined in this section are used to
L6230: define subexpressions: in other words, a meta element
L6231: carrying a property defined in this section MUST have a refines attribute referencing a resource or expression being
L6232: augmented.
L6233: The prefix URL for referencing these properties is
L6234: http://idpf.org/epub/vocab/package/meta/#.
L6235: D.3.1 alternate-script
L6236: Name:
L6237: alternate-script
L6238: Description:
L6239: The alternate-script property provides an alternate expression of the
L6240: associated property value in a different language and/or script.
L6241: The language tags of the alternate-script property and its associated property — as expressed
L6242: by their respective in-scope xml:lang attributes — MUST NOT be the same.
L6243: This property is typically attached to creator and title
L6244: properties for internationalization purposes.
L6245: Allowed value(s):
L6246: xsd:string
L6247: Cardinality:
L6248: zero or more
L6249: Extends:
L6250: All properties.
L6251: Example 83: Author name expressed in English and Japanese
L6252: <metadata …>
L6253: …
L6254: <dc:creator id="creator">
L6255: Haruki Murakami
L6256: </dc:creator>
L6257: <meta
L6258: refines="#creator"
L6259: property="alternate-script"
L6260: xml:lang="ja">
L6261: 村上春樹
L6262: </meta>
L6263: …
L6264: </metadata>
L6265: D.3.2 authority
L6266: Name:
L6267: authority
L6268: Description:
L6269: The authority property identifies the system or scheme the referenced
L6270: element's value is drawn from.
L6271: Allowed value(s):
L6272: xsd:string
L6273: Note
L6274: The former IDPF EPUB
L6275: 3 Working Group maintained a registry of
L6276: subject authorities for use with this property. This Working Group no
L6277: longer maintains the registry.
L6278: Cardinality:
L6279: zero or one
L6280: Extends:
L6281: dc:subject
L6282: Example 84: Expressing A BISAC subject heading
L6283: <metadata …>
L6284: …
L6285: <dc:subject
L6286: id="subject01">
L6287: FICTION / Occult &amp; Supernatural
L6288: </dc:subject>
L6289: <meta
L6290: refines="#subject01"
L6291: property="authority">
L6292: BISAC
L6293: </meta>
L6294: …
L6295: </metadata>
L6296: D.3.3 belongs-to-collection
L6297: Name:
L6298: belongs-to-collection
L6299: Description:
L6300: The belongs-to-collection property identifies the name of a collection
L6301: to which the EPUB publication belongs. An EPUB publication MAY belong to one or more
L6302: collections.
L6303: It is also possible to chain these properties using the refines attribute to indicate that one collection is
L6304: itself a member of another collection.
L6305: To allow reading systems to organize collections and avoid naming collisions (e.g.,
L6306: unrelated collections might share a similar name, or different editions of a
L6307: collection could be released), EPUB creators SHOULD provide an identifier that uniquely
L6308: identifies the instance of the collection. The dcterms:identifier
L6309: property must carry this identifier.
L6310: The collection MAY more precisely define its nature by attaching a collection-type property.
L6311: The position of the EPUB publication within the collection MAY be provided by
L6312: attaching a group-position property.
L6313: Allowed value(s):
L6314: xsd:string
L6315: Cardinality:
L6316: zero or more
L6317: Extends:
L6318: Applies to the EPUB publication and can refine other instances of itself.
L6319: Example 85: Indicating a publication belongs to a set
L6320: In this example, the publication belongs to the Harry Potter set of books.
L6321: <metadata>
L6322: …
L6323: <meta
L6324: property="belongs-to-collection"
L6325: id="c02">
L6326: Harry Potter
L6327: </meta>
L6328: <meta
L6329: refines="#c02"
L6330: property="collection-type">
L6331: set
L6332: </meta>
L6333: …
L6334: </metadata>
L6335: D.3.4 collection-type
L6336: Name:
L6337: collection-type
L6338: Description:
L6339: The collection-type property indicates the form or nature of a
L6340: collection.
L6341: When the collection-type value is drawn from a code list or other formal
L6342: enumeration, EPUB creators SHOULD attach a scheme
L6343: attribute to identify its source.
L6344: This specification also defines the following collection types when no scheme is
L6345: specified:
L6346: series
L6347: A sequence of related works that are formally identified as a group,
L6348: typically open-ended with works issued individually over time.
L6349: set
L6350: A finite collection of works that together constitute a single intellectual
L6351: unit, typically issued together and able to be sold as a unit.
L6352: Note
L6353: Although reading systems are not required to support these values, specifying
L6354: them provides the option to group related EPUB publications in more meaningful
L6355: ways.
L6356: Allowed value(s):
L6357: xsd:string
L6358: Cardinality:
L6359: zero or one
L6360: Extends:
L6361: belongs-to-collection
L6362: Example 86: Identifying a publication belongs to a series
L6363: In this example, the publication belongs to the series The New French Cuisine Masters.
L6364: <metadata …>
L6365: …
L6366: <meta
L6367: property="belongs-to-collection"
L6368: id="c01">
L6369: The New French Cuisine Masters
L6370: </meta>
L6371: <meta
L6372: refines="#c01"
L6373: property="collection-type">
L6374: series
L6375: </meta>
L6376: …
L6377: </metadata>
L6378: D.3.5 display-seq
L6379: Name:
L6380: display-seq
L6381: Description:
L6382: The display-seq property indicates the numeric position in which to
L6383: display the current property relative to identical metadata properties.
L6384: This property only applies where precedence rules have not already been defined
L6385: (e.g., precedence is given to creators based on
L6386: their appearance in document order).
L6387: Allowed value(s):
L6388: xsd:unsignedInt
L6389: Cardinality:
L6390: zero or one
L6391: Extends:
L6392: All properties.
L6393: D.3.6 file-as
L6394: Name:
L6395: file-as
L6396: Description:
L6397: The file-as property provides the normalized form of the associated
L6398: property for sorting.
L6399: Allowed value(s):
L6400: xsd:string
L6401: Cardinality:
L6402: zero or one
L6403: Extends:
L6404: All properties.
L6405: Example 87: Expressing an author name for sorting
L6406: <metadata …>
L6407: …
L6408: <dc:creator
L6409: id="creator01">
L6410: Lewis Carroll
L6411: </dc:creator>
L6412: <meta
L6413: refines="#creator01"
L6414: property="file-as">
L6415: Carroll, Lewis
L6416: </meta>
L6417: …
L6418: </metadata>
L6419: D.3.7 group-position
L6420: Name:
L6421: group-position
L6422: Description:
L6423: The group-position property indicates the numeric position in which the
L6424: EPUB publication is ordered relative to other works belonging to the same group
L6425: (whether all EPUB publications or not).
L6426: EPUB creators can attach the group-position property to any metadata
L6427: property that establishes the group but it is typically associated with the belongs-to-collection
L6428: property.
L6429: An EPUB publication can belong to more than one group.
L6430: Allowed value(s):
L6431: A single xsd:unsignedInt or series of decimal-separated numbers (e.g.,
L6432: 1 or 2.2.1).
L6433: Cardinality:
L6434: zero or one
L6435: Extends:
L6436: All properties.
L6437: Example 88: Identifying a publication's position in a set
L6438: In this example, the publication is the second in the Lord of the Rings set of books.
L6439: <metadata …>
L6440: …
L6441: <meta property="belongs-to-collection" id="c01">
L6442: The Lord of the Rings
L6443: </meta>
L6444: <meta
L6445: refines="#c01"
L6446: property="collection-type">
L6447: set
L6448: </meta>
L6449: <meta
L6450: refines="#c01"
L6451: property="group-position">
L6452: 2
L6453: </meta>
L6454: …
L6455: </metadata>
L6456: Example 89: Expressing a decimal-separated value for position
L6457: In this example, the decimal-separated value in the group-position attribute
L6458: indicates this publication is volume 98, issue 4 of a periodical.
L6459: <metadata …>
L6460: …
L6461: <meta
L6462: property="belongs-to-collection"
L6463: id="cygnus-x-1">
L6464: Physical Review D
L6465: </meta>
L6466: <meta
L6467: refines="#cygnus-x-1"
L6468: property="collection-type">
L6469: series
L6470: </meta>
L6471: <meta
L6472: refines="#cygnus-x-1"
L6473: property="group-position">
L6474: 98.4
L6475: </meta>
L6476: …
L6477: </metadata>
L6478: D.3.8 identifier-type
L6479: Name:
L6480: identifier-type
L6481: Description:
L6482: The identifier-type property indicates the form or nature of an
L6483: identifier.
L6484: When the identifier-type value is drawn from a code list or other formal
L6485: enumeration, EPUB creators SHOULD attach a scheme
L6486: attribute to identify its source.
L6487: Allowed value(s):
L6488: xsd:string
L6489: Cardinality:
L6490: zero or one
L6491: Extends:
L6492: dc:identifier, dc:source
L6493: Example 90: Indicating an identifier is an ISBN
L6494: In this example, the ONIX code list 5 scheme defines the meaning of the numeric value
L6495: 15.
L6496: <metadata …>
L6497: …
L6498: <dc:identifier
L6499: id="isbn-id">
L6500: urn:isbn:9780101010101
L6501: </dc:identifier>
L6502: <meta
L6503: refines="#isbn-id"
L6504: property="identifier-type"
L6505: scheme="onix:codelist5">
L6506: 15
L6507: </meta>
L6508: …
L6509: </metadata>
L6510: D.3.9 meta-auth (deprecated)
L6511: Use of this property is deprecated.
L6512: Refer to the meta-auth property definition in [epubpublications-30] for more
L6513: information.
L6514: D.3.10 role
L6515: Name:
L6516: role
L6517: Description:
L6518: The role property describes the role of a creator,
L6519: contributor or publisher in the creation of an EPUB
L6520: publication.
L6521: When the role value is drawn from a code list or other formal
L6522: enumeration, EPUB creators SHOULD attach a scheme
L6523: attribute to identify its source.
L6524: When attaching multiple roles to an individual or organization, the importance of the
L6525: roles should match the document order of their containing meta elements
L6526: (i.e., the first meta element encountered should contain the most
L6527: important role).
L6528: Allowed value(s):
L6529: xsd:string
L6530: Cardinality:
L6531: zero or more
L6532: Extends:
L6533: dc:contributor, dc:creator, dc:publisher
L6534: Example 91: Differentiating creator roles
L6535: In this example, MARC Relators vocabulary values differentiate the author from the illustrator of
L6536: a work.
L6537: <metadata …>
L6538: …
L6539: <dc:creator
L6540: id="creator01">
L6541: Lewis Carroll
L6542: </dc:creator>
L6543: <meta
L6544: refines="#creator01"
L6545: property="role"
L6546: scheme="marc:relators">
L6547: aut
L6548: </meta>
L6549: <dc:creator
L6550: id="creator02">
L6551: John Tenniel
L6552: </dc:creator>
L6553: <meta
L6554: refines="#creator02"
L6555: property="role"
L6556: scheme="marc:relators">
L6557: ill
L6558: </meta>
L6559: …
L6560: </metadata>
L6561: Example 92: Identifying a creator who has multiple roles
L6562: In this example, the creator is both the author and illustrator of the work.
L6563: <metadata …>
L6564: …
L6565: <dc:creator
L6566: id="creator01">
L6567: Maurice Sendak
L6568: </dc:creator>
L6569: <meta
L6570: refines="#creator01"
L6571: property="role"
L6572: scheme="marc:relators">
L6573: aut
L6574: </meta>
L6575: <meta
L6576: refines="#creator01"
L6577: property="role"
L6578: scheme="marc:relators">
L6579: ill
L6580: </meta>
L6581: …
L6582: </metadata>
L6583: D.3.11 source-of
L6584: Name:
L6585: source-of
L6586: Description:
L6587: The source-of property indicates a unique aspect of an adapted source
L6588: resource that has been retained in the EPUB publication.
L6589: This specification defines the pagination value to indicate that the
L6590: referenced dc:source element is the source of the pagebreak properties defined in the
L6591: content.
L6592: Allowed value(s):
L6593: pagination
L6594: Cardinality:
L6595: zero or one
L6596: Extends:
L6597: dc:source
L6598: Note
L6599: See [epub-a11y-tech-11] for information on how to provide accessible page
L6600: navigation.
L6601: Example 93: Identifying the print pagination source of a publication
L6602: <metadata …>
L6603: …
L6604: <dc:identifier
L6605: id="isbn-id">
L6606: urn:isbn:9780101010101
L6607: </dc:identifier>
L6608: <meta
L6609: refines="#isbn-id"
L6610: property="identifier-type"
L6611: scheme="onix:codelist5">
L6612: 15
L6613: </meta>
L6614: <dc:source
L6615: id="src-id">
L6616: urn:isbn:9780375704024
L6617: </dc:source>
L6618: <meta
L6619: refines="#src-id"
L6620: property="identifier-type"
L6621: scheme="onix:codelist5">
L6622: 15
L6623: </meta>
L6624: <meta
L6625: refines="#src-id"
L6626: property="source-of">
L6627: pagination
L6628: </meta>
L6629: …
L6630: </metadata>

L6662: D.3.13 title-type
L6663: Name:
L6664: title-type
L6665: Description:
L6666: The title-type property indicates the form or nature of a
L6667: title.
L6668: When the title-type value is drawn from a code list or other formal
L6669: enumeration, EPUB creators SHOULD attach a scheme
L6670: attribute to identify its source. When a scheme is not specified, reading systems
L6671: SHOULD recognize the following title type values:
L6672: main, subtitle, short,
L6673: collection, edition and
L6674: expanded.
L6675: Allowed value(s):
L6676: xsd:string
L6677: Cardinality:
L6678: zero or one
L6679: Extends:
L6680: dc:title
L6681: Example 95: Expressing different title types
L6682: <metadata …>
L6683: …
L6684: <dc:title id="t1">
L6685: A Dictionary of Modern English Usage
L6686: </dc:title>

L6688: refines="#t1"
